## Phone: what ships now, what the native port owes (consolidated 2026-10-12)

**Two phone codebases exist.** The Expo / React Native app in `apps/mobile` is the complete phone
client and is the one that installs on a phone today. The SwiftUI app in `apps/ios` is a read-only
foundation (connection form, chat list, transcript snapshots over direct HTTPS) with no pairing,
relay, sending, approvals or on-device models. Decision: **ship the Expo app via EAS now; the native
port continues behind it and replaces it only once the parity gates below pass.** Nothing in
`apps/mobile` is deleted or frozen until then.

### What the Expo app already does on the phone (reference inventory for the port)

- **Remote control of the computer** over the E2E relay (v2 protocol, PBKDF2 → AES-GCM, sealed HELLO
  against the host's device registry): QR / paste / deep-link pairing (`nekko-agent-pair:` scheme),
  one-time pair codes, multi-computer list with live connection state, reconnect, revocation.
- **Chat operations**: chats list with Working / Needs-you badges, new chat on the computer's models and
  folders, streamed replies, Markdown rendering, tool approvals, the agent's questions, queued
  follow-ups, Stop. Terminal / changes / tasks / training events ride the same relay.
- **On-device models** through llama.rn (llama.cpp): memory-fit GGUF catalog, Hugging Face downloads,
  offline local chats, one loaded model.
- **Push** (relay-sent APNs / FCM; content-free body), Keychain / Keystore secret storage, JSON file
  state, haptics, light/dark theme matching the desktop palette.
- **Tests**: unit tests pin the Hermes crypto and relay client to the shared implementation; the
  phone ↔ relay ↔ computer integration test (`npm run test:relay`) passes against current builds
  (re-verified 2026-10-12; it needs fresh `dist/` for shared, core, host, cli, server and relay).

### Connectivity: Tailscale first, hosted relay second

- [x] **Private relay over Tailscale**: `npm run relay:tailscale` (`scripts/private-relay.ps1` /
  `.sh`) runs `apps/relay` bound to the computer's Tailscale address and prints the
  `ws://<magicdns>:4400` URL for Settings → Remote access. Verified reachable by tailnet IP and MagicDNS
  name from this machine; plain `ws://` is acceptable because WireGuard already encrypts the hop
  and the E2E layer sits on top, and Expo's default ATS allows it. Documented in docs/REMOTE.md. ·
  2026-10-12
- [ ] The hosted relay `wss://nekko-agent-relay.fly.dev` **does not resolve** (the Fly app is gone

  Either redeploy it under the new name (`fly deploy -c apps/relay/fly.toml`, one machine, needs a
  Fly login) or change the default to "private relay on this computer" and treat hosting as the

### Discovery and transport rules (added 2026-10-12)

- [ ] **PH1, ws only toward private ranges.** Shared helper `isPrivateRelayUrl(url)` (loopback, RFC 1918, link-local, `100.64.0.0/10`; a `.ts.net` name is not enough on its own, check the literal host or resolve first) used by the desktop (refuse Enable / pairing-link generation with a plaintext URL elsewhere, with a one-line reason and a "use wss" hint), by the phone's pairing parser (reject plaintext public links) and by the headless `NEKKO_RELAY_URL` path. Tests for every range and for IPv6 forms. Docs: REMOTE.md transport section states the rule.
- [ ] **PH2, Bonjour / mDNS advertisement from the desktop.** Service `_nekko-agent._tcp` with TXT `name`, `relay` (URL), `pair=1` while a pairing window is open, `v`. No secrets in TXT. Implementation without native node modules: a small mDNS responder in the host (multicast UDP via `dgram`, as `bonjour-service` does) behind a Remote access toggle "Visible on this network", default on while remote access is enabled. Windows and Linux work through the same responder; macOS can use the system Bonjour via the same code path. Unicast DNS-SD over Tailscale: publish the same record under the tailnet's split DNS if present, otherwise fall back to the MagicDNS name.
- [ ] **PH3, phone-side discovery.** Expo app: browse `_nekko-agent._tcp` (Zeroconf module, iOS needs `NSBonjourServiceTypes` + local-network permission prose, Android NSD) and list found computers in Computers → "On this network" with name, relay host and a Pair button; tapping starts pairing against that relay, the desktop shows the approve prompt and the code exchange runs as today. Also browse over the tailnet when Tailscale is active. Native SwiftUI port gets the same via `NWBrowser` (gate 3).
- [ ] **PH4, "Relay on this computer" in Remote access.** Desktop option that starts `apps/relay` in-process or as a child on the Tailscale (or LAN) address, replaces the script from PR #443, and pre-fills the ws URL; respects PH1. Shows the tailnet name, warns when Tailscale is off.

### Hosted relay: free push, limited free traffic, paid Cloud, analytics (added 2026-10-12)

- [ ] **PH5, redeploy the hosted relay** under the nekkoagent.com name (Fly, one machine; `fly deploy -c apps/relay/fly.toml`), fix the default in `RemoteAccess.tsx`, and point the website at it. Needs a Fly login (user-side).
- [ ] **PH6, free push for everyone.** Relay accepts `register-push` + `notify` on a room without a traffic entitlement; the desktop can be configured "traffic via my relay / Tailscale, push via nekkoagent.com" (two URLs, push one optional). APNs key + FCM service account on the relay (user-side credentials, see provisioning runbook).
- [ ] **PH7, free traffic tier + fair-share limiter.** Per-room token bucket refilled from a shared pool, paid rooms excluded from the pool and given a fixed rate; daily request budget and frame-byte cap per device, one computer + two devices per free room. Limits in env with documented defaults; control frames `limit` (remaining, resets-at) and `throttled` so the phone can show "free relay busy, resumes at …" and offer Tailscale or Cloud. Never drops a frame mid-run without a control frame first. Load test with the existing itest harness: 50 free rooms + 5 paid, paid latency flat.
- [ ] **PH8, Cloud entitlement wiring.** `/api/relay/authorize` returns `{ok, tier, devices, computers, budget}`; relay enforces it; Settings → Remote access shows the tier and the remaining budget as the relay reports it. Self-hosted image: a `NEKKO_RELAY_TIER_DEFAULT=paid` style env so a self-hoster gets everything with no account. Pricing line for the Cloud plan in spec-web-and-hosted.md §6.
- [ ] **PH9, PII-free analytics on the relay.** Counters only: daily active rooms / devices (daily-salted hashes), platform + app version, desktop OS, coarse region resolved at the edge (country, then drop the IP), content-free event names (run finished, approval needed, question, push sent), tier and limit hits, optional session `kind` when the desktop's "Share anonymous usage" switch is on. Raw connection log rotates in 24 h; analytics leave the relay as aggregates (start with a daily JSON snapshot to object storage, a dashboard later). Both apps show the exact field list and the switch; off changes no feature. Privacy review against SPEC's list before the first deploy; the website states the same list.

### Native port (SwiftUI iOS, then Kotlin / Compose Android): ordered gates, unchanged

1. **Foundation (implemented; simulator build, protocol and credential tests verified):** SwiftUI shell, Foundation protocol package and tests, HTTPS-only direct-host chat reader, explicit Connect, device-only Keychain bearer, Disconnect/Forget. XcodeGen and macOS CI build/test workflow. No provider settings requests, insecure transport exceptions, or automatic connection on launch.
2. **iOS validation (partially verified):** Foundation tests, simulator build, two device-Keychain/host-binding tests and a first-run UI test pass in CI. Full-height light/dark simulator captures inspected. Still pending: authenticated live host, iPad/physical device, accessibility/Dynamic Type and signed distribution acceptance.
3. **Encrypted remote access (planned):** port PBKDF2/AES-GCM wire and relay v2 state machine from shared/mobile references; fixture parity tests; QR/paste pairing, one-time code consumption, Keychain identity, reconnect/revocation, multi-computer switching. No insecure direct-host fallback. The Tailscale private relay above is the first transport to support.
4. **Chat operations (planned):** streaming events, transcript reconciliation after sleep, new chat/provider model/project choice, queue/stop, pending approvals/questions and exact risk payloads. Sending stays disabled until approvals and event lifecycle are proven.
5. **On-device models (planned):** audited llama.cpp Swift bridge with Metal, memory-fit catalog, cancel/resume downloads with integrity validation, backup-excluded model storage, offline phone chat persistence, one loaded model and thermal/background handling. No remote fallback.
6. **Distribution (planned):** signing/team and bundle ID confirmation, physical-device/security testing, privacy manifest review, notification permission after pairing, APNs credentials, TestFlight then App Store. Requires owner Apple account and macOS/device access.
7. **Android (started 2026-10-11, in parallel with iOS):** standalone Kotlin/Compose app in `apps/android`; see "Native Android app" below. Preserve Expo Android until verified.

### Native Android app (2026-10-11)

Layout: `apps/android` is a standalone Gradle build (wrapper 9.8.1, AGP 9.4.1 with built-in Kotlin 2.4.21, JDK 17, compileSdk 37.2, targetSdk 36, minSdk 28). `:protocol` is pure Kotlin/JVM (E2E crypto, pairing links, relay v2 client on coroutines + OkHttp, narrow wire decoders, transcript model ported from `apps/mobile/src/lib`), so it is tested on the JVM without Android. `:app` is Compose + Material 3 with manual DI (`NekkoApp` → `ComputersRepository`), `ChatController` (port of `useRemoteChat`, no Android types), Keystore-backed `AndroidSecureStore`, Navigation Compose. CI: `.github/workflows/android-native.yml`.

## Part 1, Plan (how we build it)

> The **technical plan**: how we build what [SPEC.md](SPEC.md) describes. Update this whenever the technical approach changes.

## Stack (decided, do not relitigate)

- **Monorepo**: npm workspaces. **No pnpm** (broken on this machine by a corepack/yarn override up the directory tree).
- **Desktop**: Electron **44.7.0** (exact-pinned under workspaces) + Vite 7 + React 19 + TypeScript 7 + Tailwind CSS v4 + Zustand 5. `electron-vite` v5 drives the build; `electron-builder` v26 creates installers. These versions reflect the dependency upgrade already present on `main`; keep the lockfile and package manifests aligned and verify packaged main/preload bundling when upgrading again.
- **Core engine**: `packages/core`, pure TS, no Electron imports, unit-testable with Vitest.
- **Host services**: `packages/host` (`@nekko-agent/host`), transport-agnostic Node services + a `createHost()` facade (Phase-2 extraction; see Architecture).
- **Shared types/IPC contracts**: `packages/shared` (`@nekko-agent/shared`).
- **Web server**: `apps/server`, Fastify v5 (+ @fastify/static, @fastify/websocket).
- **Cloud**: `apps/cloud`, Fastify, multi-account, file-backed store (Postgres-swappable).
- **CLI**: `apps/cli` (`nekko-agent`), ESM, Node 22 globals (`fetch`/`WebSocket`), no deps.
- **Relay**: `apps/relay`, Fastify WS dumb pipe.
- **Mobile migration**: platform-native SwiftUI in apps/ios; Kotlin/Compose in apps/android (standalone Gradle build, not an npm workspace; AGP 9.4 + built-in Kotlin, JDK 17). Existing `apps/mobile`, Expo (React Native, Expo Router, new architecture) with llama.rn for on-device models; standalone, not a root workspace (own `package-lock.json`). Types come from `@nekko-agent/shared` as type-only imports (tsconfig path, erased by Babel); runtime protocol code (E2E crypto via `@noble`, relay client) is reimplemented for Hermes and pinned to the shared implementation by tests. Native modules are allowed here (the "no native modules" rule is the desktop/engine's). Changed from Capacitor 2026-10-03, see Key Technical Decisions.
- **Website**: `apps/website`, static hand-crafted HTML/CSS/JS (no framework, GitHub Pages), download buttons → GitHub Releases.
- **Storage**: JSON files under the app data dir; usage analytics as JSONL. **No native modules**, spawn `ripgrep`/git via child_process when available, with JS fallbacks.
- **Engine daemon** (revised 2026-09-30 by Philip): **Rust**, a Cargo workspace at the repo root with crates under `crates/`. `nekkod` (binary: tokio + axum, the `/api` wire, supervision, strangler proxy), `nekko-term` (portable-pty sessions), `nekko-infer` (inference router and runtime adapters), `nekko-bench` (benchmark harness). Shipped as a standalone per-platform binary in the app's resources, never loaded into Node. The UI stays Electron + React (web technology, for maintainability); GPUI or another native shell is deferred (see Backlog).

## Architecture Overview

```text
apps/desktop/
  src/main/        Electron main: thin IPC wiring over createHost() (windows, IPC router,
                   workspace add dialog, window-state), service logic now lives in packages/host
  src/preload/     contextBridge API (typed, from packages/shared) → window.nekko
  src/renderer/    React app (shared verbatim across all editions; see Design System & UI/UX)
packages/core/     providers/ (anthropic, openai-compat, ollama-native, lmstudio, vllm,
                   openrouter), agent/ (loop, tools, prompts), guardrails/ (risky-command
                   classifier + policy), memory/ (markdown memory store), indexer/ (file
                   tree + outline + search), context/ (context assembly with provenance -
                   powers the Context panel), connectors/ (linear, slack, discord, gmail, gdrive)
packages/host/     services/ (settings, sessions, chat, tools, workspace, memory, usage,
                   connectors, mcp, spec, relay-agent, terminal, files, changes); host.ts
                   createHost() → NekkoApi surface + EventEmitter; dispatch.ts createDispatcher(host);
                   paths.ts (data dir, ALS)
packages/shared/   types, IPC channel contracts, defaults, e2e.ts (WebCrypto seal/open)
apps/server/       Fastify: POST /api/:channel + /api/events WS over createHost(); serves renderer;
                   relay-agent mode; esbuild bundle → npx nekko-agent
apps/cloud/        multi-account hosted edition (accounts, entitlements, per-account Host)
apps/relay/        dumb E2E pipe + push sender (APNs/FCM)
apps/cli/          nekko-agent CLI + MCP server (local in-process or remote HTTP+WS)
apps/ios/          SwiftUI iOS app (native target, in migration)
apps/android/      Kotlin / Jetpack Compose Android app: :protocol (JVM) + :app (Compose)
apps/mobile/       Expo iOS/Android app: migration reference (relay client + llama.rn on the phone)
apps/website/      marketing site (index.html, styles, mascot sprite, downloads)
```

### One codebase, multiple runtimes (the host extraction)

The keystone Phase-2 decision: service logic was extracted out of `apps/desktop/src/main` into the transport-agnostic **`packages/host`**. `createHost(opts)` returns one object implementing every `NekkoApi` method (minus the `on*` subscriptions, which become an `EventEmitter` emitting `agentEvent` / `indexProgress` / `terminalEvent` / `changesUpdated`). A shared **`createDispatcher(host)`** provides one routing table reused by every transport. Then every edition wraps the same `Host`:

- **Desktop** (`apps/desktop/src/main`): thin IPC wiring, `ipcMain.handle(channel, …) → host[method](...)`; forwards host events to `webContents.send`. Overrides only `workspaceAdd` for the native folder dialog.
- **Web/Docker** (`apps/server`): `POST /api/:channel → host[method]` + a `/api/events` WebSocket, both routed by `createDispatcher`. Serves the same built renderer.
- **Cloud** (`apps/cloud`): per-account `Host` (own data dir + event emitter), every request run inside `withDataDir()`.
- **Renderer transport adapter**: `renderer/web-client.ts` provides `window.nekko` over fetch/WS (auto-installed by `main.tsx` when no Electron preload exists), so the React UI is **shared byte-for-byte**. It can also route over the relay (`?relay=&room=&key=`).

> **The five-touch rule (recurring):** adding any renderer↔host capability means touching, in order: `shared/ipc.ts` (channel + `NekkoApi` type) → host impl + `host.ts` interface → `dispatch.ts` → `preload/index.ts` (Electron) → `web-client.ts` (web). Keep all five in sync. (Extending `setSessionOptions` is the same shape: update the `Pick` in **three** places, `sessions.ts`, the `host.ts` interface, and `shared/ipc.ts`.)

See [spec-web-and-hosted.md](spec-web-and-hosted.md) for the full web/Docker/Cloud design, edition matrix, relay protocol, and ZDR boundary.

### The Rust engine daemon (`nekkod`) and the strangler migration

Today `createHost()` runs inside the Electron main process, so the agent loop, every pty, the llama-server router and synchronous session I/O share a process with window management and IPC. The target is a Rust daemon that owns the engine, with the TS host shrinking channel by channel until it is gone. The migration never has a big-bang cutover:

- **One wire.** `nekkod` speaks exactly the web edition's protocol: `POST /api/:channel` with `{ args }`, and a `/api/events` WebSocket of `{ channel, payload }`. The renderer's `web-client.ts` therefore works against it unchanged. Terminal data gets its own binary WebSocket per terminal (`/api/term/:id`) so bulk output never goes through JSON.
- **Strangler routing.** `nekkod` holds a table of channels it owns. Anything else is forwarded to a **TS host backend** it spawns and supervises (the existing host, run headless on a loopback port with a per-launch token, the same way `apps/server` runs it). Events are merged: `nekkod` subscribes to the backend's `/api/events` and fans them out with its own. Porting a service means moving its channels into the owned table and deleting the TS implementation; nothing else changes.
- **Desktop topology.** Electron main becomes a thin shell: it spawns `nekkod` (from `resources/bin`), passes the data dir, a random port and a per-launch token, and keeps only what needs Electron (native dialogs, `shell`, the updater, window chrome) on `ipcMain`. The TS backend is started by `nekkod` with the Electron binary under `ELECTRON_RUN_AS_NODE=1`, so no separate Node runtime ships. The renderer receives the daemon URL and token from the preload and builds `window.nekko` from the web client plus the Electron-only overrides.
- **Supervision.** `nekkod` restarts a crashed TS backend with backoff; Electron main restarts a crashed `nekkod`. The renderer's web client already reconnects its event socket; in-flight requests fail with a readable error rather than hanging.
- **Security.** Loopback bind only, a bearer token on every HTTP and WebSocket request, and Host/Origin checks, matching `apps/server`'s `createApiSecurityHook`. The token never appears in a URL the renderer logs.
- **Port order.** Terminals first (the hottest data path), then the inference router, then session storage, then providers and the agent loop, then tools/guardrails/changes, MCP, connectors/messaging/workflows, the relay agent and training. The web, npx and Docker editions move behind `nekkod` once the desktop path is proven, with per-platform binaries shipped as npm optional dependencies.

### Workspace windows (chat / terminal / file / files / browser / diff / pr / hypergate)

A workspace is a **split tree**, not a tab stack. `renderer/layout.ts` owns the tree and is pure and unit-tested: `WbPane` leaves and `WbSplit` branches (`dir: 'row' | 'col'`, `children`, parallel `sizes` summing to 1), with `splitPane` / `removePane` / `movePane` / `resizeSplit` / `retargetPane` / `extent` / `canSplit` (the 8x8 ceiling, `MAX_ACROSS`). Splitting beside a sibling on the same axis joins that split rather than nesting inside it, and a split left holding one child collapses into it, so the tree keeps the shape you would draw.

`store.ts` holds `workspaces: Workspace[]` (`{ id, anchor, root, activePaneId }`) plus `activeWorkspaceId`, and renames the old project-folder field to **`activeProjectId`** (`settings.workspaces` is the folder list the UI has always called Projects) so the two concepts never share a name. `WbPane.kind` is `'chat' | 'terminal' | 'file' | 'files' | 'browser' | 'diff' | 'pr' | 'hypergate'`; `WbPane.refId` holds the file path (file/diff), folder path (files), URL (browser), PR URL (pr), or the Hypergate manager's URL (hypergate). `WorkspacesView.tsx` routes each kind to a component. Store openers mirror `openChatPane` (locate-or-create, focus): a chat with no workspace starts one, everything else lands beside the focused window via `openInActive`. `splitPane` / `movePane` / `closePane` / `resizePanes` / `canSplitPane` wrap the tree functions; `newChatInPane` / `newTerminalInPane` create the host object first, then split. Hypergate is the one singleton window: there is a single manager, so an existing one is re-pointed rather than duplicated when the port changes.

## Data Model

- **Storage**: desktop/server/CLI share `~/.nekko-agent` by default (`NEKKO_DATA_DIR` override). Electron profile data lives in `desktop/`, cloud deployment data defaults to `cloud/` with per-account isolation, and managed model downloads use `models/{type}`. Legacy profiles require a confirmed move with a resumable journal, no silent merge, and preserved model IDs/presets. Usage analytics remain JSONL.
- **Settings** (`AppSettings`): providers, defaults (provider/model), guardrails ruleset, prompts (`PromptTemplate[]`), MCP servers (`McpServerConfig[]`), favorites, language, default chat mode, auto-update prefs, `specMethodology`, `orchestration`.
- **Session**: messages, `workspaceId`, `attachedPaths`, `specLinked`, `mode` (ask/guardrails/yolo), `disabledTools`, `offline`, `incognito`, `pinned`, `tags`, `title`, `modelId`, `autoModel`, `specMethodology`, `parentSessionId`, `ContextPrefs` (toggle/pin state). Context-use estimates in the renderer are ephemeral; compaction stages a bounded summary and replaces older transcript messages only after success, preserving recent turns when they fit and leaving the original untouched if cancelled.
- **Memory**: markdown entries, global + per-workspace.
- **Workspace**: folder roots + index (file tree, outline, search).
- **Usage**: JSONL append; `UsageSummary` aggregates byModel / bySession (drives cost estimation).
- **Changes** (diff/approval): keyed by sessionId, `{ path, original }` recorded the first time `write_file`/`edit_file` touches a path in a session; `listChanges`/`revertChange`/`acceptChange` operate over it.
- **Cloud**: file-backed `CloudStore` (`cloud.json` + per-account dirs under `accounts/<id>`) behind an interface so Postgres can drop in; `setPlan` ready for Stripe. Each account gets its own data dir, scoped via `withDataDir` (`AsyncLocalStorage` in `paths.ts`); the settings cache is keyed by data dir (`Map<dataDir, AppSettings>`).

## Integrations & APIs

- **Model providers**: Anthropic native; OpenAI-compatible SSE (OpenAI/OpenRouter/LM Studio/vLLM); Ollama native (list/pull/ps/load/unload). Base-URL `/v1` normalization for bare `host:port`.
- **Desktop browser control**: the optional `browser` tool uses Stagehand v4 with a dedicated visible local Chromium browser or a user-enabled loopback CDP port. The desktop host alone offers it; cloud/web, offline chats and unattended runs do not. Each action gets explicit approval regardless of chat mode, and the attached browser is never closed by the app. Deterministic selectors and page inspection need no additional LLM key or remote browser service.
- **Agent command log**: guarded `bash` tool calls append the command and stream stdout/stderr to a read-only virtual terminal keyed by session. The existing command executor remains authoritative; opening the pane never re-runs a command.
- **File IPC** (powers file panes / explorer / editor): `readFile(path) → {content, truncated, binary}` (1 MB cap, NUL-byte binary detect), `writeFile(path, content)`, `listDir(path) → DirEntry[]` (dirs-first), host `files.ts`, honoring sandbox/jail checks where applicable.
- **Connectors**: Linear/Slack/Discord via real REST (token-based); Gmail/Drive OAuth scaffold (full flow pending Google client creds).
- **MCP**: hand-rolled JSON-RPC-2.0 client, no SDK dep, two transports: **stdio** (`shell:true` on win32 so `npx`/`.cmd` resolve) and **streamable HTTP** (`McpServerConfig.url` + `token` → plain `fetch` POSTs with `Accept: application/json, text/event-stream`, parses JSON or SSE-framed replies, echoes `mcp-session-id` for stateful servers). `initialize` + `notifications/initialized`, `tools/list`, `tools/call`. Host-side `detectHypergate()` probes `localhost:7777` (`NEKKO_MCP_URL` override) for a Hypergate daemon (`hypergated`) (`mcp:hypergate` channel). Nekko Agent is also an MCP *server* via `nekko-agent mcp`.
- **Vaizer skills hub**: host `vaizer.ts` reads the public catalog of [nekko-labs/vaizer](https://github.com/nekko-labs/vaizer) (`vaizer:catalog` / `vaizer:skillMd` channels). Offline-first: bundled snapshot / `vaizer.json` cache by default, live GitHub fetch only on explicit refresh; SKILL.md fetched verbatim at install time. Non-catalog installs ride an `installSkill` payload + record snapshot.
- **Push (relay)**: APNs (HTTP/2 + ES256 JWT) and FCM (HTTP v1, service-account RS256 JWT → OAuth → messages:send), both dependency-free in `apps/relay/src/push.ts`. Tokens registered per deviceId; dropped on device revocation (`push-remove`). Config via `APNS_*` / `FCM_SERVICE_ACCOUNT` env. Credential setup: [provisioning.md](../../obsurdian/projects/nekko-agent/provisioning.md).
- **Relay v2 protocol (phone remote control)**: relay tags each device connection with a `cid` (`{type:'c', cid, data}` toward the agent; agent unicasts `{type:'d', cid, data}` and can `kick`); device auth is END-TO-END, a sealed HELLO (`deviceId` + optional one-time `pair` code) validated against the host's registry (`remote.json` via `packages/host/src/remote.ts`; pure `verifyHello` in `shared/remote.ts`). Requests before a welcome are refused; events fan out only to welcomed connections (terminal/changes/tasks/training events now ride the relay too). Relay hardening: timing-safe key compare, 10 MB frame cap, token-bucket rate limit, client/room caps, optional `NEKKO_RELAY_AUTHZ_URL` agent-enrollment gate (cloud `/api/relay/authorize`). Managed instance: `wss://nekko-agent-relay.fly.dev` (Fly.io, `apps/relay/fly.toml`, ONE machine, rooms are in-memory); self-host image `ghcr.io/nekko-labs/nekko-agent-relay`. Guide: [docs/REMOTE.md](docs/REMOTE.md).
- **Cloud auth**: email+password (scrypt via `node:crypto`, `salt:hash`, `timingSafeEqual`), bearer session tokens.

## Infrastructure & Deployment

- **Desktop**: electron-builder targets win (MSI/NSIS/zip), mac (dmg/zip, arm64 ad-hoc signed), linux (AppImage/deb). Release CI on `v*` tags builds all 3 OSes and publishes a draft. Auto-updates via electron-updater (GitHub feed, NSIS). To cut a release: bump version (root/desktop/server) + `git tag vX.Y.Z && git push` → CI publishes a draft → publish it.
- **Web/npx**: `npm run web` (in-repo) or `npm run bundle:web` → esbuild-bundled self-contained `nekko-agent` package in `apps/server/cli-dist` (server+engine inlined, fastify external) → `npx nekko-agent`. Publish needs the user's npm login.
- **Docker**: multi-stage `Dockerfile` (`ELECTRON_SKIP_BINARY_DOWNLOAD=1`, dev deps pruned, a cargo stage for `nekkod`, non-root node:22-trixie-slim with tini, health check on `/health`) + `docker-compose.yml` (volume workspace + data, `host.docker.internal:host-gateway`, publishes to 127.0.0.1:1440); GHCR publish workflow on `v*` tags.
- **Mobile**: `.github/workflows/mobile.yml` runs on mobile/relay changes (and manually): typecheck, unit tests, and the phone ↔ relay ↔ computer integration test (needs the root shared/core/host/cli/server/relay builds), then `expo prebuild` + an Android debug APK and an unsigned iOS simulator build. Locally on Windows: `subst` a short drive letter over the worktree before `gradlew assembleDebug` (CMake path limits), JDK 21 from Android Studio's `jbr`, `-PreactNativeArchitectures=x86_64` for the emulator. llama.rn's postinstall downloads its prebuilt libraries with `tar`; under Git Bash that is GNU tar, which reads `C:` as a remote host, so run installs from PowerShell or run `npm run llama:native` after.
- **Cloud**: `npm run cloud` (:4318) locally; hosted deploy target TBD (open question).
- **Local test loop**: `npm run local` builds + launches the built desktop app (electron-vite preview, no installer/Defender); `npm run web` for the browser edition.
- **Ports**: web 1440, cloud 4318. Kill a stale dev server by port (`Get-NetTCPConnection -LocalPort … | Stop-Process`), npm-wrapped node survives `pkill`.

## Design System & UI/UX

**UI map (renderer).** Left rail (icon nav, expands over the content on hover to show labels; its right divider is visible only while expanded): **Command Center** (dashboard), **Chat** → **Workspaces**, **Skills**, **Training**, **Goals**, **Design** (the design studio + snapshot board), **Model Providers** (cloud keys, subscriptions, servers elsewhere), **Nekko Server** (independent model/agent servers on the left, model library/downloads/folders on the right, with optional image generation), **Connectors**, **Memory**, **Settings**. (The dedicated **Projects** view was removed 2026-07-18; folders are added from a chat's + menu / Context Inspector, and the index still powers `@` mentions + context.) **Mascot**: Nekko, a right-facing 2D hand-drawn outline secret-agent cat. `AgentHead` is the canonical head geometry: slim ink-filled sunglasses, discreet earpiece/coiled wire, folded collar and small tie. Glasses perch above closed eyes in sleep/stretch; the mini simplifies the details for 16-24px legibility. Theme ink/paper and ginger ears/tail remain, as does the full mascot's line-boil wobble. Website hero/favicon/social artwork reuse the same geometry rather than drifting into another style. The renderer-local state machine and exact timings still map agent work, user activity, active pauses, and AFK/visibility to bug-watching, wake-and-spin, stretching, lying, and sleeping poses (T124/T135 foundations, AN10 visual refinement).

**Workspaces (the Chat surface).** A multi-window shell driven by a Zustand split-tree model (see *Workspace windows* above). The Command Center wall (`components/CommandWall.tsx`, state in the pure `commandWall.ts`) renders the same tree with the same `PaneFrame`, `SplitCompass` and the shared `components/Divider.tsx`, so dividers, the compass and drag-to-a-side behave identically on both surfaces; `PaneFrame` takes `ringColor` / `stripStyle` / `addable` / `closeTitle` for the wall's needs-you treatment and its own kinds. The left sidebar lists workspaces grouped by project (project bucket + a "General" bucket), each a three-line card reporting its state, model, binding limit, folders, and window count; sub-agent sessions (`parentSessionId`) nest under their parent. The centre is the active workspace's split tree, with no tab strip anywhere. Components: `WorkspacesView` (orchestrator + sidebar + canvas), `WorkspaceCard` (the sidebar card), `PaneFrame` (per-window title strip, drag handle, and the drop target that reads the nearest edge via the pure `edgeAt`), `SplitCompass` (the add-a-window compass, portaled out of the window's own clipping), `ExplorerPane` (a file explorer as a window), `ChatPane` (self-contained conversation, per-pane provider/model, streaming, inline Context Inspector; refactored out of the old monolithic `ChatView`), `TerminalPane` (Warp block terminal), `FilePane` (markdown preview / mono editor), `BrowserPane` (`<webview>` + URL bar), `DiffPane` (session change review). Terminals are an in-memory host service (`packages/host/terminal.ts`): one persistent shell process per terminal, per-command marker echo delimits blocks + carries the exit code (`$?`-aware on PowerShell), output streamed over a `terminalEvent` bus channel. Sub-agents: `spawn_agent` builtin tool handled in `host/chat.ts` (creates a child session, runs a nested `sendChat`, returns its last assistant message; depth-capped).

**Chat surface.** Messages render as **speech bubbles** (user right, assistant left with a tail) flowing into the composer. Reasoning streams into a Claude-Code-style collapsible **Thinking** box (dimmed, auto-collapses when the answer starts). A **metrics bar** above the composer shows context used/total (hover → token breakdown by source), tokens/sec, thinking on/off, an **effort** cycle (low/normal/high → `EFFORT_TEMPERATURE` {0.2,0.7,1.0}), and per-chat estimated cost. The composer also hosts the **prompt analyzer** (see below).

**Instrument strip (above the composer).** Two rows, split by the question each answers: row one **how this agent runs** (Mode, Tools, then a hairline divider and the Offline / Incognito privacy switches pushed right), row two **which brain it uses** (model picker, Thinking, Effort, then Automate pushed right). Three control families keep them from reading as one run of pills, and they are the vocabulary for any new strip control: **`.ctl-menu`** for a value picker (transparent bordered field, `.ctl-menu-label` for the muted name, `.ctl-caret` for the ▾), **`.ctl-toggle`** for an on/off switch (no background until hover; the on-state is color plus a lit `.ctl-dot`, never a filled pill; `.ctl-toggle-neutral` for a switch that shouldn't claim the accent, `.ctl-toggle-on` for a read-only indicator that can't carry `aria-pressed`), and a plain icon button for an action. Everything is `shrink-0` except the model chip, which truncates first, so the strip never wraps.

**Markdown (`Markdown.tsx`).** A dependency-free line scanner (not a blank-line block splitter, which mis-parses the dashed lists people actually type) shared by both sides of the transcript and the file viewer: lists nested by indentation, headings, quotes, rules, pipe tables, fenced code, inline bold/italic/strike/code/links + bare-URL autolinking, single newlines kept as `<br>`. Emphasis is stars-only with hugging delimiters so `snake_case` and `2 * 3 * 4` survive. Covered by `Markdown.test.tsx`.

**File / editor / browser surfaces (IDE-like).** `FilePane` reads via the file IPC; `.md` → toggle between rendered (`Markdown.tsx`) and source; other text → editable mono `<textarea>` with Save (Ctrl/Cmd-S) + dirty dot; binary/oversized → notice. `BrowserPane` is an Electron `<webview>` (`webviewTag: true` in the main `webPreferences`) with go/back/forward/reload/open-external. `FileTree.tsx` (`ProjectFiles`) is a collapsible per-project tree with `fileIcons.tsx` color-tinted glyphs (Linguist/Material palette), lazy children via `listDir`. `DiffPane.tsx` computes a client-side LCS line diff and renders added/removed lines with per-line keep/revert checkboxes + per-file and all-files Keep/Revert; refreshes on the `changesUpdated` event. The `SpecPanel` ↗/row click calls `openFilePane(path)` (not `openPath`), keeping an explicit "reveal in OS" affordance as fallback.

**Prompt analyzer.** `promptAnalysis.ts` (pure, renderer-side, no LLM): given the draft, returns `{ parts, findings, score: 'A'..'F', model }`. Part detection (role/task/context/examples/output-format/constraints/reasoning/tone/variables) + lint rules (vague terms, weak/passive verbs, missing role/format, ambiguous pronouns, length, filler, conflicting instructions, secret/PII leak) + a model hint (multi-step + large context → frontier; short single-shot → fast/cheap). `PromptAnalyzer.tsx` in the composer shows the grade + part checklist, expandable to grouped findings, with inline wavy underlines over flagged spans.

**Context Inspector (right panel, toggleable).** Two parts, each section self-explaining on hover (`InfoHint` group-hover popover):

- **Sources**: folders wired into the chat (active highlighted, add/remove, multi-root), attached files (native multi-file picker; `prompt` fallback on web), the chat's `SPEC.md` (highlighted + openable) with Build-from-chat / Live controls, links to detected guideline files (AGENTS.md/CLAUDE.md), and a memory count.
- **Breakdown**: live view of exactly what's entering the prompt this turn (files, guidelines, memory snippets, connector data, index snippets), each item toggleable/pinnable with token counts, plus a context-window headroom bar.

**Design tokens.** Cleaner cooler ink/paper neutrals, one warm accent (salmon ~`#ff7a59`) + a friendly `--accent-2` paired into `--brand-grad`, 8px spacing grid, rounded-xl cards with soft elevation `--shadow-sm/md`, hairline borders, focus `--ring`, Inter/system font, JetBrains Mono for code. `--accent-soft`/`--ring`/gradient all derive from `--accent` (color-mix) so they track the user's chosen accent. Dark and light themes via CSS variables + `data-theme`, with `color-scheme: light/dark` set on `:root`/`[data-theme=dark]` (and styled `select option` bg/color) to avoid white-on-white.

**Panels and the squircle.** Every surface of the workbench is a **panel**: the sidebar, each window, the inspector, the empty state. One `panel` utility carries the shape (`--pane-radius`, `corner-shape: squircle`, `overflow: hidden`, paper background) and `panel-ring` carries the outline. The ring is a **`::after` over the contents, not an `inset` box-shadow under them**, because an inset shadow paints on the element's own background layer: any child with a background of its own (a title strip, a toolbar) covers the stretch of ring running behind it, which is why a window's top corners read as square while its bottom ones were round. Focus is `--panel-ring-color`, set per panel. Panels float on one `--surface-2` field at `--pane-gap`, so the three columns of the workbench are one system rather than two full-bleed columns flanking a tray of rounded windows. Beyond that, a base rule gives `rounded-lg/xl/2xl/3xl` the superellipse everywhere; `rounded-full` is deliberately excluded, since a superellipse at a 50% radius is not a circle and the dots, avatars and pills that use it are meant to be round.

**Responsive / PWA.** ChatView session list + context panel become slide-over drawers on phones; header selects shrink/hide via `md:`. Installable PWA (manifest + network-first `sw.js` that never caches `/api`, registered only on http origins).

### Inline editor comments (planned), design

- **Surface**: in `FilePane`'s mono editor, a gutter **+** appears on the hovered/active line. Clicking opens an inline comment box anchored to that line.
- **Capture**: a comment carries `{ path, line, lineText, comment }`. Comments persist per session (extend the host `changes`/a new `comments.ts` store, keyed by sessionId; surfaced via a new `comments:*` IPC channel set following the five-touch rule). A small marker stays on commented lines until resolved.
- **Actions**: **Add to prompt** appends a formatted block (`> file:line, comment`) into the active pane's composer draft so several annotations batch into one ask; **Run now** dispatches immediately via `sendChat` on the pane's session with the same block as the user turn. Both reuse the existing composer/send path, no new agent plumbing.
- **Constraint**: zero new deps; gutter affordance built with the existing editor textarea + an overlay (same technique as the analyzer underline overlay).

### Design board (planned), design

- **Surface**: a new left-rail **Design** view (and/or a `WbPane.kind = 'design'`). A zoomable/pannable board lays out captured **page snapshots** as cards (Figma-canvas feel) using CSS transform pan/zoom, no new canvas/graph dep for v1.
- **Snapshots**: v1 = read-only images. Capture per page/route. For web/desktop, snapshot the rendered app (reuse the existing screenshot path / `webview` capture for the running preview); store under the app data dir keyed by route. A capture refresh is triggered on demand and when the agent reports a UI change.
- **Notes & comments**: clicking a card opens a side sheet. **Notes** persist with the board (a `design.json` per workspace). **Comments** reuse the inline-comment actions, **Add to prompt** / **Run now**, carrying the page identity as context.
- **Live update + indicator**: when an agent edits files that map to a page, mark that card with an **"updating" badge**; on the agent's `done`/`changesUpdated`, re-capture so the user watches the snapshot change. Clicking the badge calls `openChatPane(sessionId)` to jump to the driving agent.
- **Scope (v1)**: snapshots only (not an editable vector canvas, see Non-goals in spec). Page→file mapping starts heuristic (route table / manual tag) and can tighten later.

## Performance design (the speed contract)

How the rules in [SPEC's speed contract](SPEC.md#speed--responsiveness-the-speed-contract) are met and enforced.

- **Store subscriptions are narrow.** No component subscribes to the whole zustand store. Components select the fields they render with `useShallow` or single-field selectors. Composer drafts live in component state and persist to `composerDrafts.ts` on a trailing debounce, so a keypress re-renders the composer and nothing else.
- **Transcript windowing.** `components/agent-console/VirtualTranscript.tsx` (in-repo, no dependency) renders only the rows intersecting the viewport plus an overscan. Row heights are measured with one shared `ResizeObserver` and kept in a height cache keyed by block id; unmeasured rows use an estimate. The list sticks to the bottom while streaming unless the user has scrolled up, and keeps the anchored row in place when heights above it change.
- **Frozen blocks.** Assistant text is split into top-level markdown blocks. Blocks before the last are memoized by content, so streaming re-parses only the tail block. Transcript rows are `React.memo` with stable props; streaming state reaches only the live row.
- **One streaming path.** Agent events are folded once (`liveRuns.ts`) and delivered to subscribers at most once per animation frame. A chat pane does not also run its own 50 ms flush over the same events.
- **Warm set for switching.** A renderer session cache (LRU, at most 8 chats and about 24 MB of message text) holds transcripts of recently opened chats and serves them instantly, revalidating in the background. The last 3 chat windows stay mounted, hidden with `content-visibility: hidden` so their layout and scroll positions are kept and showing one is a paint (virtualized, so a hidden pane costs its visible rows only). `listSessions` returns summaries without messages; full transcripts are fetched per chat.
- **Terminal path.** The daemon coalesces pty output per terminal into one frame per 8 ms (or 64 KB, whichever first) on a binary WebSocket. The client acknowledges consumed bytes; above 1 MB unacknowledged the daemon stops reading the pty, which back-pressures the program instead of the UI. Keystrokes are fire-and-forget WebSocket frames, not request/response calls. Scrollback is a byte ring buffer, never string concatenation. The renderer is xterm.js with the WebGL addon by default; `ghostty-web` (Ghostty's VT core in WebAssembly) is the experimental opt-in (`settings.terminal.renderer`), and falls back to xterm on its own if its WebAssembly cannot load. ghostty-web acks right after `write()` returns (it parses synchronously and only calls back on the next paint, which a hidden pane never gets); xterm acks from its parse callback. Both are code-split, so neither loads until a terminal opens. The renderer CSP allows `'wasm-unsafe-eval'` and `data:` in `connect-src` for ghostty-web's inlined module.
- **How it is tested.** `scripts/perf/` drives headless Chromium over the DevTools protocol (the `ws` client already in the tree, no Playwright) against the web edition with a scripted OpenAI-compatible mock provider and a seeded 1,000-message chat. Keypresses are real `Input.dispatchKeyEvent` events; latency is measured in the page from the event's timestamp to the first frame after its effect is committed (`requestAnimationFrame` then a message-channel tick), with Event Timing entries recorded alongside. Streaming frame work is the main-thread time in each of the renderer's own animation frames, read from a DevTools trace in a vsync-paced browser (the Long Animation Frames API only reports frames over 50 ms). Each budget runs enough samples for a stable p95. The SPEC targets are for a desktop at 120 Hz and `npm run perf:strict` enforces them there; the `perf` CI job, on a 4-vCPU runner with no GPU and a 60 Hz display, fails when a p95 exceeds two and a half times the target (the regression gate in `scripts/perf/budgets.mjs`; at exactly twice, healthy builds sat at 94-104% of the gate and the job went red at random). The report prints each raw p95 with both numbers and is uploaded as a CI artifact, so a regression shows the number, not only the failure.

## Coding Conventions

Extends `../../knowledgebase/principles/coding.md` (which these override).

- **TypeScript everywhere**; `packages/core` is pure (no Electron) and unit-tested with Vitest.
- **Keep the build green**: `npm run build` at repo root (order: shared → core → host → desktop) before each commit; typecheck across all workspaces; core tests passing.
- **Land code via auto-merged PRs** on `nekko-labs/nekko-agent`: branch → push → `gh pr create` → `gh pr merge --squash --admin --delete-branch`. PR descriptions stay **short and plain**, a few human-readable bullets.
- **Human-readable tasks first**: checklist items read like plain product statements; technical detail goes as indented sub-bullets, never the top line.
- **Cross-platform** code (Windows-first dev). MCP/stdio spawns use `shell:true` on win32.
- **No new deps when avoidable**: diff (LCS), prompt analysis, file icons, MCP, push JWTs are all in-repo.
- **MCP server stdout hygiene**: `runMcpServer` reassigns `console.log = console.error` so stray stdout never corrupts the protocol stream.
- Autonomous build: don't ask the user; use best judgement; record open questions in `memory.md`.

## Constraints

- **No native node modules** (avoids Electron rebuild pain); **no pnpm**; **Windows-first** dev but keep code cross-platform. Rust lives in the separate `nekkod` binary only, never as a Node addon.
- **Rust daemon hygiene**: `cargo fmt --check`, `cargo clippy -- -D warnings` and `cargo test` pass in CI on Linux, macOS and Windows; the daemon never panics on bad input (errors become a `{ error }` reply), and every child process it spawns is killed when it exits.
- **Not a full IDE**: lightweight textarea editor (not Monaco/CodeMirror), no LSP/debugger; `<webview>` browser is a preview, not a hardened browser; Design board shows snapshots, not an editable canvas.
- The web/Docker server grants file + shell access to whoever can reach it → **bind to `127.0.0.1` by default**; exposing beyond localhost requires `--host`/`NEKKO_HOST=0.0.0.0` **and** a token (`NEKKO_TOKEN`), with a prominent banner. Guardrails + sandbox modes live in the host (not the UI), so they apply identically across editions.
- **Local Windows `dist` is blocked** by Defender quarantining `app-builder.exe` (AV false-positive), release CI runners are unaffected; don't change Defender settings unprompted.
- **Privacy invariant**: the relay sees only ciphertext + the fact "a run finished" (for push); never message content. Never read/echo a secret value into the conversation (the rotate script is no-echo by design).
- **GUI can't be exercised headless here**: interactive surfaces (webview, drag, diff line-revert math, analyzer overlay, the planned inline comments + design board) are verified by typecheck + build in this environment and need a hands-on pass on a real desktop/web run.
- **Standing rule**: confirm before first-time public exposure / new permissions/credentials unless explicitly authorized this session ([[confirm-before-public-or-permissions]]). Building tooling is fine; only *executing* the public/permission action needs the prompt.

## Key Technical Decisions

- **Host extraction (`packages/host`)**: one transport-agnostic `createHost()` + `createDispatcher()` so desktop/web/Docker/Cloud/CLI all reuse the same engine and the renderer is shared verbatim. The single biggest architectural move; everything else hangs off it.
- **Per-account isolation via `AsyncLocalStorage`**: `withDataDir(dir, fn)` scopes the data dir per request; the settings cache became `Map<dataDir, AppSettings>` (also a general correctness fix). Editions that never call `withDataDir` are byte-for-byte unaffected.
- **E2E-encrypted, outbound-only relay**: the local agent dials out (no inbound ports); keys derived from the pairing secret (PBKDF2 → AES-GCM); the relay forwards opaque ciphertext. This is what makes relayed local-model use inherently ZDR.
- **Open files in-app, not the OS**: matches the "stay in the app" goal and fixes the dead click (`openPath` silently fails with no registered `.md` handler); OS hand-off kept only as a "reveal" fallback.
- **Editor is a lightweight textarea**, not CodeMirror/Monaco, honors "simple, not a full IDE" + the small-dependency footprint; markdown gets the existing zero-dep `Markdown` renderer.
- **Browser pane uses Electron `<webview>`** for v1 (DOM-flow, simplest inside splittable panes); `WebContentsView` is more robust but needs main-process bounds syncing across split groups, deferred.
- **Diff/approve snapshots originals** on first agent write, then diffs current-vs-original; writes still happen immediately (tool loop never gated), "reject" reverts, full review/revert UX without blocking the agent.
- **Prompt analyzer is fully client-side** (regex/structural heuristics), instant, offline, free; the marketable always-on feel. LLM rewrite is a later opt-in.
- ~~**Capacitor over React Native for mobile**~~ (superseded 2026-10-03): it was chosen to wrap the shared renderer. Once the phone became its own client (PF21) and gained on-device models (PF22), the reuse argument was gone and llama.cpp needed a real native binding, so the app moved to **Expo + llama.rn** (the stack PocketPal ships on both stores). The full app in a phone browser over the relay remains for people who want every surface.
- **Tags over hierarchical folders** for conversation organization (lighter, less sidebar restructuring).
- **Hand-rolled MCP & push** (JSON-RPC over stdio; APNs/FCM JWTs via `node:crypto`), dependency-free, in keeping with "no native modules" and a lean tree.

## Road to v1.0

> **What v1.0 means**: the polished, installable **OSS product** (Desktop + self-hosted web + Docker), every differentiator and IDE surface working and verified, the two new visual/editing features landed, and clean signed installs + npm publish. **Nekko Agent Cloud (paid)** runs as a *parallel track* (T31/T32/T34) and does **not** gate the OSS v1.0 launch.

**Done toward v1.0**: core engine + agent loop, all providers + local auto-discovery, the differentiators (Context Inspector, guardrails, memory), workbench + terminals + sub-agents + Command Center, spec-driven dev + orchestration + optimize + auto-mode + refined design system, the **IDE-surfaces wave** (file viewer/editor, browser pane, file explorer, diff/approval, hoverable inspector, prompt analyzer, T66), **inline editor comments (T72)** and the **Design board (T73)**, and all distribution editions (desktop release through **v0.8.0** (Windows/Linux/macOS, signed and notarized), web/npx, Docker, mobile shells, relay-mediated push).

**Remaining for v1.0:**

- [ ] **GUI verification pass** of the IDE-surfaces wave (T66) with a model connected, webview browsing, the diff line-revert math, the analyzer overlay. (The file editor + tree + the new comment/design surfaces are now hands-on verified over the web edition; the rest is best exercised with a live model.) *2026-09-30 headless pass* (`%TEMP%/nekko-verify/verify-gui-pass.mjs`, web edition + mock OpenAI provider + headless Edge over CDP, 11/11): app shell and navigation (Command Center, Models, Connectors incl. the Messaging channels section, Settings), chat pane mount, a turn streamed through the mock and rendered, suggestion chips + composer ghost text, ArrowRight ghost acceptance, the analyzer bar (grade/parts/findings chips on a weak prompt), and session persistence across reload + reopen. Still unverified headlessly and needing the live-model hands-on pass: the **webview browser pane** (Electron-only surface, no equivalent in the web edition), the **diff line-revert math** (needs a real tool-driven edit), and real-provider behavior around slow/erroring streams.
- [x] **v0.7.1 published for Windows + Linux; the macOS build is pending on Apple notary credentials (user-side), and the CLI publish stays gated.** The original macOS failure was upstream [electron-builder#10066](https://github.com/electron-userland/electron-builder/issues/10066): `importCerts()` passed the `.p12` import password to `set-key-partition-list -k`, which must authenticate against the temporary keychain's own random password; older macOS never verified `-k` on an already-unlocked keychain and the 20260831 `macos-26-arm64` image does. Fixed upstream in `app-builder-lib@26.16.1` (PRs #10067/#10101), so the dependency bump `electron-builder` `^26.15.3` → `^26.16.1` was the whole fix, and on v0.7.1 the mac leg signed `NekkoAgent.app` with the Developer ID correctly. The earlier hypothesis about p12 export encryption was wrong (the `security import` leg already succeeded). The build then failed one step later: **`xcrun notarytool` gets HTTP 401 Unauthenticated from Apple**, deterministic across a rerun. The new `.github/workflows/notary-probe.yml` (dispatch-only) isolated it: the stored `APPLE_API_KEY_P8` parses fine, but Apple rejects the key/id/issuer trio on a read-only `notarytool history` call too, so the credential is dead or mismatched (the same trio notarized v0.6.1 on 2026-08-28 and hypergate v1.25.0 on 2026-09-03). Re-set the three org secrets from a consistent key per `docs/signing.md`, then `gh run rerun 34556109654 --failed` attaches the mac assets to the already-published release. Philip approved publishing Windows/Linux ahead of the fix: the release went public 2026-09-11 with `NekkoAgent-0.7.1-x64.exe`, `-amd64.deb`, `-x86_64.AppImage`, and the updater manifests, and the release notes carry a macOS-pending line. The superseded v0.7.0 draft was deleted. Also removed a stray `.worktrees/plan` gitlink with no `.gitmodules` entry (it made `submodule foreach` fail during CI cleanup) and gitignored `.worktrees/`. · Done: 2026-09-11 (Windows/Linux; macOS when notary creds rotate)
- [ ] Pending user-side credentials/infra (below): remaining Windows signing, CLI npm publishing, full Gmail/Drive OAuth, real APNs/FCM delivery, and wiki push. macOS signing/notarization shipped in v0.6.0 (T125). The older T116 domain/project rename is superseded by the Nekko Agent cutover AN3-AN5 and the AN11 clean cut below. Relay now lives at `wss://nekko-agent-relay.fly.dev`; the `nekko-agent-relay` Fly app is torn down once the replacement is verified (AN11).
- [ ] Cut the **v1.0 release** once the above land (bump versions + tag). *Status 2026-09-30: PR #203 (`chore/release-v0.8.0`) sits open and mergeable as the next version bump, a pure 5-file version change; merging it fires the public release workflow, so it waits on the same credentials below (npm publish cred, remaining Windows signing, macOS notary trio) plus an explicit go. The GUI pass above is the only functional gate left on the list.*

### GPU fallback preview (added 2026-10-08)

- [ ] **T185**, Older discrete GPUs can be found through the installed engine when the vendor tool fails. · [spec](SPEC.md#running-a-model-with-nothing-else-installed) · PR #382, Preview, not shipped.
  - Implemented: Windows/Linux vendor-first `llama-server --list-devices` fallback, 15-second display cache and fresh load measurements, recursion-safe external/managed/PATH binary lookup and `llama-server` source. Integrated-memory readings are excluded, including Vega 8/11 Graphics, without excluding discrete RX Vega 56/64. Registering an engine clears stale no-GPU monitor results. No migration, network probe or runtime installation; existing model presets remain unchanged.
  - Decision: resolve the installed binary without `detect()` because detection reads GPU stats to rank builds and would recurse. Keep CUDA selection tied to vendor evidence rather than assuming a listed GPU implies a CUDA runtime.

  - Release acceptance pending: affected laptop app GPU display and new-download preset; AMD/Linux hardware matrix; integrated-name heuristics and upstream output-format stability. Existing-model first-load presets and GPU-unused/Windows error-state hints are planned, not implemented.

Paired quick profiles miss the same gate on base (40.6 ms) and head (28.4 ms); no causal GPU regression established. The base already contains the original fallback from merged #380, so #382 duplicates landed work until its review fixes. Two instances of one chat coexist; both read the same live run and cache. · Done: 2026-10-05

Stage is a column: composer above or below the wall. · Done: 2026-10-05

### WP: Command Center responsiveness (added 2026-10-04)

Philip: opening a chat from the wall into the Agent tab (and switching to the Agent tab) should load fast, and the wall showed every agent as Idle for a second or two before they turned Working. Measured with a CDP script (headless Edge, no GPU, real mouse clicks, Event Timing click-to-paint) against a sanitized copy of the real sessions (58 chats) and against seeded chats with four mock-model streams running, medians of 8-9 switches. On main: opening a chat painted in 40 ms (56 ms with streams), back to the wall in 88 ms (104 ms with streams); the wall showed every streaming chat as Idle on its first frame. After: the same switches within noise (idle: 40 / 80 ms; streams: 64 / 104 ms), the newest reply in view sooner on the real data (87 → 53 ms), and all four streaming chats read Working on the first frame. The switch itself was never slow in this harness, so the slowness felt in the desktop app likely comes from GPU or IPC pressure there; the forced-layout and repaint fixes below remove real per-frame work in any case. · [spec](SPEC.md#chat--cowork-unified-surface)

- [x] **WP1, Status on the first frame.** `running` on the wall and `statuses` in the Agent tab start from `liveRuns.runningSessionIds()` (the app-wide fold of agent events) and are confirmed by a new `chat:running` query (`runningSessions()`, host `getRunningSessionIds` = the chats with an abort controller), the fifth sibling of `pendingInput`. Before, both sets began empty and filled in only as events arrived. · Done: 2026-10-04
- [x] **WP2, No forced layout per chat window.** `useElementCompact` read `clientWidth`/`clientHeight` on mount, a synchronous layout of the document for every window (nine on a wall): 531 ms of self time across the measured switches. It now reads sizes from the ResizeObserver entries only, and the wall passes each window its expected density through `PaneDensityHint` (from `leafRects` × the stage size) so a compact window renders compact on its first frame. · Done: 2026-10-04
- [x] **WP3, PR scan once per transcript.** `ChatPane` ran `collectSessionPrUrls(messages)` (a regex over every message) on every render for the PR dock; memoized on `messages`. · Done: 2026-10-04
- [x] **WP4, Border beam on the compositor.** The working chat's beam animated a registered `--beam-angle` on a conic gradient, repainting the whole composer every frame in every working window. It is now a masked ring (`.composer-beam-ring`) with a rotating gradient square inside (`.composer-beam-spin`, a transform animation, sized with container units so its corners cover the ring), rendered only while streaming. · Done: 2026-10-04
- [x] **WP5, Not done: keeping both surfaces mounted.** Tried keeping the wall and the Agent tab mounted once visited (display:none, then content-visibility: hidden, with a deferred pause). Back to the wall improved (88 → 48 ms paint) but opening a chat got slower (40 → 48 ms paint, newest reply 87 → 148 ms): the hidden nine-window wall kept re-rendering on store updates during the switch. Reverted; the measurements and the script live in the project memory. · Done: 2026-10-04


### CP: Competitor parity, in sequential PRs (added 2026-10-04)

Philip asked (2026-10-04) for a short list of what Claude Code, Codex, OpenClaw, pi and Cursor have that Nekko Agent lacks, then PRs for them one after another. The list, after reading the spec against each: **steering a running turn** (pi, Claude Code, Codex: type while it works; here Enter queued for later or Stop restarted), **unattended operation** (OpenClaw heartbeats and Claude Code autonomous runs carry on; here `ask_user` waited for ever), **background processes** (Claude Code `run_in_background`, Codex exec sessions; here bash dies at 120 s and agents hack around it with `start /b`), **lifecycle hooks** (Claude Code PreToolUse/PostToolUse/Stop, Codex `notify`; none here), **model failover on an exhausted subscription window** (OpenClaw auth rotation then fallback; here Auto only picks at the start of a turn), **web fetch** as a builtin (every competitor; here only the browser tool), and **OS notifications when a hidden chat finishes or needs you** (phone push exists, desktop toast does not). Provider resilience itself landed in UR. · [spec](SPEC.md#chat--cowork-unified-surface)

- [x] **CP1, Steer a running reply + unattended questions.** `chat:steer` IPC (`steerChat` in chat.ts): a running TS-loop turn gets a per-session inbox the loop drains at each tool boundary (`pullSteering`, `steered` event, host persists + `session_meta`); a daemon run gets `loop:steer` (`Loops::steer` into the run's `Steering` inbox, drained in `run.rs` before each call after the first); leftovers at turn end become queued prompts; a chat that is not running queues. Both Anthropic converters fold consecutive user turns into one (tool results, then steering). Composer: Ctrl/⌘+Enter steers while streaming, queued rows gain **Steer** beside **Send now**, the placeholder says so. `unattendedQuestionMinutes` (settings + Agent loop section): `askUser` resolves with `ASK_UNATTENDED` past the limit (approvals untouched). Tests: `loop-steering.test.ts`, `crates/nekko-loop/tests/steer.rs`, provider request golden regenerated for the merged user turn. · Done: 2026-10-04
- [x] **CP2, Background processes.** `packages/host/src/processes.ts` (registry per chat, `PROCESS_LIMIT` 8, `PROCESS_BUFFER_MAX` 1 MB tail-kept, `READ_WAIT_MAX_MS` 120 s, output mirrored to the agent terminal, exited processes forgotten once drained); `start_process` / `read_process` / `kill_process` in `BUILTIN_TOOLS` and `executeTool` (same shell, guardrails and approvals as `bash`, which now says it is killed at 120 s); `abortChat` and host exit kill a chat's processes. Host-run (not in `DAEMON_TOOLS`). Tests: `processes.test.ts`. · Done: 2026-10-04
- [x] **CP3, Desktop notifications.** **Historical 2026-10-04 implementation; Electron ownership and settings placement superseded by BN1 below.** `apps/desktop/src/renderer/notifications.ts`: `decideNotification` (pure: done / non-Stop error / question / approval, skipped for the chat on screen in a focused window, for `relayOnly` echoes, and when `desktopNotifications` is `false`) + `startDesktopNotifications` (Notification API, 5 s dedupe per chat and kind, click focuses the window and opens the chat), started from `App.tsx`; the toggle sits in Settings → Agent loop. Tests: `notifications.test.ts`. · Done: 2026-10-04
- [x] **CP4, `fetch_url` builtin.** `packages/host/src/web-fetch.ts` (`fetchUrl`: http(s) only, `FETCH_TIMEOUT_MS` 30 s, `FETCH_MAX_BYTES` 2 MB read cap, charset from the header, binaries refused; `htmlToText`: scripts/styles/comments dropped, title and headings as `#`, lists bulleted, code backticked, links as `label (href)` resolved against the page, entities decoded; `describeFetched` header line) + the `fetch_url` tool in `BUILTIN_TOOLS` and `executeTool` (approval only in ask-everything). Host-run. Tests: `web-fetch.test.ts`. Web search deferred until a provider is chosen. · Done: 2026-10-04
- [x] **CP5, Lifecycle hooks.** `HookRule` / `HookEvent` in shared settings; `packages/host/src/hooks.ts` (`matchingHooks`, `runHook` with stdin JSON + `NEKKO_HOOK_EVENT`, `decisionOf` from exit 2 or a JSON verdict, `preToolHooks` first block wins and a hung or missing hook never blocks, `postToolHooks` appends clipped stdout, `turnEndHooks` fire-and-forget); `chat.ts` wraps `executeTool`, re-reads settings per call, sends `native: []` to the daemon while a tool hook is on, and fires TurnEnd after the reply with its stop reason; `HooksSection` in Settings (event, name, matcher, command, on/off, remove, add). Tests: `hooks.test.ts`. · Done: 2026-10-04
- [ ] **CP6, Model failover mid-run.** Tested foundation: optional TS-loop replacement at a failed response boundary, Auto-pool opt-in selector, local/offline and delegation restrictions. Not connected to sendChat; verified pool acquisition, route-aware accounting, subscription trigger and daemon support remain unfinished.  When a subscription window is exhausted (`limits.ts` knows `resetAt`) or a provider keeps failing after the UR retries, continue the turn on a configured fallback model with a note in the transcript.

### UR: Runs never left hanging (added 2026-10-04)

Philip ran four agents overnight on 2026-10-03/04 and two never finished. Both were Claude-subscription turns (which run in the TS loop, since only that side records the rate-limit headers); both went quiet right after a tool call whose assistant message was never checkpointed, so the record showed a chat cut off with no error and no way to continue. Investigation found no idle timeout on any model stream (TS readers and the Rust reqwest client alike), no retry on transient provider failures, no checkpoint between "the model asked for tools" and "the tool answered", no record that a turn was in flight when the host died, and no way for the host to notice a daemon that lost a run. The fix follows what Codex (`stream_idle_timeout_ms` 5 min, `stream_max_retries` 5), Claude Code (mid-stream retries with backoff on 529/overloaded), pi (retry on overloaded/5xx, abort cancels the delay) and OpenClaw (auth-profile rotation then model failover) do. · [spec](SPEC.md#chat--cowork-unified-surface)

- [x] **UR1, Stream idle and connect limits.** `readWithIdle` in `packages/core/src/providers/sse.ts` (SSE and Ollama's NDJSON reader) cancels a body that sends nothing for `streamIdleMs()` (5 min, `NEKKO_STREAM_IDLE_MS`) with a `StreamStalledError`; the daemon's shared reqwest client gets `connect_timeout(30 s)` and `read_timeout(stream_idle())` (`crates/nekko-agent/src/http.rs`). · Done: 2026-10-04
- [x] **UR2, Transient failures retried.** `ProviderHttpError` (status + `retry-after`) thrown by every TS provider; `isTransientProviderError` / `is_transient_error` (408/409/425/429/5xx/529, dropped connections, stalled streams, "overloaded"); `streamWithRetry` (loop.ts) / `stream_with_retry` (run.rs) give a model call `MAX_STREAM_ATTEMPTS` = 5 with `retryDelayMs` backoff, restore the turn between attempts and emit a `retry` event; the live run rolls back to its step mark and the rail shows "Retrying". Goldens regenerated (the TS golden runs with `retryBaseDelayMs: 0`, the Rust parity test on a paused clock and normalizes `delayMs`). · Done: 2026-10-04
- [x] **UR3, Checkpoint before tools run.** A `step` event after the assistant message with tool calls is appended; the host persists on it (`chat.ts`), the daemon snapshots the transcript on it (`loops.rs`). · Done: 2026-10-04
- [x] **UR4, Interrupted chats recover after a restart.** `Session.activeRun` is written while a turn runs and cleared in its `finally`; `reconcileInterruptedChats()` (chat.ts, called from `createHost`) marks leftovers with an `interrupted` reply; `lastReplyInterrupted` (shared) lets the chat pane show **Reply interrupted** with Continue from the transcript alone (`PERSISTED_INTERRUPTION`, dismiss remembered per reply). · Done: 2026-10-04
- [x] **UR5, Daemon runs that vanish.** `loop:alive` route + `Loops::is_running`; `runAgentViaDaemon` probes a silent run every 30 s (`LIVENESS_INTERVAL_MS`, two failed probes = gone) and ends it as interrupted, ends it locally when `loop:abort` cannot reach the daemon, and adopts orphaned checkpoints (`loop:end` now carries `sessionId`) for runs started by a host that has since restarted. · Done: 2026-10-04
- [x] **UR6, Smaller hangs.** Stop on a parent aborts its `spawn_agent` children (`runSubAgent` takes the turn's signal); streamable-HTTP MCP calls get a 10-minute `AbortSignal.timeout`. · Done: 2026-10-04
- [ ] **UR7, Follow-ups.** Elapsed no-reported-progress chip implemented for tracked running tools and collapsed activity groups, minute-boundary updates and conservative reset on observed run updates; desktop visuals unverified.  A visible "no progress for N min" chip on a tool that is still running (bash already dies at 120 s; MCP, browser and sub-agents can run long legitimately); an unattended-mode auto-answer for `ask_user` after a timeout (never for approvals); model failover when a subscription window is exhausted (`limits.ts` already knows `resetAt`), tracked under the competitor-parity work; the two tests `user-data.test.ts` "moves the desktop browser profile" (needs symlink privilege on Windows, fails on main too) and the perf gate remain environment-dependent.

### CW: Command Center as a split-tree wall, and the cat mark (added 2026-10-04)

Philip compared three interactive prototypes on 2026-10-04 (stage + dock with a Focus layout, the Agent tab's split tree on the wall, the grid kept with density fixes) and picked the **split tree**, keeping the prototype's density, colors, controls and divider feel; the prototype's outline-cat mark becomes the brand icon everywhere. The left rail and the other pages stay as they were apart from the mark. · [spec](SPEC.md#chat--cowork-unified-surface)

- [x] **CW1, The wall is a split tree.** `commandWall.ts` (pure, tested: `autoShape`/`tileTree` for a fresh tiling and Auto-arrange, `addPane` beside the biggest window along its longer side within the 8×8 ceiling, `filterTree`, seed/reconcile with the watermark, `loadWallState` with field-by-field sanitizing and id reservation, and `migrateGridState` carrying `nekko.commandGrid` cells over as a tiled wall) + `CommandWall.tsx` (renders the tree with `PaneFrame` + the shared `Divider`, compass splits create the chat/shell first and insert it at the pointed side, drag-to-side / swap through `layout.ts`, stacked windows under 720 px). `layout.ts` gains the `automations` / `insights` kinds; `SplitCompass` takes `addable`. `commandGrid.ts` and `CommandGrid.tsx` removed. State in `localStorage` under `nekko.commandWall`. · Done: 2026-10-04
- [x] **CW2, Density follows the window.** `useElementCompact` (width < 470 or height < 440) folds `ChatPane`'s two instrument rows into one `composer-summary` chip (model · mode · thinking · offline/incognito, Automate kept at the right) that opens the rows on click; the reply box drops to one line. · Done: 2026-10-04
- [x] **CW3, Needs you stands out.** A window with a pending approval or question wears a warning ring and a tinted strip (`PaneFrame.ringColor` / `stripStyle`); `CommandCenterView` draws a `wall-ribbon` listing each one (the approval's command when it has one) with **Go**, which scrolls to the window, rings it once (`pane-flash`) and focuses its composer. · Done: 2026-10-04
- [x] **CW4, Panels are windows.** `AutomationsPane.tsx` (the former board, count in the strip's metadata slot) and `InsightsBox` (gear portalled into the strip via `PaneActions`; the top/bottom placement and the hide button are gone, the window's × does that) render as wall windows; the toolbar's **Add window** menu brings a missing one back and offers new agent / new terminal / already-running chats and shells; **Auto-arrange** re-tiles. · Done: 2026-10-04

All raster assets regenerated. · Done: 2026-10-04. Same day, after a proof sheet of variations: the face (eyes, smile) is gone, the forehead dips in a soft curve between the ears, the jaw is a 7.4-radius arc, the line is thin (1.2 at icon size, 1.5 under 64 px, 2.0 under 32 px) with miter joins and flat caps for sharp tips; `icon-art.cjs` no longer exports `FACE`.

The perf harness seeds `nekko.commandWall` as a tiled tree. Not done, on purpose: dragging a window out of the wall into the Agent tab, since the strip's **Open** button does that in one click. · Done: 2026-10-04


### CC: Command Center as a live grid, and six fixes (added 2026-10-03)

Philip's 2026-10-03 batch: a Command Center that is a wall of live windows instead of a card board, plus the cloud output cap, worktree names, first-launch size, rail order, composer Tab and the workspaces → agents rename. · [spec](SPEC.md#chat--cowork-unified-surface)

- [x] **CC1, Cloud providers are not capped.** The Settings output cap (default 8,192) was sent to every provider as `max_tokens`, so long cloud replies stopped mid-sentence. The host now passes it only for local kinds (`isLocalProvider`); OpenAI-compatible and Codex requests omit the field, and Anthropic (where it is required) sends the model's own ceiling from `claudeMaxOutputTokens` (64k for Opus 4.5+, Sonnet 3.7+, Haiku 4.5+, Fable/Mythos; 32k for Opus 4/4.1 and unknown ids) and learns a lower one from a `max_tokens: X > Y` 400, retrying once. TS and Rust providers both; goldens re-recorded. Settings label says "local models". · Done: 2026-10-03
- [x] **CC2, Worktrees named in words.** `chat-worktrees.ts` names a checkout `adjective-colour-animal` (or a slug of a chosen title), unique per repository; the Git management list maps a folder to its chat through `session.gitWorktrees` instead of the folder name (old id-named folders still resolve). · Done: 2026-10-03
- [x] **CC3, First launch at 70% × 80%.** `initialWindowBounds(workArea)` sizes and centres the window before `BrowserWindow` is created (`show: false` until ready-to-show), so there is no visible resize; saved bounds still win on later launches. · Done: 2026-10-03
- [x] **CC4, Agent above Command Center** in the rail and the phone tab bar; a saved `navOrder` still wins. · Done: 2026-10-03
- [x] **CC5, Tab indents list lines in the composer**, Shift+Tab outdents (`composerLists.ts`, two spaces, any `-`/`*`/`+`/`1.`/`1)`/task line; off a list line Tab moves focus as before). · Done: 2026-10-03
- [x] **CC6, Workspaces are called agents** in the Agent tab's UI strings (sidebar header, create menu, empty state, card close, split compass). Code identifiers unchanged. · Done: 2026-10-03
- [x] **CC7, The grid.** `commandGrid.ts` (pure, tested: auto shape, tracks, rects, seeding, reconcile, persistence) + `CommandGrid.tsx` (absolute-positioned cells from computed rects, draggable column/row dividers with pointer capture, drag-to-swap, plus cell with new agent / new terminal / already-running lists, `PaneSlots` so the chat's header actions land in the cell strip) + `InsightsBox.tsx` (gear menu, top/bottom, auto-fit panel columns, vitals strip) + `EmptyIllustrations.tsx` (agents / automations / insights line art). `SessionBoard.tsx` removed. State in `localStorage` under `nekko.commandGrid`. · Done: 2026-10-03

Make the grid state a setting (so phone and web share it); a "fit to window" option for the fixed picker that shrinks cells instead of scrolling; drag a cell out of the grid into the Agent tab; keyboard resizing of dividers.

The job still fails on the shared runner, as it did on main before this change: once on the cold-switch budget by 0.1 ms (20.9 vs 20.8 ms) and once on "could not find the sidebar card for Perf chat 11" (the Agent-tab sidebar lookup, 10 s). The full harness passes locally apart from one flaky frames start in a long combined run. Worth a look at the sidebar lookup's wait and the runner budgets.
