### MA: Native phone app, remote client + on-device models (added 2026-10-03)

Philip asked (2026-10-03) to continue the phone app as a native iOS/Android client that runs Nekko Agent from remote and also runs local models. That merges PF21 and PF22 into one app (PF22 had planned on-device models as a separate product; the user's call overrides that). · [spec](SPEC.md#your-agent-in-your-pocket-the-ios-and-android-app)

- [x] **MA1, Replace the Capacitor shell with an Expo app.** `apps/mobile` is now Expo SDK 57 (RN 0.86, new architecture, Expo Router, React Compiler) with three native tabs (Chats / On this phone / Computers), Nekko Agent's palette and the outline Nekko head drawn with react-native-svg. `appId` stays `dev.nekkolabs.nekkoagent`; schemes `nekko-agent` and `nekko-agent-pair` (a `+native-intent` rewrite turns a scanned `nekko-agent-pair:?…` link into the pairing screen). Android ships `arm64-v8a` + `x86_64` only, the ABIs llama.rn prebuilds. · Done: 2026-10-03
- [x] **MA2, Relay v2 client for Hermes.** `src/lib/e2e.ts` is the shared PBKDF2 → AES-256-GCM construction in pure JS (`@noble/hashes`, `@noble/ciphers`; Hermes has no `crypto.subtle`), with a UTF-8 decoder for runtimes without `TextDecoder` and an `expo-crypto` RNG polyfill. `src/lib/relayClient.ts` does the sealed HELLO, request/response with per-call timeouts (`chat:send` gets 12 h, since it replies only when the turn ends), event fan-in, backoff reconnect, offline (`room not paired`) polling, denial/kick handling, and push-token registration. The derived key is cached in the Keychain/Keystore so reconnects skip the second of PBKDF2. Tests pin both directions of the crypto against `shared/e2e.ts`, and `relay.itest.ts` runs the client against a real local relay, a headless agent (`apps/server` relay-agent mode) and a fake streaming model: enrollment, provider listing with keys stripped, session create, a streamed turn, and a refused replay of the spent code. · Done: 2026-10-03
- [x] **MA3, Computer chats.** Chat list from `sessions:summaries` (top-level, unarchived), Working / Needs you badges from the event stream plus `chat:pending` on connect, connection banner with deny-reason copy, pull to refresh. New-chat sheet: provider/model picker seeded from the computer's defaults, folder chips, `session:create` + `session:setOptions`. Chat screen: saved transcript (`session:get`) plus a live overlay folded from `agent:event` (pure `applyEvent`), tool rows with expandable output, reasoning fold, Markdown (own small parser, tested), Allow this? card (`tool:approve`), question card (`chat:answer`, Skip sends none), queue-while-running (`chat:queue`), Stop (`chat:abort`), re-read on reconnect. Verified in the web preview against the harness: pair via link → online banner → new chat → streamed reply → approval card → approve → command ran on the computer → reply with its output. · Done: 2026-10-03
- [x] **MA4, On-device models.** Curated catalog (`src/lib/catalog.ts`) with exact byte sizes read from the Hugging Face API, RAM fit (`fitFor`: file + ~0.6 GB runtime against 35/50/65% of RAM) and a recommendation (Qwen3.5 2B when it fits comfortably, else 0.8B). Downloads with `File.createDownloadTask` into `<documents>/models` via a `.part` file, size-checked before install, cancellable, keep-awake while running. One llama.rn context at a time (`n_gpu_layers: 99`, mmap, `ctx_shift`), reasoning parsed by llama.cpp (`reasoning_format: auto`), a Think switch for reasoning models, tokens/s from the timings. Local chats persist as JSON with write-then-rename. Web preview reports the engine unavailable (`llama.web.ts`). · Done: 2026-10-03
- [x] **MA5, Push and relay fixes.** Notification permission asked after pairing only; the native APNs/FCM token is registered with the relay on every connect. The relay rejected every FCM token (they contain `:`), so Android push could never have worked; the token pattern now allows it (new relay test). `connectRelayAgent().stop()` also never removed its `limitsUpdated` listener. · Done: 2026-10-03
- [x] **MA6, CI.** `mobile.yml` runs typecheck, unit tests and the relay integration test, then builds the Android APK and an unsigned iOS simulator build, on any mobile/relay change. · Done: 2026-10-03
- [ ] **MA7, Verify on real phones.** Android emulator run of the APK covers the app shell and llama.rn on x86_64; still needed: an iPhone (Metal) and a recent Android phone (OpenCL/Hexagon) for load times, tokens/s and memory pressure with each catalog model, and the camera QR scan.
- [ ] **MA8, Store release.** EAS project + signing, App Store / Play listings and screenshots, APNs key and FCM service account on the managed relay (see provisioning in the workspace), privacy labels (no data collected; models download from Hugging Face).
- [ ] **MA9, Follow-ups.** Exclude downloaded models from iOS backup; resume interrupted model downloads (`DownloadTask.savable()`); images in phone chats for vision models; switch a chat's model mid-chat; delete/rename/archive chats from the phone; a "send to my computer" hand-off from a phone chat; remove the Capacitor-only `RelayPairing` and the `window.Capacitor` branches from the desktop renderer now that nothing wraps it.

### PRL: Merged-PR banner unreadable in light mode (added 2026-10-05)

Reported 2026-10-05 with a screenshot: in light mode the merged-PR banner's left side (`owner/repo#N` and the start of the title) is almost invisible; dark mode is fine. The installed build renders the pre-#283 merged branch of `PrCard`, which hard-codes dark-only text (`text-violet-100` label and status, `text-violet-50/90` title, `text-violet-200` stars) over a gradient that is `transparent` on the left. In light mode that puts near-white text on the near-white paper. Measured WCAG contrast at the left edge (`#ede9fe` on `#fbfbfd`): **1.15:1** for the label, 1.19:1 for the title, 1.57:1 for the status on the purple end, against 16:1 / 13.5:1 / 11:1 in dark. #283 (2026-10-03, after v0.8.0) rewrote the card to theme ink (`--ink` / `ink-soft` / `--accent`), which is readable at the left (17:1) but still fails on the right: `--accent` on the light purple end is **2.41:1**, and the stars stay `violet-200`. The same light-only failure exists in the merged chip in `PrBadge` (`#c084fc` on an 18% purple tint, 1.95:1) and the merged link colour in `SessionPrLinks.tsx` (`#c084fc`).

- [x] **PRL1, Theme-aware merged palette.** `styles.css` gains `--merged-ink`, `--merged-star`, `--merged-line`, `--merged-wash`, `--merged-wash-soft` and `--merged-chip`, kept apart from the user-recolourable `--accent`. Light: violet-800 `#5b21b6` text on a 22% wash (6.2:1 on the purple end, 8.6:1 on bare paper); `solar` lowers the wash to 16% so its browner `ink-soft` title stays at 4.6:1. Dark keeps the old wash and border, with `#c4b5fd` text (7.3:1; the old `--accent` status there was 3.0:1). · Done: 2026-10-05
- [x] **PRL2, Merged `PrCard` uses the tokens.** `MERGED_CARD_STYLE` (exported) builds the gradient and border from the tokens; the status reads `--merged-ink`; label and title stay on `--ink` / `ink-soft`; stars take `--merged-star` and stop pulsing under reduced motion. · Done: 2026-10-05
- [x] **PRL3, Badges and links.** `PrBadge`'s merged chip (`--merged-chip` / `--merged-ink`, 1.95:1 → 7.3:1 in light) and `SessionPrLinks` `prTone` (`var(--merged-ink)`). · Done: 2026-10-05
- [x] **PRL4, Regression test.** `renderer/contrast.ts` (WCAG luminance, compositing, ratio) and `contrast.test.ts`, which reads the tokens out of `styles.css` and holds `--ink`, `--ink-soft` and `--merged-ink` to 4.5:1 at bare paper, soft wash and full wash, plus the chip and link on paper and surface, for light, solar, dark, nebula, terminal and nord (26 checks). Swapping the old `#ede9fe` back in fails at 1.15:1. `PrCards.test.tsx` asserts the merged card and badge read the tokens and carry no fixed light violets. Desktop typecheck and 439 tests green. · Done: 2026-10-05
- [x] **PRL5, Visual evidence.** Before/after of the merged card and badge, light and dark, same seeded chat (live PR #315 via `gh`), 1280px at 2x with reduced motion, in the PR description via `scripts/pr-media.mjs`. · PR #325 · Done: 2026-10-05

  - Reuse the RuntimeCard power glyph; preserve independent controls and responsive stacking.
- [ ] **UX41**, One model row collects variants and opens details alongside the list. [Spec](SPEC.md#server-model-browser-and-desktop-tray)
  - Normalize explicit base-model/repository names conservatively, keep model size/training distinctions, combine catalog and local entries, mark downloads, and retain existing load/download/setup controls in the detail pane. Test grouping and GPU guidance.
- [ ] **UX42**, Server settings save after two seconds or field blur. [Spec](SPEC.md#server-model-browser-and-desktop-tray)
  - Save patches only, serialize writes, preserve dirty fields across status polls, validate numeric input and show saving/error states. Test debounce, blur and concurrent edits.
- [ ] **UX43**, Plus menus stay open when clicked and new workspaces start with a terminal. [Spec](SPEC.md#server-model-browser-and-desktop-tray)
  - Keep hover preview separate from click pinning; create terminal workspaces through the existing split-tree store. Place folder/branch metadata immediately after titles with a slash divider.
- [ ] **UX44**, Desktop tray controls the service and model server, and window close hides to tray. [Spec](SPEC.md#server-model-browser-and-desktop-tray)
  - Electron owns the tray and serialized lifecycle actions. Reuse existing backend channels for model power. Restart the supervised process safely and reload the renderer transport after endpoint changes; explicit Quit performs cleanup. Test manual stop versus automatic crash restart.
- [ ] **UX45**, Verify model, chat and tray changes. [Spec](SPEC.md#server-model-browser-and-desktop-tray)
  - Desktop tests, typecheck and production build; visual checks for both themes and responsive layouts, plus native tray hide/restore and service control. Keep unverified platform checks open.
- [ ] **UX46**, The composer renders markdown live: headings grow, bold is bold, Ctrl/Cmd+B and +I wrap the selection, and the caret and selection line up with the text. [Spec](SPEC.md#chat--cowork-unified-surface)
  - Bug: MS10's overlay sits under a transparent textarea, and any sizing or offset mismatch (padding, gutter, trailing newline) makes the selection drift away from the painted glyphs. A transparent textarea cannot show sized headings at all, so the fix is to change how the composer is built, not to nudge the overlay.

Tests cover both dialects and the conflicting `mmap:false, mlock:true` case.

GPU only writes the full layer count explicitly, Hybrid reveals the layer slider (number input when the file reports no layer count) plus CPU threads, CPU only keeps threads. GPU options disable when the planner found no GPU.

### Composer folder picker (added 2026-10-08)

- [x] **UX53**, [Primary folder](SPEC.md#working-in-a-codebase) picked from the composer's bottom strip, first item before the model. Lists project folders (supporting ones marked), **No folder**, and **Add folder…** (native dialog; the host reuses an already-registered path rather than adding or indexing it twice). Switching or clearing keeps the old primary as supporting, the Folders section's rule; both surfaces now share `renderer/sessionFolders.ts`. Disabled mid-reply. Also fixed: the composer **+** menu's Folder registered the folder but never attached it to the chat; it now attaches it (primary if none, else supporting). Auto-filing now applies only to a chat's first prompt while it has no folders, so it neither overwrites a primary picked before sending nor undoes **No folder**. Verified in an isolated web edition (switch, clear, add, cancel, duplicate path, + menu, Escape, light/dark).

### PF: Speed contract and the Rust engine daemon (added 2026-09-30)

Philip's call: keep the UI on web technology, move the engine into a Rust daemon, make local inference the fastest option for an agent, and hold the UI to one frame (8 ms at 120 Hz) with CI enforcing it. Design: [speed contract](SPEC.md#speed--responsiveness-the-speed-contract), [tokens/s plan](SPEC.md#running-a-model-with-nothing-else-installed), [daemon architecture](#the-rust-engine-daemon-nekkod-and-the-strangler-migration), [performance design](#performance-design-the-speed-contract). IDs use the `PF` prefix so they cannot collide with concurrent `T` numbering.

- [x] **PF1**, CI measures the speed contract and fails a change that breaks it.
  - `scripts/perf/`: headless Chromium over CDP against the web edition, scripted mock provider, seeded 1,000-message chat; keypress-to-paint (composer, terminal), frame work while streaming at 300 tok/s, warm/cold chat switch. p95 against the SPEC budgets; new `perf` CI job with the report as an artifact.
  - Built: `scripts/perf/run.mjs` (budgets in `budgets.mjs`, CDP client, browser finder, mock provider, seed, probes and trace analysis under `lib/`), the `perf` job in `ci.yml` (report to the step summary and the `perf-report` artifact). Latency runs in a browser with the frame-rate limiter off, so a sample is the app's work rather than a wait for a virtual vsync; frame work runs in a second, vsync-paced browser and is the renderer's own `AnimationFrame` spans from a trace (the Long Animation Frames API only reports frames over 50 ms). The terminal row opens a terminal beside the chat (Ctrl+J) and times each key to the frame after its echo is drawn; in the web edition that is still the TS host's pty over the event bus. `--app <checkout>` measures another build for before/after, `--profile`/`--timeline` print where the time went, `check.mjs` is a functional pass (drafts, following the stream, scrolling up, switching mid-reply) and `serve.mjs` leaves the seeded world running. Before on this branch's base (Ryzen 9 9950X3D, Edge 154): composer keypress p95 87.5 ms, frame work 52.2 ms, warm switch 1,564 ms, cold frame 36.4 ms, cold history 1,442 ms; after: 6.3, 2.7, 5.3, 7.9 and 31.2 ms. · Done: 2026-09-30
  - 2026-10-12, harness fix: with the frame-rate limiter off, Chromium still holds the frame after an empty one (a rAF that draws nothing, the harness's own settle before a click, a stepped decoration's tick on the same step) to a 60 Hz tick, so latency samples included up to 16.7 ms of frame-clock idle wake whenever no looping CSS decoration was animating. Proven on a bare page (click right after a double rAF: 16.5 ms p50 idle, 0.6 ms with any animation running; the hold ends one interval after the empty frame) and on the app in an Ubuntu 24.04 container with Chrome stable (the #438 decoration clock: warm switch 18.9, cold frame 18.7, terminal key 37.2 ms p95; with the fix 10.4, 13.0, 4.8). The latency browser now runs a 1 px compositor-only animation outside the app (`FRAME_PUMP`), the report prints the frame wake (`frameWake`, `--no-frame-pump` to compare), slow switches (50 ms or more) carry the long animation frames that overlapped them, and `--only frames` no longer clicks where the composer was before the chat finished laying out.
  - 2026-10-12, decoration clock (`apps/desktop/src/renderer/decorClock.ts`, started from `App.tsx`): the looping decorations named in `DECOR_ANIMATIONS` are paused on `animationstart` and their `currentTime` advanced by a 30 Hz `setInterval` from the start time read once (no `playState`/`effect` reads per tick, which would recalc style); a decoration the stylesheet cancels is dropped on `animationcancel`; the clock stops while the document is hidden or `data-app-inactive`, and stops its timer when nothing is held. First tried in #438 and reverted there because the harness then measured the 60 Hz idle wake (fixed by the frame pump above).
- [x] **PF2**, Typing re-renders only the composer.
  - Selector-based store subscriptions everywhere on the chat path; drafts out of the global store with debounced persistence; memoized transcript rows and stable callbacks; one streaming delivery path per frame (drop ChatPane's duplicate 50 ms flush over `liveRuns`).
  - Built: every `useStore()` whole-store read became a `useShallow` field selector (app shell, workspace view, chat, palette, toasts, boards, settings, onboarding). The draft stays in the pane's state (persisted to `composerDrafts.ts` on the existing debounce); its store mirror for the Context Inspector now trails by 150 ms, and what the draft implies (analyzer, plan rail, Auto's pick, the gauge's draft count) renders from `useDeferredValue`. `liveRuns.ts` is the only fold: it owns the 40k live-buffer cap (cut back at a paragraph break so the kept text stops shifting) and hands the finished run to the pane once (`takeFinishedRun`) so the reply is held until its stored copy lands. `LiveTurn` and the small live gauges read the run once a frame; the pane no longer re-renders per token. Also: `ChatPane`, `WorkspaceCard`, `ContextInspector`, bubbles and step groups memoized, the duplicate `session_meta` case removed, and no-op status updates no longer re-render the workspace view per token. · Done: 2026-09-30
- [x] **PF3**, Long chats render only what is on screen.
  - In-repo `VirtualTranscript` with measured variable heights, bottom anchoring while streaming, scroll-position preservation.
  - Built: `components/agent-console/VirtualTranscript.tsx` with pure helpers in `virtualGeometry.ts` (tested). One ResizeObserver for the scroller, the column and every rendered row; heights cached per chat by row key (12 chats kept), estimates from `estimateRowHeight` for rows never rendered, 500 px overscan. It sticks to the bottom until the reader scrolls up (only an upward scroll lets go, so content re-measured above never unpins it), moves the scroll position by the size change of rows that ended above the viewport, and offers the jump pill when the live turn grows while scrolled up. `scrollToKey` is there for a future jump-to-message; no feature scrolls to a message today. Rows (`toTranscriptRows`) reproduce the old spacing exactly, including the PR card's 8 px gap; row UI state (an opened step list, an edit in progress) lives in `useRowState` so it survives a row scrolling out. · Done: 2026-09-30
- [x] **PF4**, Streaming replies re-parse only the block being written.
  - Block-split markdown with memoized finished blocks; tail block parsed at most once per frame.
  - Built: `markdownSegments` splits chat text at fences and blank lines (every block kind ends at one), and each segment renders through a memoized component keyed by where it starts, so only the tail re-parses; it renders at most once per frame because the live turn does. Documents still parse whole. `Markdown.test.tsx` holds the split output to the whole-text output for every fixture and every prefix of a mixed reply, an unterminated fence included. · Done: 2026-09-30
- [x] **PF5**, Switching chats, windows and tabs is instant.
  - 2026-10-09 follow-up: returning to an already-selected pane preserves workspace/list identities, and unchanged mounted canvases are memoized. This avoids layout-subscriber invalidation and unrelated canvas renders without changing visibility, focus, warm-set size or the speed budgets. Shared Linux switch failures on PRs #394/#396/#399/#400/#401 also occur in their unchanged bases; CI gate resolution remains unverified until the fix is measured there.
  - Summary-only `listSessions`; renderer LRU session cache with stale-while-revalidate; last 3 chat windows kept mounted and hidden; cold chats paint their frame first and the newest history next.
  - Built: a new `sessions:summaries` channel (all five touches) returns `SessionSummary` (the session's fields plus message counts, first prompt, last reply, transcript tokens, stall flag, the last 8 turns capped at 4,000 characters each, PR URLs); the host builds it asynchronously and caches it by file mtime and size, refreshed on every write, so a listing parses only chats that changed. `sessions:list` still returns whole sessions for the CLI and MCP, which count messages. Every renderer consumer of `s.sessions` moved to the summary fields (sidebar cards, board lanes and turns, sub-agent rail, insights, agent classification). `sessionCache.ts` keeps the 8 most recent transcripts (at most about 24 MB of text) and a pane paints from it, then revalidates. The last three workspaces stay mounted, hidden with `content-visibility: hidden` rather than `display: none` (layout and scroll positions are kept, so showing one is a paint, not a style and layout rebuild); a hidden workspace keeps only its chats, and its terminals and browsers mount again when shown, as before. A pane's mount-time fetches and its composer focus wait for the first frame (`afterPaint.ts`), and the composer no longer measures itself on mount when empty. · Done: 2026-09-30
- [x] **PF6**, The engine runs in its own Rust daemon, and a crash no longer takes the window with it. · Done: 2026-09-30
  - Cargo workspace at the root (`crates/nekkod`, `crates/nekko-term`). `nekkod` serves `POST /api/{channel}`, `/api/events` (with `?only=<channel>`), `/api/ws` (one socket: `{id,channel,args}` requests, `{id,result|error}` replies, events without an id) and `/api/term/{id}`; bearer token, Origin allowlist (`null` and `file://` for a packaged page, the dev server origin in dev), Host check against DNS rebinding. Channels not in `routes.rs`'s `OWNED` table forward to the TS backend over HTTP; the backend's `/api/events` is relayed into the hub.
  - TS backend: new entry `apps/desktop/src/backend/index.ts` (built beside `main/index.js` as `out/main/backend.js`), run by nekkod with the app's own Electron binary under `ELECTRON_RUN_AS_NODE`, so it can never import `electron`. It owns the host, the local API server for the CLI/MCP, the workflow loopback listener and the desktop-only channels formerly in `ipc.ts` (`backend/channels.ts`), and serves a dependency-free wire (`backend/wire.ts`, `backend/ws.ts`) with the same routes as the daemon. Ready handshake: `NEKKO_BACKEND_READY {"port":N}` / `NEKKOD_READY {"port":N}` on stdout; stdin EOF is the shutdown signal on every platform, backed by a kill-on-close Windows job object and `PR_SET_PDEATHSIG` on Linux.
  - Electron main (`main/engine-process.ts`) starts nekkod, restarts either process with backoff, and answers only native dialogs, `shell`, app version, the updater and `engine:endpoint`. The preload keeps the `NekkoApi` object as it was but routes `inv` and `on*` to the engine socket (`preload/engine-socket.ts`) instead of `ipcMain`; a call in flight when the engine restarts fails with a readable error rather than being replayed. Without a nekkod binary the app runs the TS backend directly (`mode: 'backend'`) and still works.
  - Packaging: `apps/desktop/scripts/stage-daemon.mjs` builds `nekkod` release and stages it to `resources/bin` (electron-builder `extraResources` → `<resources>/bin`); `dist`/`release` fail without cargo, `npm run local` warns and continues. CI gains a `daemon` job (fmt, clippy `-D warnings`, tests) on Linux, macOS and Windows; the release workflow installs the Rust toolchain.
  - Verified: end-to-end daemon tests with a fake backend (forwarding, auth and origin refusal, event relay without echo, RPC socket, live terminal stream, exit on stdin close); the real backend under the real daemon, from the dev tree and from a packaged `win-unpacked` layout (backend running from inside `app.asar`); the desktop app driven over CDP with an isolated profile. Forwarded call 0.9 ms average in release, daemon-owned call 0.4 ms, daemon ready in ~20 ms, clean shutdown in ~20 ms, no processes left behind.
- [x] **PF7**, The terminal keeps up with anything and never freezes the app. · Done: 2026-09-30
  - `nekko-term`: one reader thread, writer thread and waiter thread per pty; a pump task forwards the first output after a quiet frame at once (so echo is never delayed) and coalesces the rest per 8 ms / 64 KB; 2 MB byte ring realigned to a line start on trim; per-subscriber acknowledgement flow control (1 MB high water; a subscriber over it for 2 s stops counting; 8 MB behind is disconnected). ConPTY opens with a cursor-position query and holds all output until answered, so the daemon answers the first one itself (a terminal nobody is viewing would otherwise look frozen).
  - The TS host forwards pty calls to the daemon when it runs under one (`useTerminalDaemon`), keeping only the agent command logs, so the relay and CLI see the same terminals as the UI; `terminals:list` merges both. Host terminal functions became async.
  - Renderer: `TerminalPane` streams through `window.nekko.openTerminalStream` (new optional `NekkoApi` method; null in the web edition, which keeps the event-bus path), xterm.js + WebGL by default, ghostty-web experimental, a Settings → Terminal renderer choice, and a Nerd Font fallback list so themed prompts render in both renderers.
  - Measured on the desktop app (Windows, RTX 5090 box): keypress to frame in another input while a visible terminal prints 40 MB, 7.1 ms p95 / 7.4 max (before: 7.6 / 10.9), longest frame 4.4 ms (before 20.9); ghostty-web under the same load 27.3 ms p95, hence experimental. Keystroke echo delivered to the renderer: cmd 0.8 ms p50; the pty floor alone is 0.2 ms (cmd), 0.9 ms (Git Bash) and ~15 ms (PowerShell, its own input loop). Idle echo was already sub-millisecond before, so the win is under load and in isolation, not in the idle case.
  - Still to do: the web, npx and Docker editions keep the TS terminals until they run behind nekkod (PF14). The PF1 harness now measures terminal keypress to paint (in the web edition, so on the TS terminal until PF14). Update 2026-10-12: the web edition and the PF1 harness now run behind nekkod (see PF14).
- [x] **PF8**, The model server's router runs in Rust, and a backend crash no longer drops loaded models. · Done: 2026-09-30
  - `crates/nekko-infer`: a `Supervisor` owning every model-server process (spawn with a `{port}` placeholder it fills, health polling with an optional body match, a 200-line log ring per process, kill-on-drop, a watcher that forgets a process that dies) and an `EngineRouter` serving the OpenAI-compatible port: `/health` open, bearer or `x-api-key` auth, CORS as the TS router did it, `/v1/models` from the policy, streaming passthrough with the request counted in flight until the body finishes or the client leaves, image-vs-text route checks, and serving the same config twice as a no-op. New daemon channels `infer:serve/stopServing/serving/spawn/kill/list/log`.
  - Split kept deliberately: the TS engine (`engine/server.ts`) still decides what to load and how (arguments, companions, drafts, VRAM measurement, eviction, idle TTL, load-on-demand policy) and, under the daemon, runs it through `infer:spawn` instead of `child_process`; it reads residency back every 3 s (`infer:list`), reattaches to models still running when the backend restarts, and answers the router's `engine:routerLoad/routerModels/routerModel` calls. Endpoint setting changes re-serve the listener without unloading models. The web edition keeps the TS listener.
  - Verified live on the RTX 5090 with the real daemon, backend and llama.cpp b11011: engine start through the backend, a load-on-demand request streamed through the Rust router, the backend killed mid-session (daemon restarted it), the loaded model answering while the backend was down, the restarted backend adopting it, stop leaving no process. Router overhead: 27.39 ms against 27.29 ms straight to the model server (median of 30 one-token requests). Also fixed in passing: window bounds had stopped saving after #215 (main read the host's uninitialised `dataDir`), and main now imports only `@nekko-agent/host/user-data` instead of the whole host.
- [x] **PF9**, A chat's prompt cache survives between turns. · Done: 2026-09-30
  - Plan changed once the pinned llama.cpp source was read: b11011 already keeps per-slot caches, picks slots by prompt similarity and has an 8 GB RAM prompt cache, so explicit session pinning and our own disk save/restore were not built. Instead `buildArgs` passes `--cache-reuse 256` and `--no-cache-idle-slots` (the default copies every idle slot to RAM on each new request, which cost 0.5-0.8 s on a new chat's first turn and made revisits slower). Measured: new-chat first turn 2.3 s to 1.7 s, revisit of an evicted chat (six chats, four slots) 1.7 s to 0.8 s.
- [x] **PF10**, Speculative decoding is on by default. · Done: 2026-09-30
  - `--spec-default` (llama.cpp's n-gram-mod lookup, no draft model) unless `speculative: false`; kept for vision models, verified with a projector loaded (edit 633 tok/s, decode 152), where llama.cpp itself drops only `--cache-reuse`. Optional `draftModelId` (new on `LoadParams` and the saved preset) adds `--spec-draft-model … --spec-draft-ngl all --spec-type draft-simple`; the Advanced drawer gets a Speculative decoding switch and a Draft model picker limited to same-architecture models at most a quarter of the target's size. Measured: code edit 117 to 534 tok/s, decode 127 to 154; a 7.5B draft on a 12B target raised the edit to 624 but halved prose (74 tok/s), hence opt-in. Still open: the fit planner does not yet count a draft's memory.
- [x] **PF11**, Sub-agents share one loaded model instead of queueing. · Done: 2026-09-30
  - Already true: llama.cpp b11011 defaults to automatic slots (4 here) with a unified KV pool, and the engine does not override it. Four concurrent requests: 256-343 tok/s total against Ollama's 113 (one at a time by default). Follow-up: the Advanced drawer's "Parallel slots" hint still says each slot gets its own full cache, which is no longer how the default works.
- [x] **PF12**, Each machine gets its fastest backend, including MLX on Apple Silicon. · Done: 2026-09-30
  - Shipped: `gpu-adapters.ts` reads the OS's adapter list (Win32_VideoController vendor ids, Linux `/sys/class/drm`), and the build ranking offers Vulkan to AMD and Intel GPUs that `nvidia-smi` cannot see, where they used to get the CPU build. Also shipped: every new flag goes through `probeFlags` (the binary's own `--help`, read once), so an engine installed before these flags existed still starts.
  - MLX shipped (unverified on a Mac; this machine is Windows): `engine/mlx.ts` installs pinned `uv` 0.12.21 (a tracked, checksum-verified download job) which installs pinned `mlx-lm` 0.31.3 with its own Python into `<engine>/mlx` (system Python untouched; uninstall deletes the folder; a user's own `mlx_lm.server` on PATH is used and never removed). The library recognises MLX model folders (`config.json` plus `.safetensors`, and MLX quantization or `mlx` in the folder or publisher name), including LM Studio's MLX downloads, as `format: 'mlx'` rows with an MLX tag; off Apple Silicon they say why they cannot run. Loads go through the same router as `mlx_lm.server --model <dir>` (health `/health`, same as llama.cpp), with a same-format draft model passed as `--draft-model`. Install card beside the image runtime's, rendered only on Apple Silicon.
  - MLX catalog downloads, done 2026-10-01 (unverified on a Mac): on Apple Silicon the catalog has a GGUF | MLX switch. MLX search uses Hugging Face's `mlx` tag with text-generation models only (`mlx_lm.server` serves those; mlx-community also publishes audio models), and an MLX repo is one whole-folder download (its config, weights, index and tokenizer files; READMEs and images left behind) into `<models>/mlx/<owner>_<repo>/`, which is what makes the library read it as MLX even without a quantization block. A repo that ships both GGUF and MLX offers both on its page. Elsewhere MLX folders are never offered. Checked live against Hugging Face (search, sizes, file lists); no model was downloaded.

- [ ] **PF14**, The rest of the engine moves to Rust, service by service, and the TS host is retired.
  - In order: session storage, providers + agent loop, tools/guardrails/changes, MCP, connectors/messaging/workflows, relay agent, training. Then the web, npx and Docker editions run behind `nekkod` (per-platform binaries as npm optional dependencies).
  - Web edition behind nekkod, done 2026-10-12: `apps/server` finds `nekkod` (`NEKKOD_PATH`, the `@nekko-agent/nekkod-<platform>-<arch>` npm package, `bin/` beside the script, the newest cargo build in the checkout), starts it on `127.0.0.1:0` with a per-launch token and the data dir, and supervises it (`src/nekkod.ts`, restart with backoff, stdin EOF to stop, SIGKILL after a grace period). `nekkod` runs the TS backend as this same script with `--nekko-backend` under the server's Node (`src/backend.ts`: fastify on loopback, `POST /api/:channel` and `/api/events` with every host event, the web channel overrides, `useTerminalDaemon`/`useEngineDaemon`; `--experimental-websocket` on Node < 22). The public fastify server keeps `createApiSecurityHook` for HTTP, checks socket upgrades the same way (Host, Origin, bearer or `?token=`), forwards channel calls over a keep-alive agent with no client timeout and splices `/api/events`, `/api/ws` and `/api/term/:id` to the daemon byte for byte with the daemon's token (`src/proxy.ts`); the public token is stripped from what children inherit. Webhooks reach the backend's host through the internal `web:dispatchWebhook` channel (refused from outside). `web:capabilities` tells the page whether terminals stream; `web-client.ts` then opens `/api/term/:id` (`renderer/terminal-socket.ts`). `NEKKO_DAEMON=auto|nekkod|ts`. nekkod's non-`loop:run` body limit rose from 2 MB to 25 MB to match the server's public limit (image attachments arrive as `POST` here, not over the desktop's socket). CI's perf job builds `nekkod` release and runs with `NEKKO_DAEMON=nekkod`; the harness logs the engine and its terminal note names it. Verified on Windows with an isolated data dir and ports 4510-4514: chats listed and opened in headless Chromium, a terminal pane streaming over `/api/term` (with and without `NEKKO_TOKEN`), a chat turn on the scripted model (streamed text equal to the saved reply), the backend and the daemon each killed and restarted, the server killed outright leaving no daemon, backend or shell, and token/Host/Origin refusals (401/400/403) on HTTP and sockets. Keystroke echo through the public server to Command Prompt: 0.88 ms p50 (desktop direct to the daemon: 0.8 ms). PF1 terminal keypress to paint on this box (PowerShell, whose own ~15 ms input loop dominates): in-process TS 16.7 ms p50 / 18.2 p95; behind nekkod 17.3 / 18.5 and 17.1 / 18.1. Still open: relay-agent mode, and the duplicate terminal output on `/api/events` (nekkod publishes it to the bus as well as the stream; the PF1 probe times echo off the bus).
  - npx package with nekkod, done 2026-10-12: `apps/server/scripts/nekkod-packages.mjs` writes `@nekko-agent/nekkod-{win32-x64,linux-x64,linux-arm64,darwin-arm64}` (no Intel macOS: ort-sys has no prebuilt ONNX Runtime for it) (`os`/`cpu` set, the binary plus the ONNX Runtime sidecars such as `DirectML.dll`, mode 755), and `bundle.mjs` lists them as `optionalDependencies` of `nekko-agent-web` at its own version (`--with-local-nekkod` copies a cargo build into `bin/` for trying a tarball). `.github/workflows/nekkod-npm.yml` builds the matrix (Linux on Ubuntu 24.04 x64/arm runners: the prebuilt ONNX Runtime needs a GCC 13 libstdc++), called by `release.yml`, whose new `publish-web` job publishes the platform packages and then the bundle, gated like `publish-cli` (`NPM_TOKEN` or Trusted Publishing; skips versions already published). Also fixed, all pre-existing and fatal at startup in the published bundle: node-pty's dynamic require of its prebuild (now external and a dependency), CommonJS modules requiring Node built-ins inside the ESM bundle (a real `require` from `createRequire` in the banner), and the embedded CLI reading `../package.json` beside the bundle (version inlined at build time); the server's version now reads its own `package.json` in both layouts. Verified on Windows: packed the bundle and the win32-x64 package, installed both into an empty project (npm skipped the other platforms), `npx --no-install nekko-agent-web` started with `engine: nekkod (npm package ...)`, then a chat turn, a terminal pane over `/api/term` in headless Chromium, and no process left after killing the server. Owner action before the first publish: own the `@nekko-agent` npm scope and register `release.yml` as Trusted Publisher for `nekko-agent-web` and each `@nekko-agent/nekkod-*` package (or keep `NPM_TOKEN`).
  - Docker image behind nekkod, done 2026-10-12: a `rust:1-slim-trixie` stage builds `nekkod` (`g++`, `libssl-dev`; cargo registry and target as BuildKit cache mounts) into `apps/server/dist/bin/`, where the server finds it. Base images moved to Debian 13 (`node:22-trixie-slim`): the prebuilt ONNX Runtime `nekkod` links needs GCC 13's libstdc++, which bookworm lacks (the first build failed on `_M_replace_cold`), and Node 22 has the global WebSocket the backend's terminal client uses. The runtime adds `tini` (PID 1: forwards `docker stop`, reaps orphans), `libssl3`, `NEKKO_DAEMON=nekkod` and a `HEALTHCHECK` on `/health` requiring the daemon ready. `docker-compose.yml` unchanged. Verified with Docker Desktop 29.8.1 on Windows: image built, container `healthy` within 11 s on `127.0.0.1:4530` with `NEKKO_TOKEN`; `engine: nekkod (bundled)`; process tree tini, server, nekkod, backend; a chat turn on the scripted model on the host (`host.docker.internal`); a bash terminal over `/api/term` with echo at 2.4 ms p50 through the published port; 401 without the token; the shell's environment without any token; `docker stop` in 0.7 s with exit code 0.
  - Session storage, read side, done 2026-10-01: new crate `crates/nekko-store` serves `sessions:summaries`, `sessions:list`, `session:get` and `session:images` from `<data>/sessions` (mtime+size summary cache, ids validated before they touch a path, which the TS `pathFor` never did), run off the async threads; nekkod owns them when Electron main passes `dataDir`. The summarizer is a port of `summarizeSession` down to JavaScript's UTF-16 lengths, `trim`/`s` whitespace set and `JSON.stringify` number format, held to it by a golden set the TS function writes (`packages/host/src/session-summary.golden.test.ts`, which fails if the file goes stale) and `crates/nekko-store/tests/golden.rs` (exact match, key order included, 11 fixtures). `saveSession` now writes a temp file and renames it, so a concurrent reader never sees half a chat. Measured with the TS side kept busy (context previews and library scans): opening a 7.1 MB image chat 403 ms -> 13.6 ms p50, listing 301 chats 797 ms -> 22 ms; idle 28 -> 14 ms and 30 -> 21 ms.
  - Session writes, the UI's own, done 2026-10-01: `session:create`, `session:delete`, `session:setOptions`, `session:setWorkspace`, `session:setSupportingWorkspaces`, `session:setAttachments`, `session:truncate`, `spec:setLinked`, `chat:queue`, `chat:dequeue` are nekko-store's (`write.rs`), serialized per store and saved atomically in `saveSession`'s exact layout. Held to the TS functions byte for byte by a second golden set: `golden/ops.json` run by `packages/host/src/session-writes.golden.test.ts` and `crates/nekko-store/tests/golden.rs` (it caught serde_json's swap-remove reordering keys, now `shift_remove`). Running-turn writes stay in the TS host until the agent loop moves.
  - Session clear-by-date implemented: the daemon owns `sessions:clear` with a data directory; otherwise it forwards to TS. Inclusive local today/month boundaries use `updatedAt` with JS coercion and setter order. Clear and single delete await the host-owned command-log queue and tombstone callback before removing JSON; callback failure or an unavailable backend preserves JSON. TS/Rust golden and timezone fixtures cover retained files, malformed JSON, summary-cache refresh, midnight gaps/repeats and skipped days. Focused host callback and real-daemon HTTP tests cover queued writes, racing appends, unlink failures and later append suppression. Windows verification passes; cross-platform CI remains pending. PF14 stays open: running-turn writes, remaining engine services and web/npx/Docker daemon adoption are not complete.
  - Found and fixed on the way: `setSessionOptions` assigned the whole patch (`Object.assign`), so any caller could set any field, the transcript included; both sides now apply only the listed options. And a running turn's save wrote back the whole chat as it read it at the turn's start, undoing a rename, pin, or option change made meanwhile (only the queue was merged back); `saveTurnSession` now keeps the user's fields from disk and the user's own title. Verified live: a chat created by the daemon, a turn run by the TS host on Gemma 4 12B, a follow-up queued and the chat renamed through the daemon mid-turn; the queued prompt ran, and the rename survived.
  - Turn context, done 2026-10-01 (groundwork for the agent loop; not wired in yet): new crate `crates/nekko-context` ports `buildSystemPrompt`, `assembleContext` / `renderContextBlock`, and the disk gatherers `collectGuidelines` (with its pointer and duplicate rules), `collectAttached`, `listMemory` / `parseMemory`, plus Node's `path.join` normalization so item ids and paths match. The JavaScript string, number and JSON rules both ports need moved into `crates/nekko-js` (UTF-16 lengths and slices, `trim`, `Number()`, `JSON.stringify`). Parity is exact on a tree built at test time from `golden/fixtures.json` (so no checkout's line endings can change it): `packages/host/src/context.golden.test.ts` writes what the TS functions produce, including a CRLF memory file, invalid UTF-8, a byte-order mark, a pointer `CLAUDE.md`, a duplicate guideline across workspaces, exclusions and pins, and `crates/nekko-context/tests/golden.rs` matches it. Connector fetches and workspace search stay in TS and arrive as snippets.
  - Loop helpers, done 2026-10-01 (not wired in yet): new crate `crates/nekko-loop` with `repairInterruptedHistory` and the resume strings, the runaway guard (in UTF-16 units, so it trips on the same delta as the TS one), `windowHistory` and `asSeenByChatModel`. Held to the TS functions by `golden/cases.json` (8 recorded streams including emoji and no-newline cycles, 4 interrupted histories, 8 window limits including 0, negative and fractional, 6 image-turn messages) run by `packages/host/src/loop-helpers.golden.test.ts` and `crates/nekko-loop/tests/golden.rs`. The runaway note lost its em dash on both sides.
  - The agent loop, done 2026-10-01 (not wired in yet): `crates/nekko-loop/src/run.rs` ports `runAgent`, generic over a `ModelClient` (a stream of text, reasoning, tool-call, usage and done chunks) and a `ToolRunner`, with a shared `Cancel`: runaway cut-off and trimming, the empty-turn nudge (reusing the same turn), tool results including a runner that throws, interrupted endings that keep the partial reply (and read "Stopped" when the user asked), resume nudges, history windowing, and the tools-withheld wrap-up pass at the step limit. `packages/host/src/agent-loop.golden.test.ts` plays 15 scripted runs through the real TS loop against a fake provider and records the events, the transcript and every request it sends; `crates/nekko-loop/tests/runs.rs` plays the same scripts and matches all three exactly (ids and timestamps normalized). The stalled-reply message lost its em dash on both sides. Next, `chat:send` in the daemon, in this order, each its own PR with the same golden-parity bar:
    1. Adapters: `nekko-agent`'s providers as a `ModelClient` (the loop's `Cancel` hooks fire the provider's `AbortController`), `nekko-tools`' executor as a `ToolRunner`; a live smoke of the Rust loop on the engine's llama.cpp. Providers half done 2026-10-01 (`crates/nekko-chat`): an end-to-end test drives the real OpenAI-compatible provider over a fake wire (a tool call streamed across chunks, its result sent back, the answer; Stop ending a silent stream at once), and a live run on llama.cpp b11011 with Gemma 4 12B called a tool and answered in 300 ms (first text at 221 ms). The tools half waits on `nekko-tools`.
    2. and 3. Pending input and the turn itself: done 2026-10-12, see "The whole turn in the daemon" below. Still to do for them: `chat:send`'s admission (the worktree setup, MCP sync, the decision runner, connector and index snippets, the token refresh) moves into the daemon, so a turn's first text stops waiting on a busy host.
    4. After a turn: the auto-title and the queue drain, done 2026-10-12 (see "After a turn in the daemon" below); suggestions and prompt fill already run on `provider:complete`.
    5. Fold the three copies of the JavaScript helpers (`nekko-js`, `nekko-agent::js`, `nekko-tools::js`) into `nekko-js`. `nekko-agent`'s copy folded in 2026-10-01 (the stricter `Number()` grammar and the template-literal `display` are now shared; every golden suite still matches); `nekko-tools`' folded in with it (lengths, cuts, `trim`, truthiness, `display` and numbers come from `nekko-js`, whose `number_to_string` now follows Number::toString exactly: it printed `1e-6` for `0.000001` and integers past 2^53 unrounded). What stays in `nekko-tools::js` is the tools' own: `String()` of an absent argument, `split`/`replace` match counting and `$&` expansion, Node's argument-type errors.
  - The agent run in the daemon, done 2026-10-01 (a halfway step to 3.): `loop:run` in `crates/nekkod/src/loops.rs` runs a chat turn's reply on the Rust loop and providers, while the TS host still decides the turn (provider and fresh token, tools, context, system prompt) and still runs every tool through `loop:tool`, so approvals, questions, MCP and sub-agents are unchanged. The daemon sends each event to the UI itself, in order; the host hears them through `loop:event` (deltas batched every 100 ms, the transcript at each tool result and at the end) to save checkpoints and relay them, and re-emits them marked `relayOnly`, which nekkod drops. A reply's `done` or `error` reaches the UI only after the host has saved it. `loop:run` returns at once and `loop:end` reports the end, since a run can outlast Node's five-minute fetch header timeout. `packages/host/src/daemon-loop.ts` wraps it as a generator with `runAgent`'s shape, so `chat.ts` picks a route and nothing else changes. Anthropic subscription turns (which read rate-limit headers) stay on the TS loop, and `NEKKO_AGENT_LOOP=ts` turns the route off. Verified live on Gemma 4 12B: a `read_file` turn (events in order, none duplicated, the streamed text equal to the saved reply), a `bash` approval in ask mode, and Stop saving the partial reply as interrupted. Token delivery measured against the TS route, idle and with 8 concurrent context previews and library scans on the host: first text 714 vs 843 ms idle and 729 vs 710 ms busy, gaps between text events p50 4-7 ms, p95 8-9 ms, max 39-47 ms on both. That load does not yet starve the TS event loop, so the gain shows once more of the turn moves.
  - The whole turn in the daemon, done 2026-10-12 (steps 2 and 3): `chat:turn` in `crates/nekkod/src/turns.rs` drives a chat turn end to end. The TS host still admits it (provider, session, sandbox, worktree setup with its setup command, offline and endpoint checks), syncs MCP, asks the decision runner, fetches connector and index snippets and resolves the provider with a fresh token, and puts all of that in the spec (`packages/host/src/daemon-turn.ts`). The rest is the daemon's: the tool list and system prompt (new crate `crates/nekko-turn`: ports of `turnTools`, `turnSystemPrompt`, `routingPrompt`, `applyTurnInput`, `parseAskRequest`, `formatAskAnswers`, `applyPlanUpdate`, `planEcho`, `TurnStatsAccumulator`, the title prompt and the queued-prompt options, with the tool specs and fixed text embedded from the TS through `src/catalog.json`), guidelines, attached files and memory read from disk (`nekko-context`), the user's message, `activeRun`, every checkpoint and the end saved through `nekko-store` (`save_turn`, the port of `saveTurnSession`) before the UI hears of the step, the loop and the built-in tools, `ask_user`, `set_chat_title`, `complete_session` and an ordinary chat's `update_plan`, the turn stats on the last message, and steering left over at the end queued. Pending input lives in the daemon: approvals (the built-in tools', and through `turn:approval` and `turn:approved` those of the tools still in the host) and questions with their unattended limit; `tool:approve`, `chat:answer`, `chat:pending` (merged with the host's own), `chat:steer`, `chat:abort` and `chat:running` are answered there, and forwarded only when the host owns the turn. The host runs the tools that have not moved (`turn:tool`: MCP, `spawn_agent`, `delegation_targets`, the browser and capture, processes, `fetch_url`, `agent_watch`, `decide`, training reports and a goal run's plan, whose change comes back as a patch) and tool hooks (`turn:hook`), and records usage, the reply log and subscription limits from the events it is sent (`turn:event`, deltas batched, marked `relayOnly` so the phone relay and messaging see them and the UI does not see them twice). Anthropic subscription turns moved too: the Rust provider hands their rate-limit headers to `turn:headers`. A host restarted mid-turn leaves the daemon's turns alone (`turn:running`). `NEKKO_AGENT_LOOP=ts`, sandbox turns, a daemon without a data directory and the no-daemon editions keep the in-process route; a daemon too old for `chat:turn` still gets `loop:run`. Hooks added in the middle of a turn apply from the next turn (the TS route re-read them per call).
    - Parity: `packages/host/src/turn-plan.golden.test.ts` (89 tool lists, 12 prompts, 9 transcript inputs, 5 title prompts and 9 cleanings, 3 queued prompts, 7 question parses and 4 answer sets, 7 plan updates, 3 stats runs) and `session-turn-save.golden.test.ts` (9 saves over files the user changed mid-turn, queue claims, chats deleted mid-turn) write golden sets from the real TS functions, failing when stale, which `crates/nekko-turn/tests/golden.rs` and `crates/nekko-store/tests/golden.rs` match as text, key order included. The context golden now also covers the prompt's custom instructions, about-user, turn wrapper and checkout sections, which the Rust prompt had been missing. Four real-binary tests in `crates/nekkod/tests/daemon.rs` play whole turns against a scripted model and a fake host: a question answered from `chat:pending`, a host-run tool, an approval for a built-in and one for a host tool, Stop, and a queued prompt that changed before it started. Across the whole stack (the desktop backend under nekkod, `scripts/bench-chat-turn.mjs --dump-requests`), both routes send the model byte-identical requests.
    - Verified live on Gemma 4 E4B (llama.cpp on the CPU, since the GPU was in use): a streamed reply, then a `write_file` in ask mode, approved through the daemon and written.
    - Measured with `scripts/bench-chat-turn.mjs --fake` (a model streaming 40 tokens 15 ms apart, so the numbers are the engine's), 10 turns per route, idle and with the host's event loop saturated by 4 concurrent workspace-index searches over 2,500 files (about 110 ms of blocking each). Main is `loop:run` (origin/main 8a783119), "turn" is `chat:turn`, TS is `NEKKO_AGENT_LOOP=ts`:

      | | idle: main / turn / TS | host busy: main / turn / TS |
      | --- | --- | --- |
      | approval, `tool:approve` to `tool_result` | 2.5 / 5.6-6.2 / 3.1-3.5 ms | 451 / 5.1-5.8 / 800-1004 ms |
      | approval, `tool:approve` answered | 1.9-2.2 / 1.0-1.1 / 1.4-1.5 ms | 450-451 / 1.0-1.3 / 417-430 ms |
      | first text | 16-19 / 13-14 / 13 ms | 1221-1480 / 1048-1268 / 1521-1759 ms |
      | gaps between text events | p50 15.6 ms, max 31-32 ms on all three | main and turn p50 15.6 ms, max 30-31 ms; TS batched (p50 0 ms, max 387-446 ms) |

      The idle approval is 3 ms slower than main's because the daemon now saves the step before showing its result, as the TS host always has; `loop:run` showed it first. First text under load still waits on the host, which admits `chat:send` (the remaining step above).
  - After a turn in the daemon, done 2026-10-12 (step 4): the daemon writes the chat's title itself once a turn ends (`generate_title` in `crates/nekkod/src/turns.rs`: the ported title prompt and cleaning from `nekko-turn`, the call on the turn's own provider and model through `provider:complete`'s code, landed by `SessionStore::set_auto_title`, the port of the new `applyAutoTitle` in sessions.ts, only while `titleAuto` is still true), sends the call's usage to the host's usage log (`turn:usage`) and its `session_meta` to the UI and the host's listeners (`turn:notify`). It also runs the next queued prompt: the host admits it (`turn:prepare`, the same `admitTurn` as `chat:send`), and the daemon drives it as the next turn of the chain (`next` in the `turn:end` summary), so `sendChat` resolves once the queue is worked through, as before, with each turn's TurnEnd hooks and linked spec still run by the host. A queued prompt that can only run in process (its chat became a sandbox chat) is handed back (`drain: 'host'`). Parity: the title prompt and cleaning were already in the turn golden set; `applyAutoTitle` joins the session-writes golden set; a real-binary test (`crates/nekkod/tests/daemon.rs`) runs a turn whose follow-up was queued mid-reply and checks the transcript, the claimed queue, the landed title and its usage. Measured with `scripts/bench-chat-turn.mjs --fake --runs 5`, from a reply's `done` to the queued follow-up's first text: idle main 23 ms, step 3 20 ms, this 21 ms, TS 20 ms; with the host's event loop saturated (4 concurrent index searches) main 1101 ms, step 3 952 ms, this 790 ms, TS 806 ms. The follow-up still waits on the host to admit it, like `chat:send`; moving the admission is what removes that.
  - Usage, replies and limits in the daemon, done 2026-10-12 (the first of the turn's callbacks to leave the host): new crate `crates/nekko-usage` ports `usageSummary` and `replyStats` (over the same `settings.json`, `usage.jsonl` and `replies.jsonl`), `estimateCost` with the price tables embedded from the TS (`src/pricing.json`), the usage, reply and quota-history lines (`usage-records.ts` and `quotaHistoryRecord`, split out of `chat.ts` and `quota-history.ts` as pure functions), and the pure half of `limits.ts` (Anthropic headers and usage JSON, ChatGPT WHAM, OpenRouter `/key`, window naming, merging and order, Retry-After, error classes, backoff), with a slice of `Date.parse` and `toISOString` and JavaScript's key order, `NaN`-as-`null` and coercions. `crates/nekkod/src/accounts.rs` is the stateful half: `usage:summary`, `limits:get`, `limits:problem`, `limits:headers`, `limits:poll`, `limits:clear` (snapshot per key, 30 s throttle, one poll per key at a time, problems with backoff, quota history), and a daemon-driven turn now writes its own usage and reply lines, records Anthropic subscription headers and polls after a subscription reply (`turn:account`, from `facts.account`), so `turn:event` no longer accounts and `turn:headers` is gone from that route; the title call's usage (`turn:usage` since step 4) is written here too (`sideUsageRecord`). The host keeps two things and is asked for them: renewing a sign-in (`oauth:refresh`; a refresh rotates the refresh token, so it keeps one writer, and the daemon reads the renewed token back from `tokens.json`, so no token crosses the wire) and the phone relay (`limits:notify`, re-emitted `relayOnly`). Owned only with a data directory, and only then advertised, since a host that delegated limits to a daemon forwarding them back would loop; without a daemon (web, server, cloud) and on the TS route the TS services run as before, and `limits.ts` becomes a client of the daemon when one owns them. Parity: `packages/host/src/usage.golden.test.ts` (175 cost estimates, 9 summaries over real files including torn lines, `null` counts, integer-like keys and dates before 1970, 6 usage lines, 18 reply lines, 18 side-call lines, 6 quota lines, and the limit parsers: 6 header sets, 6 usage answers, 5 WHAM answers, 7 OpenRouter answers, merges, Retry-After, 11 error classes, backoffs, window names) written from the real functions, failing when stale, matched as `JSON.stringify` text by `crates/nekko-usage/tests/golden.rs`; it needed serde_json's `float_roundtrip`, since the default parser is one unit off on prices like 0.049999999999999996. Real-binary tests: a turn's lines, the summary, a header snapshot read back, history, `limits:notify`, a signed-out problem, and a daemon without a data directory forwarding all of it.
    - Found and fixed on the way: since `chat:turn` moved, Anthropic subscription rate limits were never recorded on the daemon route. The headers arrive as an array of pairs, and `headerMap` took an array for a `Headers` object (both have `forEach`), so it threw on the index it was handed as a name.
    - Verified live on Gemma 4 12B (the engine's llama.cpp on the GPU, isolated data dir): three turns, one with an approved `write_file`, left 4 usage lines and 3 reply lines written by the daemon, and the daemon's `usage:summary` reported them. `NEKKO_AGENT_LOOP=ts` still accounts through the host.
    - Measured with `scripts/bench-chat-turn.mjs --fake --usage 20` (20 `usage:summary` calls over a 20,000-record log), main's nekkod (the TS host answers) against this one, idle and with 4 workspace-index searches keeping the host busy: idle 40.4 ms median (TS) / 36.9 ms (daemon), busy 397 ms (p95 493) / 37.7 ms (p95 40). The first daemon cut was 61 ms idle, from pricing every record from scratch (lower-casing, two regex replacements and a table walk); a memo per model id fixed it. Under load the TS route also had one turn's lines still unwritten when the turns ended (6 of 7), which the daemon route never shows: the lines are written before the UI hears `done`.
  - MCP inside the daemon turn, done 2026-10-12 (the second callback to leave the host): a daemon-driven turn now syncs its MCP servers and calls them itself. A host that sees `turn:mcp` in `daemon:info` stops syncing at admission and hands the server list over (`facts.mcpServers`, none for an offline chat); the daemon syncs the enabled ones (`Mcp::sync`), offers their tools from its own snapshot, and runs an `mcp__*` call in the turn's tool runner: the approval (ask mode, or the ask-everything sandbox policy) is parked with the rest of the turn's input in the daemon and the call goes straight to the server, so neither `turn:tool` nor `turn:approval` touches the host. The approval rule is `hostToolApproval` (split out of `chat.ts` into `turn-plan.ts`, shared by `spawn_agent`), held to the TS by 72 more cases in the turn golden set and ported as `nekko_turn::host_tool_approval`. Hypergate discovery moved too: `mcp:hypergate` (probe, no side effects) and `mcp:hypergate:connect` (probe, claim the agent token, then the host saves the entry through `mcp:hypergate:save`, since it owns the settings, and the daemon brings the gateway's tools online) are `crates/nekkod/src/hypergate.rs`, a port of `detectHypergate`/`resolveHypergate` with the same time budgets and the same `HYPERGATE_URL`/`NEKKO_MCP_URL` defaults; `packages/host/src/hypergate.golden.test.ts` plays 10 scripted Hypergates (current, pre-agent, refused, not Hypergate, not JSON, `null` bodies, an error page) through the TS functions and the Rust test the same answers through the port, matched as text. A daemon that would not take the turn leaves it to this host, which then syncs as before; without a daemon, or with `NEKKO_AGENT_LOOP=ts`, nothing changes. Real-binary tests: an ask-mode turn whose MCP call is approved in the daemon and answered by an HTTP MCP server with no host round trip, and Hypergate found and connected.
    - Verified live on Gemma 4 12B with a loopback MCP server: the model called `mcp__bench__lookup`, the approval was answered through the daemon and the result came back 4.9 ms after it.
    - Measured with `scripts/bench-chat-turn.mjs --fake --mcp` (new: a third turn per run calling a loopback MCP tool with an approval), 5 runs, main's nekkod against this one, idle and with 4 workspace-index searches keeping the host busy: `tool:approve` to the MCP call's `tool_result` 4.2 / 4.0 ms idle and 623 / 4.5 ms busy; first text under load 1082 / 778 ms, since admission no longer waits on `mcp:sync`.
  - Sub-agents driven by the daemon, done 2026-10-12 (the third callback): a daemon-driven turn runs `spawn_agent` itself when its host says it can make children (`facts.spawn`, `turn:spawn` in `daemon:info`). The approval (`hostToolApproval`) is parked in the daemon. The host is asked once per attempt (`spawn:prepare`), and only for what needs it: checking the call against the chat, the settings and the target provider's model list, creating the child session with the host's defaults (`prepareSubAgent`, split out of `runSubAgent`), and admitting the child's turn (`admitTurn` + `buildDaemonTurn`, the child's events relayed to the parent's listener, so the phone relay and messaging see them as before; `adoptTurn` runs its after-work when it ends, with nobody in the host waiting). The daemon drives the child as its own turn (`Turns::launch`, which also hands back the last `error` the child reported), stops it when the parent is stopped, resumes it once after a recoverable failure (`canResumeChildFailure`, `MAX_CHILD_RESUMES`), and answers with the child's last written message or the interrupted message with its saved progress (`subAgentAnswer`, `childInterruptedMessage`, `subAgentFailure`, now pure in `delegation-recovery.ts` and ported as `nekko_turn::delegate`, held to the TS by `delegation.golden.test.ts`: 17 resume decisions, 7 answers, 14 interrupted messages with UTF-16 cuts, 9 coded failures). Depth and limit rules are unchanged (they are the host's checks). Real-binary tests: a parent that delegates and answers with the child's reply, the child a separate daemon turn and `spawn_agent` never run in the host; and Stop on the parent stopping the child.
    - Verified live on Gemma 4 12B: the model delegated with `spawn_agent`, the approval was answered through the daemon, and the child's answer came back as the tool result (2.6 s for the whole child turn).
    - Measured with `scripts/bench-chat-turn.mjs --fake --spawn` (new: a turn per run that delegates and is approved), 5 runs, main's nekkod against this one: `tool:approve` to the parent's `tool_result`, which spans the whole child turn, 682 / 674 ms idle and 2368 / 2021 ms with 4 workspace-index searches keeping the host busy. What remains under load is the child's admission, which still waits on the host's event loop (settings, the child session, worktree setup, connector snippets, a fresh token): the same `chat:send` admission step that keeps the first text of any turn waiting.
  - The `decide` tool in the daemon, done 2026-10-12 (the fourth callback): when the host picked the local decision model for a turn (`facts.decideWith: "local"`), the daemon runs `decide` on its own resident model (`DecideService`, the one `decide:run` serves), where the host used to take the call only to send it straight back to the daemon. The request is built and checked as the host does (`decideRequestFromTool` from core and `checkDecisionRequest`, split out of `engine/decisions.ts`), ported as `nekko_turn::decide` with JavaScript's key order, since the request is the model's input, and held to the TS by `decide.golden.test.ts` (18 inputs: described and plain options, integer-like ids and labels, repeated ids and labels, `null` and object states, the 50,000-unit limit in UTF-16, 65 questions, junk items). The answer is written as the host wrote it. TypeSafe's Jev stays in the host (it holds the API key and calls a remote service). Real-binary test: a turn whose `decide` calls fail validation and then find no model loaded, both answered by the daemon with no host round trip. Not verified with a real Laya model: none is downloaded on this machine.
  - `fetch_url` and background processes in the daemon, done 2026-10-12 (the fifth and sixth callbacks): a daemon-driven turn runs `fetch_url` itself (new crate `crates/nekko-web`: `fetchUrl`, `htmlToText`, `decodeEntities` and `describeFetched` ported; the HTML pass runs the same JavaScript regexes on `regress` over UTF-16 through `nekko-tools::jsre`, which gained `exec` and a global `replace_all`; charsets through `encoding_rs`, WHATWG URLs through `url`), with the ask-everything approval parked in the daemon. `start_process`, `read_process` and `kill_process` run in `nekko-tools::process` (a port of `processes.ts` and the tools' cases in `executeTool`: same shell, guardrails, limits, buffers cut in UTF-16 units, messages), the registry kept by the daemon across turns, killed with the chat's Stop (`chat:abort` in the daemon) and at daemon exit (and by its job object on a crash); output reaches the agent terminal through `loop:log` as before. Held to the TS by `packages/host/src/web-fetch.golden.test.ts` (15 HTML documents, 4 entity strings, 20 replies: charsets, BOMs, sniffing, redirects, byte and character caps, refused binaries and schemes, written by the real functions; fails when stale) and `crates/nekko-web/tests/golden.rs`, plus unit tests for the process registry and an end-to-end daemon test (`a_daemon_turn_fetches_pages_and_runs_background_processes_itself`: both asked for in ask mode, neither touching `turn:tool`). Deliberate differences: process output is decoded across chunk boundaries, `read_process` no longer drains another chat's process before refusing it, a reference to a lone surrogate reaches the model as U+FFFD. A chat with tool hooks still sent the process tools to the host (the daemon handed every native tool there while hooks ran in TS); fixed by the hooks step below.
    - Measured with `scripts/bench-chat-turn.mjs --fake --tools` (new: per run, a `fetch_url` of a loopback page and a `start_process`, both approved), 5 runs, main's nekkod (b5e14033) against this one, `tool:approve` to `tool_result`: idle `fetch_url` 3.7 / 6.0 ms and `start_process` 11.0 / 8.7 ms; with 4 workspace-index searches keeping the host busy `fetch_url` 897 / 5.5 ms and `start_process` 294 / 8.9 ms.
  - Settings in the daemon, done 2026-10-12: nekkod is settings.json's single writer whenever it knows the data directory. New crate `crates/nekko-settings` ports `store.ts` (`getSettings`: the defaults with the file spread over them, array fields normalized, `maxSteps` dropped, a legacy accent migrated, and the defaults whenever `getSettings` would throw; `saveSettings` with `uniqueFolders`/`folderPathKey`; `resetSettings`; `writeJsonAtomic`'s temp file, fsync and rename) down to JavaScript's object spread, property order (integer-like keys first) and `JSON.stringify(v, null, 2)` (`nekko_js::stringify_pretty`, `js_order`, `spread_into`). The daemon serves `settings:get/update/reset`; the TS host keeps its cached copy, applies its own writes to it synchronously and sends them in order (`settings:save`, `settings:resetHost`), never touching the file, once `NEKKOD_OWNS=settings` is in its environment; the daemon tells it about the UI's writes (`settings:changed` with the settings and the patch) so its copy follows and it still stops voice and re-reads messaging as `updateSettings` did. Daemon services read through `crate::settings::current` and write through `crate::settings::update`; `SettingsStore::subscribe` reports every save. Without a daemon (cloud, `NEKKO_DAEMON=ts`) the TS store writes as before. The CLI's `migrate` (run with the app closed) still merges profiles on disk. Parity: `packages/host/src/settings.golden.test.ts` writes the embedded defaults and the results and file bytes of 31 cases (missing, invalid, BOM, null/number/array/string files, duplicate keys, escapes, odd numbers, legacy and non-string accents, folder dedupe across drive, UNC and POSIX paths, every `saveSettings` throw), failing when stale, which `crates/nekko-settings/tests/golden.rs` matches as text. A real-binary test covers ownership, the merge, the host notifications and refusals. Verified live in the web edition behind nekkod with an isolated data dir: the host's startup engine entry and a `providers:save` landed through the daemon beside a UI `settings:update`, and the host followed a UI `settings:reset`. Idle, through the public server: `settings:get` p50 1.25 ms (a host-served `providers:list` 1.39 ms), `settings:update` 3.47 ms.
  - Nekko Voice in the daemon, done 2026-10-12: `crates/nekkod/src/voice.rs` serves `voice:status`, `voice:install`, `voice:cancel`, `voice:uninstall`, `voice:transcribe` and the host's `voice:stop` when the daemon knows the data directory, as a port of `engine/voice.ts`: the pinned whisper.cpp v1.9.2 archive and Tiny.en Q5_1 model downloaded with the same 150 MB cap, SHA-256 checks, `.partial` rename and progress split (0.45 each), extracted with `tar`, the GGML magic check, the notices and `install.json` (the daemon is now their only writer); the speech server started on a free loopback port on the first transcription (same arguments and thread clamp, `/health` polled 150 x 200 ms), restarted when the runtime, model or thread count changes, the recording sent as the same multipart form, and stopped after `idleSeconds` (10 s to 10 min) or on daemon exit. The host stops it through the daemon when the voice settings change or the engine runtime stops. The TS service stays for the no-daemon backend. Tests: install from a loopback release host (archive, model, record, notices, archive removed), a bad checksum and a GGUF rejected, a stalled download cancelled (and uninstall refused meanwhile), the WAV checks with the host's messages, and a real-binary test where the daemon starts a stand-in `whisper-server` (`examples/fake_whisper.rs`, whisper-server's arguments) and returns its trimmed text with every multipart field the host sends.
    - Verified live: the real Windows runtime archive installed from GitHub through the daemon (checksum and extraction). Not verified live: a real transcription, since Hugging Face (the model's host) does not resolve from this machine; the stand-in server covers that path.
  - Tool hooks in the daemon, done 2026-10-12 (the seventh callback): a daemon that lists `turn:hooks` in `daemon:info` gets the turn's hook rules in its spec (`facts.hookRules`, with `facts.hooks` false) and runs PreToolUse and PostToolUse hooks around every call itself (`nekko-tools::hooks`, a port of `matchingHooks`, `hasToolHooks`, `runHook`, `decisionOf`, `label`, `preToolHooks` and `postToolHooks`: the matcher on `jsre` with `i`, the list fallback for a matcher that does not compile, the payload as the same JSON text, the environment, the clamped time limit, notes and blocks worded the same). So the native tools stay native while hooks are on (before, any tool hook sent every tool, `read_file` included, back through `turn:tool`), and neither `turn:hook` nor `turn:tool` is called. Held to the TS by `packages/host/src/hooks.golden.test.ts` (12 rules over 11 event and tool pairs, 13 hook outcomes, 5 labels; written by the real functions, failing when stale) and `crates/nekko-tools/tests/hooks_golden.rs`, unit tests that run real hooks (a block by exit code, a PostToolUse note, a timeout that never blocks), and `a_daemon_turn_runs_its_tool_hooks_itself` end to end. The rules are re-read per call from the daemon's own settings store (settings moved there in #449), as the TS host re-read them; the spec's copy serves only a daemon without a data directory. Differences: a timed-out hook's whole tree is killed, and `TurnEnd` hooks still run in the host after the turn.
    - Measured with `scripts/bench-chat-turn.mjs --fake --hooks` (new: a PreToolUse and a PostToolUse `echo` hook on `write_file`), 5 runs, main's nekkod against this one, `tool:approve` to the `write_file` result, both hooks included: idle 23.1 / 23.0 ms (two shell starts either way), with 4 workspace-index searches keeping the host busy 1378 / 21.9 ms.

The catalog download still only explains that no correct export is hosted (the TS `LAYA_ONNX` is null); a hosted export will need the engine's download manager, which moves later. Parity: `packages/host/src/engine/decisions.golden.test.ts` runs two scripts (35 local steps over a 25-file tree, 9 TypeSafe steps over scripted replies including 401, 429 with a string error, 400 with an object error and an HTML 502) through the real TS service with a scripted decide service and fetch, recording each answer, every decide call and settings save, and every TypeSafe request; `crates/nekkod/src/decisions_tests.rs` replays them and matches all of it as text. JavaScript's `toFixed` rounds exact ties up where Rust's formatting ties to even, so the load message uses a port (`to_fixed`).
  - Sign-in and tokens.json in the daemon, done 2026-10-12: nekkod is tokens.json's single writer when it knows the data directory. New crate `crates/nekko-oauth` ports the pure half of `oauth.ts` (authorize URLs with `URLSearchParams` encoding, `parsePasted` over WHATWG URLs, the code and refresh requests, what their responses become including every error message, `buildStatus`, the Claude and Codex CLI imports with an ECMAScript `Date.parse`, JWT claims read the way `Buffer.from(..., 'base64url')` reads them, the token store), keeping the TS object literals' key order with `undefined` left out. `crates/nekkod/src/oauth.rs` is the stateful half: pending sessions with their 10-minute expiry, the loopback listeners (8765-8795 for Claude, 1455 for ChatGPT with an earlier unfinished ChatGPT sign-in's listener reclaimed, any port for OpenRouter; bound on 127.0.0.1 and ::1), one renewal at a time per token. It serves `oauth:begin/finish/cancel/status/signout` and `providers:importCliAuth`, publishes `oauth:status`, and answers the host's `oauth:fresh` (the host's `ensureFreshToken` under `NEKKOD_OWNS=tokens`; its own writes to tokens.json then throw). The daemon's limit polls renew through it instead of `oauth:refresh`. The web edition passes `signIn: false` and gets the refusal its backend gave. To make the golden set possible, `oauth.ts` split its request building and response handling out of `exchangeCode`/`refreshTokenSet`/`importCliAuth` (`exchangeRequest`, `exchangeResult`, `refreshRequest`, `refreshResult`, `claudeCliToken`, `codexCliToken`); behavior unchanged. Parity: `packages/host/src/oauth.golden.test.ts` writes 58 cases from the real functions (fails when stale), `crates/nekko-oauth/tests/golden.rs` matches them as text. Unit tests drive a whole loopback sign-in against a local token server and two concurrent renewals that make one request; a real-binary test covers status, `oauth:fresh` and sign-out with nothing reaching the host. Not verified live against Anthropic, OpenAI or OpenRouter (no account may be used from here).

The list is written through the daemon's settings (the host hears of it), a removed signed-in provider's limits are cleared, discovery probes the well-known local servers IPv4 first with the same 1.5 s limit, a token-store provider is resolved with a fresh token from the daemon's OAuth service (`resolve`, `resolve_provider`, `list_models` for other daemon modules such as `delegation_targets`), and `lms:probe` runs `lms version` without a console window, remembered 15 s.   - Chat admission in the daemon, done 2026-10-12: `chat:send` is nekkod's when it owns settings, tokens and sessions (`crates/nekkod/src/admission.rs`, a port of `admitTurn` and `buildDaemonTurn`): the provider and chat checks, a worktree chat's checkouts and setup command (output to the agent terminal through `loop:log`), the offline and endpoint checks, the decision runner (the daemon's resident model, else a TypeSafe key), the provider resolved with a fresh token in the daemon, and the `chat:turn` spec; it answers once the chain of queued turns is done (`Turns::watch`/`take_chained`), as `sendChat` did. A compacting chat is refused from the host's own `compaction` events. The host is handed the turn without waiting (`turn:adopt`; its callbacks wait up to 5 s for a `turn_d` turn it has not registered yet) and keeps what follows a turn and the tools that still run there. Connector and index snippets are asked of the host only when a connector is connected or the prompt has words to search for, until those services move (`crate::connectors::snippets`, #462, and `crate::workspace::index_snippets`, rust-workspace). Sandbox chats and `NEKKO_AGENT_LOOP=ts` go to the host whole. The desktop app (and the bench) start nekkod with `allowBrowserControl: true`, as the desktop host has it. Also fixed: `effective_effort` in `nekko-agent` lacked `effectiveEffort`'s OpenAI-reasoning branch, so a gpt-5 or o-series turn at `medium` went out with no rung. Verified: a real-binary test (refusal, the chain, adoption, snippets and guidelines reaching the model) and `scripts/bench-chat-turn.mjs --fake --dump-requests` through the desktop backend, where daemon admission and the TS route send byte-identical model requests (11 of 11, ids and temp dirs aside). Measured with `--fake --runs 5 --load 4` (4 concurrent index searches on the host): first text median 931 ms with main's nekkod (host admission) against 543 ms (526, 301, 598, 774, 543); idle 10 ms on both. What is left of that wait is the index-snippet call to the busy host and goes with the index port; the queue drain is unchanged (the host still admits a queued prompt, `turn:prepare`).
  - Queued prompts in the daemon, done 2026-10-12: a chain the daemon admitted (`turn_d` turns) has its queued prompts admitted by the daemon too (`admission::admit_queued`, the same `admit` as `chat:send` with the queued item; `Turns::set_preparer`), so `turn:prepare` reaches the host only for a sandbox chat or `NEKKO_AGENT_LOOP=ts`, and for chains the host admitted. The host is handed the queued turn as `turn:adopt` with `chained: true`, which joins the chain it already follows (same listener, same Stop) instead of starting a new one; the previous turn stays registered there until that adoption lands, so nothing waits on it. Verified: a real-binary test (two turns, no `turn:prepare`, the second adopted as chained and named in the first's `next`) and a host test of a late chained adoption. Measured with `scripts/bench-chat-turn.mjs --fake --runs 5` from a reply's `done` to the queued follow-up's first text: idle 15 to 19 ms before and after; with 4 index searches keeping the host busy 1783, 1797, 1823, 2157, 2344 ms before against 1398, 1124, 952, 1081, 1197 ms. What is left is the index-snippet call to the busy host, which goes with the index port (rust-workspace).
