  - Automation tasks in the daemon, done 2026-10-12: new crate `crates/nekko-automation` (`tasks.rs`) ports `tasks.ts`: `tasks.json` (same layout and key order, written whole), create, update, delete, run now, the fire (the task's chat made by the host through `session:create` and named with the new `SessionStore::assign`, the turn through the host's `chat:send`, the answer read back from the chat), the patch a finished fire writes, and `tasks:updated` with the list as saved. `crates/nekkod/src/automation.rs` owns `tasks:list`, `task:create/update/delete/runNow` when the data directory is known; the backend is told so through `NEKKOD_OWNS=tasks` and then neither runs its scheduler (`unlessDaemonOwns`) nor writes the file: its own task methods, which the local API server and the CLI call, go to the daemon (`viaDaemon`, new `packages/host/src/daemon-services.ts`). The scheduler sleeps until the earliest due task (at most 15 s) and is woken by every change, so a task fires when due: measured 3 ms after its `runAt` in the real-binary test, where the TS tick fired up to 15 s late.
    - Found and fixed on the way (both sides): a fire clears `nextRunAt` when it starts and sets it again when it ends, so a task whose fire was cut short by a quit or crash stayed active with no next run and never fired again. At startup every active task without a next run is now due (`reconcileTasks` in the TS, `Tasks::reconcile`).
    - Parity: `packages/host/src/tasks.golden.test.ts` runs 32 operations through the real TS service (mocked chats, turns and settings, fake time and ids: creates with trimmed and empty titles, defaults and no model, run now on every kind, done tokens and "condition met", a failing turn, a deleted chat, resuming, extra fields, deletes with empty and used chats, a scheduler tick, a corrupt file, the restart fix) and writes `tasks.json`, the replies, the change notices, the chats made and the turns sent after each, plus 4 prompts and 49 finished-fire patches; `crates/nekko-automation/tests/tasks.rs` matches all of it as text (a tick's interleaved middle notices by count and the last one).
    - Verified: real-binary tests for a create that fires at once through `chat:send` and re-arms, pause and delete, a task cut short by a restart firing again, and a scheduled task firing on time. Not run against a real model (a fire is a normal chat turn, already covered by the turn tests).

The TS implementations stay for `NEKKO_AGENT_LOOP=ts` and a host without a daemon until the host is retired; Stagehand goes with them.
  - Machine stats in the daemon, done 2026-10-12 (the first of the engine-area services): new crate `crates/nekko-machine` serves `gpu:stats`, `system:stats` and `machine:readiness` (`crates/nekkod/src/machine.rs`), so the monitor meters and the readiness advisor no longer wait on the host's event loop. Ports of the probes' parsers (`nvidia-smi` CSV, macOS `ioreg` accelerator counters, the engine's `llama-server --list-devices` fallback with its integrated-GPU filter, `/proc/cpuinfo` and `sysctl` features, the `nvidia-smi -L` / `vulkaninfo` backends, `vm_stat` memory) and of core's `evaluateReadiness` over the catalog the TS exports (`src/catalog.json`), with the host's caches (GPU 1 s, the llama-server fallback 15 s, the CPU baseline primed over 200 ms after 30 s idle). The fallback finds `llama-server` as `installedBin` does (`engineBinPath`, then `<data>/engine/engine.json`, then PATH). Held to the TS by `packages/host/src/machine.golden.test.ts` (8 `nvidia-smi` outputs, 5 `ioreg` dumps, 8 device lists, 4 cpuinfo, 2 sysctl, 5 backend pairs, 4 `vm_stat`, and 54 readiness reports over 9 machines and 6 languages, which also writes the catalog and fails when either is stale), matched as text by `crates/nekko-machine/tests/golden.rs`. `resource:queue` moved with them (`crates/nekkod/src/resource_queue.rs`): the daemon is the only writer of `resource-queue.json` when it knows the data directory. The TS probes stay for the engine's build choice and the no-daemon backend; the settings keys these read (`engineBinPath`, `experimental.resourceQueue`) come straight from `settings.json` until the daemon's settings store lands.
  - Machine stats in the daemon, done 2026-10-12 (the first of the engine-area services): new crate `crates/nekko-machine` serves `gpu:stats`, `system:stats` and `machine:readiness` (`crates/nekkod/src/machine.rs`), so the monitor meters and the readiness advisor no longer wait on the host's event loop. Ports of the probes' parsers (`nvidia-smi` CSV, macOS `ioreg` accelerator counters, the engine's `llama-server --list-devices` fallback with its integrated-GPU filter, `/proc/cpuinfo` and `sysctl` features, the `nvidia-smi -L` / `vulkaninfo` backends, `vm_stat` memory) and of core's `evaluateReadiness` over the catalog the TS exports (`src/catalog.json`), with the host's caches (GPU 1 s, the llama-server fallback 15 s, the CPU baseline primed over 200 ms after 30 s idle). The fallback finds `llama-server` as `installedBin` does (`engineBinPath`, then `<data>/engine/engine.json`, then PATH). Held to the TS by `packages/host/src/machine.golden.test.ts` (8 `nvidia-smi` outputs, 5 `ioreg` dumps, 8 device lists, 4 cpuinfo, 2 sysctl, 5 backend pairs, 4 `vm_stat`, and 54 readiness reports over 9 machines and 6 languages, which also writes the catalog and fails when either is stale), matched as text by `crates/nekko-machine/tests/golden.rs`. `resource:queue` moved with them (`crates/nekkod/src/resource_queue.rs`): the daemon is the only writer of `resource-queue.json` when it knows the data directory. The TS probes stay for the engine's build choice and the no-daemon backend; the settings keys these read (`engineBinPath`, `experimental.resourceQueue`) come from the daemon's settings store.
    - Verified live (RTX 5090, Windows): the daemon's `gpu:stats` matches `nvidia-smi`, and `system:stats` matches Node's `os` (model, 32 cores, 48270 MB total).
    - Measured with `scripts/bench-chat-turn.mjs --fake --stats 10` (new: 10 calls per channel, 1.1 s apart so each GPU read is fresh), an earlier nekkod (the TS host answers) against this one, idle and with `--load 4`: `gpu:stats` median 55 / 55 ms idle and 732 / 55 ms busy (p95 981 / 117); `system:stats` 2.7 / 1.6 ms idle and 249 / 1.8 ms busy; `machine:readiness` 442 / 391 ms idle and 1103 / 423 ms busy (p95 1320 / 869; its time is the `nvidia-smi` and `vulkaninfo` spawns).
  - Agent watches in the daemon, done 2026-10-12 (the ninth callback, and the first host service to move whole with it): `crates/nekkod/src/watches.rs` ports `AgentWatchService` (the tool's create, list and cancel with every check and message, `nextWakeAt`, and the 15-second scheduler with its PR polling, deadlines, pending deliveries kept across failures and restarts, backoff and error state). The daemon is the single writer of `agent-watches.json` (`NEKKOD_OWNS` now lists `agent-watches`): a daemon-driven turn arms watches itself (with the incognito, goal-run and offline refusal and the ask-mode approval of `runHostTool`), `session:nextWatch` is served here, the host no longer starts its scheduler, and a turn the host runs itself (`NEKKO_AGENT_LOOP=ts`) sends its `agent_watch` calls here (`watch:tool`). Two internal callbacks remain until their owners move: `watch:snapshot` (a PR's state through pr.ts; rust-workspace's `workspace::pr_info` replaces it) and `watch:resume` (continuing the chat through the host's admission). The ten TS unit tests are ported one for one onto an injectable clock and host (`watches::tests`), plus validation and list-format tests, a host test for the forwarding and callbacks, and `a_daemon_turn_arms_agent_watches_itself_and_owns_their_file` end to end. `nekko_usage::date::iso_string` is `toISOString`.
  - Sandbox containers in the daemon, done 2026-10-12: new crate `crates/nekko-sandbox` ports `container-sandbox.ts` (the digest-pinned image check, the plain-file copy with its link, hard-link, reparse and size checks, the in-container Python helper verbatim, the isolation verification of container and volume before every operation, the label-proved cleanup that survives restarts and failed creations, apply-back refused) over a `Runner`, and `crates/nekkod/src/sandbox.rs` ports the router (`execution-router.ts`): `sandbox:configure`, `sandbox:cleanup`, `sandbox:status`, `sandbox:diff` and `sandbox:applyDiff` are served by the daemon, which becomes the single owner of the containers, manifests and runtime state (`NEKKOD_OWNS` lists `sandbox`). The chat's side of setup is `nekko-store`'s `configure_sandbox`, the port of the new `applySandboxSetup`, held to it by the session-writes golden set (a new `s_sandbox` fixture: workspace ids deduplicated, ask mode, worktrees dropped). A sandbox reply still runs in the TS host, which asks before each call as before and sends it here (`sandbox:tool`); its Stop arrives as `sandbox:abort`, which kills the container as the TS runner did. The ten TS container tests are ported one for one onto a scripted Docker (`nekko-sandbox` tests), plus the real lifecycle, and `a_sandbox_chat_runs_its_tools_in_a_container_the_daemon_owns` runs setup, tools, a refused tool, the diff, a Stop during `sleep 30` (answered within moments, the container stopped) and cleanup against a real Docker engine (both opt-in with `NEKKO_SANDBOX_TEST_IMAGE`; verified locally with `node:22@sha256:363e...`, nothing left behind). Still to do: the sandbox reply itself in the daemon (`chat:turn` refuses sandbox chats), after admission moves.
  - The runtime control plane in the daemon, done 2026-10-12: `crates/nekko-engine` gained `capacity` (ports of core's `kvCacheBytes`, `estimateOverheadBytes`, `headDimOf`, `computeFit`/`planFit` with `suggestFixes`, and `autoFit`, down to JavaScript's `NaN` through `Math.max`, `Math.round` and `toFixed`) and `runtimes` (the Ollama, LM Studio and vLLM adapters, the supervisor for the servers we start, `runtime-calibration.json`, and the control plane joining them to the daemon's own GPU and system meters). nekkod serves `runtime:status/start/stop/load/facts/plan/autoFit`, `model:pull/load/unload`, `lms:probe` and `server:stop` (`crates/nekkod/src/runtimes.rs`) with the providers from its settings store; it is the only writer of the calibration file and kills the servers it started when it exits. The engine's own adapter still asks the TS engine (new host callback `engine:runtime`: `detectEngine`, `isRunning`, `status`, `models`, `resident`, `start`, `stop`, `load`, `unload`) until the engine moves. Parity: `capacity.golden.test.ts` (220 plans over 11 models, 5 machines and 5 requests, 220 auto-fits, KV and overhead tables) and `runtimes.golden.test.ts` (45 control-plane steps over scripted Ollama, local and remote LM Studio, vLLM, the engine and a cloud provider, recording the answers, every HTTP request, CLI run and engine call, and the calibration file the loads leave), matched as text by `crates/nekko-engine/tests/capacity.rs` and `tests/runtimes.rs`; plus the Models page's calls (Ollama load/evict, `lms` load/unload with ANSI and exit-0 failures, the cached probe, the port lookup and kill) and a real-binary test against a loopback Ollama. Found on the way: a stray quote in this machine's `PATH` made Rust's program lookup find nothing after it (Ollama included) where Node's `execFile` finds it; spawns now resolve bare names the way libuv does (`resolve_program`). `server:stop`'s "already stopped" message lost its em dash on both sides.
    - Verified live (read-only, isolated data dir): the daemon found this machine's Ollama CLI (0.23.0, server not running) and LM Studio's `lms`, as the host does; nothing was started or stopped.
  - The `loop:run` route removed, done 2026-10-12: since every turn the host hands over goes through `chat:turn`, the older halfway route (the daemon driving only the reply while the host kept the turn, `crates/nekkod/src/loops.rs` and `packages/host/src/daemon-loop.ts`) was reached only when `chat:turn` refused a turn. It is gone with its callbacks (`loop:tool`, `loop:event`, `loop:end`, `loop:approve`), its channels (`loop:run`, `loop:abort`, `loop:alive`, `loop:steer`) and capabilities (`loop:unbounded`, `loop:prompt-caching`); a turn the daemon refuses now runs in the host's own loop, as with `NEKKO_AGENT_LOOP=ts`. `loop:log` stays (the agent terminal for daemon turns), with the capability check in the new `daemon-caps.ts`; pending changes now hang off the daemon context directly. The two daemon tests that drove `loop:run` (the large-body limit and a 2 MB image reply with its follow-up) now drive `chat:turn`.
  - Side calls in the daemon, done 2026-10-01 (part of 4.): the chat title, suggested replies and prompt fill run on the Rust providers through `provider:complete` (`crates/nekkod/src/sideband.rs`); the host still writes the prompt and resolves the provider, and `packages/host/src/sideband.ts` falls back to the in-process provider without a daemon or with `NEKKO_AGENT_LOOP=ts`. Verified live on Gemma 4 12B on both routes: the same kind of title, four suggestions and a filled Role part (suggestions 740 vs 681 ms, fill 268 vs 280 ms).
  - MCP servers in the daemon, done 2026-10-01: `crates/nekkod/src/mcp.rs` ports the host's MCP client (stdio JSON-RPC through `cmd.exe /d /s /c` on Windows as Node's `shell: true` does, streamable HTTP with JSON or SSE replies and the echoed `mcp-session-id`) and runs the servers, so they outlive a TS host restart. `mcp:sync` returns the tool specs and each server's state, which `packages/host/src/mcp.ts` keeps so its tool list and status stay synchronous; `mcp:call` runs a call. The host still decides when to sync, still asks for approval, and keeps Hypergate discovery (for a daemon-driven turn, all three moved on 2026-10-12, see "MCP inside the daemon turn"); without a daemon, or with `NEKKO_AGENT_LOOP=ts`, its own client runs as before. Stopping a server stops its process tree, and a server that exits fails its waiting calls at once rather than after 20 s. Verified live with a stdio server and Gemma 4 12B: the tool listed through `mcp:status`, an approved call answered from the server, disabling it stopped the process, and nothing was left after the daemon exited.
  - Built-in tools in the daemon, done 2026-10-01 (the tools half of 1.): a daemon-driven run executes `read_file`, `write_file`, `edit_file`, `list_dir`, `glob`, `grep` and `bash` itself with `nekko-tools`, from a `toolContext` the host puts in `loop:run` (the tools the chat offers, mode, sandbox mode, guardrails, workspaces, working folder). Approvals go back to the host's prompt through `loop:approve`, so pending input, the board and the phone relay are unchanged, and shell output reaches the chat's agent terminal through `loop:log`. Stop also stops a running command. Pending changes moved to the daemon (`changes:list`, `changes:accept`, `changes:acceptAll`, and `changes:record` for the host's own executor, which sub-agents and the TS route still use), so there is one list; the daemon tells the host through `changes:notify`, and the host tells the UI and the relay as before. A context the daemon cannot read (an unknown guardrail shape, say) runs every tool in the host. Verified live on Gemma 4 12B: `read_file`, an approved `bash` run in the daemon (it printed `%ELECTRON_RUN_AS_NODE%` unset, where the TS backend has it set), the command in the agent terminal, a `write_file` in the UI's pending changes and cleared by accept-all, and Stop. A first benchmark (a `grep` for one word over 3,000 files, tool call to tool result at the UI) found the Rust grep 2 to 3 times slower than Node's (237 ms against 100 ms), from the regex engine run line by line and two path lookups per file. Now: an ASCII pattern with no regex syntax, what models mostly send, is a case-insensitive substring search (exactly V8's `i` matching for such a pattern, held to the regex engine by a test on the characters that fold unusually), each file is opened once, and a regex runs over the file's UTF-16 encoded once. The same benchmark is now 84 ms against 98 ms idle and 120 ms against 98 ms with the host busy. A pattern that needs the regex engine and matches nothing is still slower in Rust (185 ms over the same tree); regress is not a JIT.
  - Also fixed: a race in the daemon's RPC socket test (the fake backend's single event could land before the test subscribed).
  - Providers, first half of "providers + agent loop", done 2026-10-01: new crate `crates/nekko-agent` ports `packages/core/src/providers` (openai-compat for openai/openrouter/lmstudio/vllm/llamacpp/openai-compat, anthropic with the sampling-shape retry ladder and learned shapes, ollama, chatgpt) behind a `Provider` trait (`list_models`, `chat` streaming `ProviderChunk`s over a channel, cancelled by an `AbortSignal` or by dropping the stream) and a `Transport` seam over reqwest's `chunk()` API. Held to exact parity by a golden set the TS providers write against a fake fetch (`packages/core/src/providers/providers.golden.test.ts`, fails if stale) and `crates/nekko-agent/tests/golden.rs`: 230 requests (10 provider configs x 23 request fixtures, headers in order and bodies byte-for-byte as `JSON.stringify` prints them), 55 recorded streams (chunks, errors, retry requests, `onHeaders`), 20 model lists. Live smoke against llama.cpp b11011 + Gemma 4 12B: `examples/llama_smoke.rs`. Not wired into nekkod or chat.ts yet; OAuth refresh, connection tests, Ollama pull/load and discovery stay in TS.
  - Tools, guardrails and pending changes, done 2026-10-01 (not wired in yet): new crate `crates/nekko-tools` ports `executeTool` for `read_file`, `write_file`, `edit_file`, `list_dir`, `glob`, `grep` and `bash`, `classifyCommand` with `DEFAULT_GUARDRAILS`, the chat-mode and sandbox policy, and `changes.ts`; `execute(call, ctx)` takes the workspaces, mode, sandbox, an async approver, the change tracker, the `agent_<session>` command log and a `Cancel`. Regexes run on `regress` over UTF-16 (V8's non-`u` semantics, with 40 case-folding differences found by an all-BMP comparison and closed), paths on a port of Node's `path.win32`/`path.posix`, errors in Node's wording, listings in libuv's order, `localeCompare` on ICU root collation. The shell is Node's (`%ComSpec% /d /s /c`, `/bin/sh -c`) with the same messages, and now kills the whole tree on timeout or cancel. Held to the TS code by five golden files `packages/host/src/agent-tools.golden.test.ts` writes (fails if stale) and `crates/nekko-tools/tests/golden.rs` replays: 241 classifications (default and a custom ruleset with lookaround, backreferences, Annex B syntax and a malformed rule), 1230 calls across every mode, sandbox and workspace layout (bash stubbed), 12 file-tool sequences (137 steps: CRLF, BOM, invalid UTF-8, binary, truncation, `$&` in edits, `String()` coercions) compared with the resulting trees byte for byte and the pending-changes records, 12 real shell runs with the command log (plus Rust-only tests of the timeout, `maxBuffer`, output cap, cancel and tree kill against messages recorded from Node), the specs, and 1268 path cases. `browser`, `spawn_agent`, `ask_user`, `update_plan`, the training reports and `decide` stay in TS.
  - Files, memory, comments and the workspace index in the daemon, done 2026-10-12: new crate `crates/nekko-workspace` serves `file:read`, `file:write`, `dir:list`, `workspace:index`, `workspace:indexStatus`, `workspace:search`, `workspace:files` and the chat's index snippets (`workspace:snippets`, which admission in chat.ts now asks for, and `crate::workspace::index_snippets` for a daemon-side admission), plus `memory:list/save/delete` and `comments:list`, `comment:add`, `comment:resolve` when the daemon knows the data directory. Ports of `files.ts` (1 MB cap, NUL-byte binary detection, image signatures and data URLs, Node's `extname`, `localeCompare` order), `memory.ts` with core's `serializeMemory`, `comments.ts`, `workspace.ts` and core's indexer (`IGNORED_DIRS`, `isIndexable`, `detectLanguage`, the outline regexes on `regress`). Single writer: the TS host no longer touches `memory/`, `comments.json` or the index under the daemon; its dispatcher sends these channels to the daemon when the daemon owns them (`DAEMON_SERVED` in dispatch.ts), so the local API server, the CLI's MCP tools and the phone relay read the daemon's index too, and a new folder is indexed there. Index progress reaches the UI from the daemon and the host's listeners through `index:progress` (relayOnly, dropped from the backend's copy). The editions without a daemon (cloud, the CLI's local mode) keep the TS functions. Differences, all deliberate: a memory id that would leave the folder is refused, comment and memory writes are atomic and serialized, a re-index reuses the outline of every file whose size and modification time are unchanged, and a search reads its files on up to 8 threads with a one-pass ASCII search (hits and their order unchanged). Parity: `packages/host/src/workspace.golden.test.ts` builds a fixture tree (binary, BOM, CRLF, invalid UTF-8 cut at the 1 MB boundary, emoji past the 200-unit cut, ignored dirs, collation names) and records 60 calls across all of these into `crates/nekko-workspace/tests/golden/expected.json`, which `tests/golden.rs` matches as text.
    - Measured with `scripts/bench-chat-turn.mjs --fake --runs 0 --workspace 10` (new mode; `--load` now keeps the host busy through `bench:hostLoad`, the same in-host search, when the daemon owns `workspace:search`), medians, main (origin/main b5e14033) against this branch (with settings in the daemon, #449), idle and with 4 host-side index searches over 2,500 files keeping the host busy:

      | | idle: main / daemon | host busy: main / daemon |
      | --- | --- | --- |
      | `file:read` | 1.3 / 0.6 ms | 264 / 0.7 ms |
      | `dir:list` (400 entries) | 2.4 / 1.6 ms | 263 / 1.7 ms |
      | `workspace:files` (400 files) | 2.2 / 1.4 ms | 262 / 1.5 ms |
      | `workspace:indexStatus` | 0.9 / 0.5 ms | 261 / 0.5 ms |
      | `workspace:search`, 400 files | 4.4 / 2.6 ms | 269 / 2.9 ms |
      | `workspace:search`, 2,500 files, no match | 69.6 / 28.6 ms | 325 / 33.4 ms |
      | `workspace:index`, 2,500 files (re-index) | 106 / 48.8 ms | 377 / 58.7 ms |

      The first Rust search was 121 ms idle on 2,500 files (line-by-line lower-casing); the one-pass ASCII search and the parallel reads brought it under 30 ms. Before settings moved, search and index still waited on the busy host for `settings:get` (445 and 478 ms); they now read the daemon's settings store.
  - Git status, pull requests and the design board in the daemon, done 2026-10-12: `workspace:gitStatus`, `pr:sessionList`, `pr:diff`, `pr:action` and `design:get/addPage/updatePage/removePage/addNote/resolveNote/generate` are `crates/nekko-workspace`'s (`git.rs`, `pr.rs`, `design.rs`, with `proc.rs` for `execFile`'s no-window, timeout and output-cap rules). The git read keeps the TS limits: one `git status --porcelain=v2 --branch` per read, cached five seconds and shared by concurrent callers, the branch's PR from `gh` held at most 3 s; PR reads are serialized, cached a minute and paused after a rate-limit answer, with the REST API (the GitHub connector's token, else `GH_TOKEN`/`GITHUB_TOKEN`) when `gh` is missing or cannot see the repository. `crate::workspace::pr_info` serves agent watches. `design.json` is the daemon's alone; a generation takes the default provider, signed in, from the host (`design:provider`, until sign-ins move) and calls it on the Rust providers (`provider:complete`'s code, 10-minute cap where the host had none). Differences, all deliberate: a missing working folder no longer marks git as not installed for the rest of the session, a force read that waited behind another read reuses it, and a generation re-reads the board after the model answers so an edit made meanwhile is kept. Parity: `git-pr.golden.test.ts` (porcelain and worktree parsing, both PR shapes, check rollups, diff splitting, which transcript lines attach a PR, rate-limit pauses) and `design.golden.test.ts` (33 board operations including imports, revisions and scripted generations, plus the prompt, cleaning, label and slug helpers) write golden sets `tests/git_pr.rs` and `tests/design.rs` match as text; `tests/live.rs` reads a real repository and drives the REST fallback against a fake API.
    - The saved folders too: `workspace:list`, `workspace:addByPath` and `workspace:remove` run in the daemon and write `settings.workspaces` through `crate::settings::update` (the settings store, #449), so the host no longer writes them. Adding reuses a folder already saved under any spelling (`folderPathKey`), names it by Node's `basename`, and indexes it in the daemon 50 ms later. Revoking asks the host which chats it is admitting or running (`chats:busy`) beside the daemon's own turns, refuses while one uses the folder, clears it from the chats that use it (`SessionStore::revoke_workspace`, the same edit `removeWorkspace` made), and drops the folder's index; the folder itself is never touched.
    - Measured with `--workspace 10` (now also a forced `workspace:gitStatus` on a folder that is not a repository, and `design:get`), medians, main / daemon: `design:get` 1.2 / 1.0 ms idle and 315 / 1.0 ms with the host busy; forced git status 27 / 35 ms idle (the spawn dominates) and 758 / 298 ms busy, p95 1455 / 498 ms (process start-up on a loaded machine is what remains).
  - Skills in the daemon, done 2026-10-12: `skills:installed`, `skills:external`, `skills:targets`, `skill:install`, `skill:uninstall`, `vaizer:catalog` and `vaizer:skillMd` are `crates/nekko-workspace/src/skills.rs` (ports of `skills.ts`, `external-skills.ts`, `vaizer.ts`, and `parseSkillMarkdown`, `skillToMarkdown`, `normalizeInstallTarget` from shared). The built-in catalog, the built-in skill names and the bundled Vaizer snapshot are embedded from the TS sources (`src/skills-data.json`, written by `packages/host/src/skills.golden.test.ts`, which fails when it goes stale). Single writer: `skills.json` and `vaizer.json` are the daemon's; the host reads installed skills without the self-heal write (`readInstalledSkills`, for workflow skill steps), and the update checks refresh the catalog through the daemon (`refreshVaizerCatalog`). Parity: the same golden test records 22 install, uninstall, listing and discovery calls over a scratch home and project (shadowed names, CRLF and BOM frontmatter, folded and literal block scalars, a skill folder with its own files), every catalog skill's SKILL.md, and 8 parses, which `tests/skills.rs` matches as text; `tests/live.rs` drives the Vaizer refresh, cache and the legacy SKILL.md path against a fake server. `integrations:*` went to rust-retire.
  - Spec files in the daemon, done 2026-10-12: `spec:path`, `spec:readDocs`, `spec:setMethodology`, `spec:toggleTask`, `spec:build` and `spec:buildDoc` are `crates/nekko-workspace/src/spec.rs` (port of `spec.ts` with `getMethodology` and `toggleTaskLine`; the methodologies embedded from the TS source as `src/spec-data.json`). A build takes the chat's provider, signed in, from the host (`provider:resolved`, until sign-ins move) and calls it on the Rust providers; the linked spec after any turn (chat.ts `afterTurnHooks`) is built there too. The methodology is saved through the session store (`SessionStore::set_spec_methodology`), so the host no longer writes the chat for it. Sandbox chats and unknown chats are refused as `assertHostExecution` refuses them. Parity: `spec.golden.test.ts` records 40 calls over fixture chats and projects (other workspaces, missing folders, the kiro methodology, toggles by number and by string, builds over a scripted model with the request it was sent) into `tests/golden/spec.json`, which `tests/spec.rs` matches as text.
  - Chat forks and the archive purge in the daemon, done 2026-10-13: `session:fork` and `sessions:purgeArchived` are nekko-store's (`fork`, `purge_archived_with` in `write.rs`) when nekkod knows the data directory. The fork writes the file `forkSession` writes, key order included (the settings defaults `createSession` reads are always overwritten from the source, so the fork needs no settings); the purge awaits the host's command-log deletion (`sessions:deleteAgentLog`) before removing each chat, as `sessions:clear` does, so a failure keeps the chat, and it skips a chat whose id would not name a file of its own (the TS builds a path from it). Parity: `packages/host/src/session-fork.golden.test.ts` writes `golden/forks.json` (9 sources: a cut in the middle, at the first message, at a missing id, an empty id, sandbox chats with and without a mode, `null` fields, a numeric title, no title) and `golden/purge.json` (12 `archivedAt` values around the 60-day edge, strings, booleans, a broken file and a `null` file) from the real TS functions; `crates/nekko-store/tests/golden.rs` matches them as text, and a real-binary test (`forks_chats_and_purges_expired_archives_itself`) covers both channels and the log callback.
  - MCP status in the daemon, done 2026-10-13: `mcp:status` is nekkod's when it has the settings store: it syncs the configured servers it already runs and reports them (`mcp::status`), where the host used to sync through the daemon and read its snapshot back. Parity: `packages/host/src/mcp-status.golden.test.ts` records the TS rows for 4 config and snapshot pairs (`crates/nekkod/tests/golden/mcp-status.json`: settings order, failed, disabled and unknown servers, `null` fields), matched as text; a real-binary test reports a live HTTP MCP server and a disabled one.
  - Suggested replies and prompt fills in the daemon, done 2026-10-13: `chat:suggest` and `chat:fillPrompt` are nekkod's when it admits turns (`crates/nekkod/src/side.rs`; not under `NEKKO_AGENT_LOOP=ts`): the host's provider, offline and endpoint checks, the provider resolved with a fresh token, the call on the Rust providers, usage written as the host's sideband wrote it, and `null` for any failure. The prompts and the reading of the answers moved out of `chat.ts` into pure functions in `turn-plan.ts` (`suggestPrompt`, `fillPrompt`, `cleanFill`), ported with `parseReplySuggestions` as `nekko_turn::side`; `side-prompts.golden.test.ts` records 9 suggest prompts, 4 fill prompts, 8 cleanings and 16 suggestion answers (JSON, prose around JSON, bullets, numbered lines, key lines, duplicates, caps in UTF-16), matched as text by `crates/nekko-turn/tests/side.rs`. A real-binary test runs both on a scripted model and the null cases without a model call.
  - Where the strangler stands, 2026-10-13. With a data directory and a backend (the desktop app and the web edition), nekkod serves 176 of the 219 UI channels the host dispatcher maps; these 43 still run in the TS backend:
    - Engine model management (21): `engine:status/settings/install/uninstall/installPreview/models/importModel/deleteModel/saveModelPreset/folders/foldersSave/downloadModel/downloadCompanions/imageCompanions/downloadImageCompanions/downloads/cancelDownload/dismissDownload/residentTtl/autoload/generateImage`. The engine's load policy (arguments, companions, drafts, VRAM fit, eviction, idle TTL) also answers the Rust router's `engine:routerLoad/routerModels/routerModel` and `engine:runtime` callbacks.
    - Training (8): `training:list/create/update/delete/start/pause/stop/hint`, plus the training reports and a goal run's plan that daemon turns still send back through `turn:tool`.
    - Turns that stay in the host: `chat:compact`, `chat:cancelCompaction`, `chat:generateImage`, `chat:interruptQueued`, sandbox chats (the container router), and every turn under `NEKKO_AGENT_LOOP=ts`.
    - Smaller services: `integrations:detect/install/snippet` (other agent tools' config files), `messaging:status` and the messaging bridges behind it, `pr:continueStack`, `pr:linkStack`, `data:wipe`, `app:info`, `updates:check`, `workspace:add` (a native picker; headless it only lists).
    - Callbacks nekkod still makes into the host: `turn:adopt`, `turn:event`, `turn:end` (the host's after-turn work: agent terminal logs, TurnEnd hooks, the linked spec, messaging), `turn:tool`, `turn:hook` and `turn:approved` (for a host that keeps those tools), `turn:prepare` and `spawn:prepare` (admitting a host-admitted chain's queued prompt, making a sub-agent's chat), `turn:usage`, `turn:notify`, `chat:connectorSnippets` and `chat:indexSnippets` (fallbacks), `chat:pending:host`, `chats:busy`, `sessions:deleteAgentLog` and `terminals:list:agent` (the agent command logs), `changes:notify`, `settings:changed`, `index:progress`, `limits:notify`, `oauth:refresh` (a refresh rotates the refresh token, so it keeps one writer), `design:provider`, `provider:resolved`, `mcp:hypergate:save`, `watch:snapshot`, `watch:resume`, `workflow:skillDefs`, `loop:log`.
  - Can the TS engine be removed? Not yet (assessed 2026-10-13). Besides the channels and callbacks above, these consumers run the TS host with no daemon at all: the CLI's local mode (`apps/cli/src/lib.ts` `localClient` calls `createHost` in process whenever no app is running and no `--url` is given; `nekko-agent mcp` uses the same client), Nekko Cloud (`apps/cloud/src/server.ts`: one in-process host per account through `withDataDir`; nekkod serves one data directory per process), the web edition's relay-agent mode (`RELAY_URL`), the no-daemon fallbacks (desktop `mode: 'backend'` when the binary is missing, `NEKKO_DAEMON=ts` and `auto` without a binary in the web edition and Docker), and Intel macOS, for which no nekkod build exists (ort-sys has no prebuilt ONNX Runtime). Every golden parity test also generates its expected output from the TS functions, so they are the oracle each port is held to. No TS implementation has been deleted for any ported channel, because these consumers still call it. Removal path, in order:
    1. Port the 43 channels and the callbacks above, engine model management and training first (the largest), then compaction and image turns, sandbox turns, integrations, messaging, PR stacks and the rest; each with the usual golden parity bar.
    2. Make nekkod mandatory: ship it for every supported platform (Intel macOS decided 2026-10-13: dropped, not built; the server and the CLI's local mode refuse to start there, see `unsupportedPlatformReason` in `packages/shared/src/platform-support.ts`), then delete the desktop `mode: 'backend'` path, `NEKKO_DAEMON=ts`, the `auto` fallback and `NEKKO_AGENT_LOOP=ts`.
    3. Move the CLI's local mode and `nekko-agent mcp` onto a spawned or running nekkod (the `httpClient` already speaks the wire), and the web edition's relay-agent mode onto nekkod's relay agent (`crates/nekko-remote`).
    4. Nekko Cloud runs on **one shared, multi-tenant nekkod** (Philip, 2026-10-13: start there; split out per-account daemons only if isolation or load demands it). That means nekkod must scope every session, setting, workspace, secret and event stream by account (a tenant id on the wire and in the data layout), enforce it on every channel, and keep one account's turns from starving another's; cloud auth, billing and the account store stay TS, since they are the service, not the engine.
    5. Freeze the golden sets as fixtures (they stop regenerating once the TS side is gone), then delete `packages/host`'s engine services, `apps/desktop/src/backend` and `apps/server/src/backend.ts`, and the backend supervision in `crates/nekkod/src/backend.rs`.
  - Step 1, engine model management, slice 1 (the model library and its folders), done 2026-10-13: `engine:models`, `engine:importModel`, `engine:deleteModel`, `engine:saveModelPreset`, `engine:folders`, `engine:foldersSave` and `engine:autoload` are nekkod's when it has the settings store (`crates/nekkod/src/library.rs` over `nekko_engine::library`, `gguf`, `folders`: ports of `library.ts`, `gguf.ts`, `ollama.ts`, `folders.ts`, `companions.ts`, `readMlxModel`, `modelModality`, `defaultContextTokens` and the facade's folder report, first-start seed and GPU-fit verdict). Single writer: `<models>/library.json` is the daemon's (`NEKKOD_OWNS=library`, written atomically); the TS engine reads the rows through the new internal `engine:library` and saves the presets its download follow-up makes through `engine:saveModelPreset` (`daemonLibrary` in `library.ts`), and its dispatcher sends the seven channels to the daemon (`DAEMON_SERVED`). The folder and autoload lists are engine settings, saved through the daemon's settings store, so the host hears them (`settings:changed`). Load state still lives in the TS model server: `engine:models` asks the host (`engine:runtime` `loadState`: resident ids, last load errors, the installed llama.cpp version for the calibration floor) and falls back to the daemon's own running children when the host does not answer; the runtime control plane's `llamacpp` facts now read the daemon's library directly. Differences, all deliberate: the first-start folder seed runs on the first library read instead of at backend start, a corrupt `library.json` (a `models` that is not an object) starts fresh, and a malformed Ollama manifest or an MLX `config.json` of `null` is skipped where the TS failed the whole listing. Parity: `packages/host/src/engine/library.golden.test.ts` writes `crates/nekko-engine/tests/golden/library.json` from the real TS functions (29 GGUF headers including truncated, oversized, unknown-type, nested-array, invalid-UTF-8 and a vocabulary past the first 4 MB read; 28 modality inputs; the other apps' folders for 3 platforms x 3 environments; folder ids; companion keys; 13 GPU-fit cases; and 26 library steps over a fixture tree with our folder, LM Studio, a Hugging Face cache, Ollama manifests and blobs, MLX folders, split models, projectors, fetched sidecars, broken and empty files, imports, deletes and presets, each with `library.json` after it), which `crates/nekko-engine/tests/library.rs` matches as text; a real-binary test (`serves_the_model_library_and_its_folders_itself`) covers the seven channels, the seed, the load-state callback and the settings writes. Verified end to end on Windows with the web edition behind a debug nekkod and the real TS backend (isolated data dir, home and port 4561): an LM Studio folder seeded, rows with GPU fit, a preset and an autoload saved, and `runtime:facts` for the engine read from the daemon's library. Remaining in the TS backend: 36 of the 219 UI channels (engine model management 14: `engine:status/settings/install/uninstall/installPreview/downloadModel/downloadCompanions/imageCompanions/downloadImageCompanions/downloads/cancelDownload/dismissDownload/residentTtl/generateImage`; training 8; the rest as listed above), and the engine callbacks `engine:routerLoad/routerModels/routerModel/runtime` (plus the new `loadState`).
  - Step 1, engine model management, slice 2 (model downloads), done 2026-10-13: `engine:downloadModel`, `engine:downloadCompanions`, `engine:imageCompanions`, `engine:downloadImageCompanions`, `engine:downloads`, `engine:cancelDownload` and `engine:dismissDownload` are nekkod's when it has the library and the catalog (`crates/nekkod/src/downloads.rs` over `nekko_engine::download`, a port of `download.ts`: `.part` files, `Range` resume, a 206/200 check, a GGUF check before the rename, cancel, dismiss, the 400 ms coalesced updates; and `nekko_engine::acquire`, ports of the facade's download plans, `sourceRepoFor`, the finished-download follow-up and `gpuFirstPreset`, and of `image-companions.ts`). The facade's planning moved out of `createEngine` into exported functions (`modelDownloadPlan`, `companionsPlan`, `imageCompanionsPlan`, `finishedModelJobs`, `downloadedModel`, `gpuFirstPreset`, `sourceRepoFor`), which the TS engine still uses without a daemon. The runtime installs (llama.cpp, stable-diffusion.cpp, MLX) stay in the TS engine's own queue until they move: under the daemon (`NEKKOD_OWNS=downloads`) the host sends its jobs to the new internal `engine:hostDownloads` instead of emitting `downloads:updated`, the daemon publishes the merged list, re-reads the host's queue on `engine:downloads` (`engine:runtime` `downloads`) and sends a cancel or dismiss of a host job back (`engine:runtime` `cancelDownload`/`dismissDownload`). A finished model turns on `autoStart` and gets its GPU-first preset in the daemon, as the host did. New config option `huggingFaceUrl` (where the catalog and downloads reach Hugging Face; tests point it at a loopback server). Differences, deliberate: a request that fails before any answer reads `fetch failed` and a body that breaks off reads `terminated` (Node's wording), and the daemon's index writes stay serialized behind the library lock. Parity: `packages/host/src/engine/downloads.golden.test.ts` writes `crates/nekko-engine/tests/golden/downloads.json` from the real TS functions (12 queue scripts over a scripted server: fresh, no length, resumed with 206, a range ignored with 200, 403, 404, a network failure, a stream error, a rejected and an accepted GGUF, a cancel midway with a duplicate start, dismiss and restart in place, each with the requests sent and the files left; 8 download plans; 8 repo guesses; 6 companion plans; 8 image models with their sets, beside-the-weights files, statuses and plans with and without a token; 5 follow-up calls; 4 landed-file matches; 9 GPU-first presets), which `crates/nekko-engine/tests/downloads.rs` matches as text; a real-binary test (`downloads_models_itself_and_merges_the_hosts_jobs`) downloads a model with its projector and tokenizer from a loopback Hugging Face, hears `downloads:updated`, sees `autoStart` turned on and the row listed as vision, cancels a stalled download keeping its `.part`, and merges and controls a host job. Remaining in the TS backend: 29 of the 219 UI channels (engine model management 7: `engine:status/settings/install/uninstall/installPreview/residentTtl/generateImage`), and the engine callbacks `engine:routerLoad/routerModels/routerModel/runtime` (now also `loadState`, `downloads`, `cancelDownload`, `dismissDownload`).
  - Step 1, engine model management, slice 3 (runtime installs), done 2026-10-13: `engine:install`, `engine:uninstall` and `engine:installPreview` are nekkod's, with the internal `engine:detect` and `engine:installedBin` the TS model server now reads its binaries through (`crates/nekkod/src/installs.rs` over `nekko_engine::builds`, `gpu_adapters`, `install` and `mlx`: ports of `builds.ts`, the build half of `diffusion.ts`, `runtime-releases.ts`, `gpu-adapters.ts`, `install.ts`, `vc-runtime.ts`'s copy step and the runtime half of `mlx.ts`). The daemon writes `<data>/engine` (`NEKKOD_OWNS=runtimes`); the install jobs run in the daemon's download queue (which gained the TS queue's `verify` and `after` hooks), so the host's own queue is now empty under the daemon. The model server is still the host's: an install stops it just before the old files are replaced and an uninstall refuses while it runs, both by asking the host (`engine:runtime` `isRunning`/`stop`), falling back to whether the daemon is serving the engine's port. The runtime control plane reads the engine's install state from the daemon directly. New config option `githubApiUrl` (tests point the installers at a loopback GitHub). Parity: `packages/host/src/engine/install.golden.test.ts` writes `crates/nekko-engine/tests/golden/install.json` from the real TS functions (every build table entry; 192 machines' build lists and stable-diffusion.cpp lists; 10 `nvidia-smi` readings; 180 CUDA-upgrade cases; asset and runtime-library matches over 19 llama.cpp and 9 stable-diffusion.cpp release names; 4 adapter lists; 20 installer scenarios through scripted GitHub answers, archive tools and PATH lookups: a CUDA install with its runtime and removal, an old card getting Vulkan, a broken CUDA install, an upgrade offer, a binary chosen by hand and one on PATH, no GitHub, no build, a release that is not the pin, missing builds and runtimes, checksum mismatches, no archive tool, an archive without the server, a runtime that is gone, untrusted records, and stable-diffusion.cpp on Linux and with CUDA on Windows, each with every request, command and stop it made and the files it left; 7 MLX scenarios), which `crates/nekko-engine/tests/install.rs` replays; a real-binary test (`installs_and_removes_the_engine_itself`) installs this machine's CPU build from a loopback GitHub, unpacks it with the system's tar, reads it back through `engine:detect` and `engine:installedBin`, and removes it. MLX is ported but unverified on a Mac (this machine is Windows; the golden covers it with `darwin`/`arm64` passed in). Remaining in the TS backend: 26 of the 219 UI channels (engine model management 4: `engine:status/settings/residentTtl/generateImage`), and the engine callbacks `engine:routerLoad/routerModels/routerModel/runtime`.
  - Step 1, engine model management, slice 4 (the model server's policy), done 2026-10-13: `engine:status`, `engine:settings`, `engine:residentTtl` and `engine:generateImage` are nekkod's, and so is the load policy the router used to ask the host for (`engine:routerLoad/routerModels/routerModel`, now answered in the daemon) and every `engine:runtime` call the runtime control plane made (`crates/nekkod/src/model_server.rs` over `nekko_engine::server` and `launch`: ports of `createEngineServer` as it runs under the daemon, `buildArgs`, `probeFlags`, `resolveCompanions` and its template extraction, `explainLoadError`, `diffusionArgs`, `mlxArgs`, `unsupportedLoadReason`, the Visual C++ runtime messages, and the facade's start, load-starts-the-engine, status, settings save and image generation, with `ensureEngineProvider`). What to load and with which arguments, drafts and companions, the before/after VRAM measurement, eviction to `maxLoaded`, the idle TTL sweep, load-on-demand, the residency sync and the autoload list now all run in the daemon over its own supervisor and router (`NEKKOD_OWNS=server`). The TS engine facade sends its server calls to the daemon (new internal `engine:start/stop/load/unload/resident/running/loadedIds`) and keeps a polled `isRunning` for the prompt-caching endpoint check; image turns still report their loading and generating stages from the host. The facade's pure helpers moved to exported functions (`imageGenerationBody`, `engineMemory`, `reservesEndpoint`) and the TS server exports `sweepIdle`, so the golden calls the real code. Also fixed, pre-existing: re-serving the engine on the same port (a new API key or CORS list while it runs) could race the old listener's shutdown and leave nothing listening; the router now waits up to 3 s for the port when it replaces its own listener. Parity: `packages/host/src/engine/server.golden.test.ts` writes `crates/nekko-engine/tests/golden/server.json` from the real TS functions (10 command lines over every flag and companion, 13 dead-server logs, 3 help texts, 10 unsupported-model reasons, DLL exits, 15 image requests, memory pools, endpoint patches, companions and template extraction for 9 models, 6 stable-diffusion.cpp argument sets, 3 MLX ones, and 6 server scenarios over a scripted daemon: start with an autoload list, reloads, eviction by idle age with ties, unloads, TTLs, reconfigure and stop; refusals, missing runtimes, a split model missing a shard, a draft of the wrong kind, a failed spawn and a missing Visual C++ runtime, all residents busy; the router's 404/400/409 answers and load-on-demand with a GPU measured, an idle sweep, a crashed child; a refused port and nothing installed; and a backend restarted under a serving daemon), which `crates/nekko-engine/tests/server.rs` replays op by op, with every serve, spawn (arguments included) and kill; a real-binary test (`runs_the_model_server_itself`) starts the engine with a stand-in `llama-server` (`examples/fake_llama_server.rs`), loads a model with the flags its `--help` lists, chats through the router, loads a second on demand evicting the first, changes the API key while serving, and stops. Verified end to end on Windows with the web edition behind a debug nekkod, the real TS backend and the stand-in server (isolated data dir, home and ports 4561/4562): the host's autostart reached the daemon, `runtime:load` loaded through the daemon's policy, the router answered a chat, `engine:residentTtl` and `engine:generateImage` answered, and `runtime:stop` stopped it, leaving no process. Engine model management now runs entirely in nekkod: 22 of the 219 UI channels remain in the TS backend (training 8, compaction and image turns, sandbox turns, integrations, messaging, PR stacks and the rest above). Engine callbacks left: `engine:runtime` `downloads`/`cancelDownload`/`dismissDownload` and the host's `engine:hostDownloads` push, which carry only the host's own download queue, now empty under the daemon (removable once the no-daemon editions go, step 2).

#### PF: decision models (added 2026-10-01)

Laya (Convai Innovations, Apache 2.0) runs natively in the daemon: typed questions in, calibrated probabilities out, in TypeSafe Jev's shape. Philip's call: native Rust with ONNX Runtime, no Python at run time. Design and numbers: [docs/decision-models.md](docs/decision-models.md), [spec](SPEC.md#models--providers).

- [x] **PF30**, A native Laya runtime in the engine daemon. · Done: 2026-10-01
  - `crates/nekko-decide`: `ort` 2.0.0-rc.13 with prebuilt ONNX Runtime 1.28 (DirectML on Windows, CoreML on macOS, CUDA on Linux behind a `cuda` feature, CPU always), `tokenizers` for `tokenizer.json`. Pre- and post-processing ported from `laya` 0.3.22 (prompt, option markers, head budget, truncation, Python `json.dumps` rendering, temperature clamping, confidence); a bad question is its own error entry. Accelerators get a load-time probe and fall back to CPU; DirectML and CoreML run one fixed shape (DirectML was 85 ms on any shape but its first, 8.6 ms pinned). Exports below opset 17 are refused, since the only hosted one (`tozp/laya-onnx`) answers wrongly and crashes the CPU provider. The workspace's serde_json now preserves key order, because criteria order is option order.
  - Verified against 60 golden requests (86 questions) from the official package: token sequences identical on all 86; fp16 on DirectML 86/86 same answers, max probability error 0.018; fp32 max 0.0001. RTX 5090: 8.6 ms per question on DirectML fp16, 81 ms for ten; CPU fp32 94 ms and 733 ms.
- [x] **PF31**, Decisions on the daemon wire and the engine port. · Done: 2026-10-01
  - Channels `decide:load`, `decide:unload`, `decide:status`, `decide:run`; `POST /v1/decisions` and `POST /v1/systemone` on the OpenAI-compatible router with its auth and CORS, 409 when no decision model is loaded. `stage-daemon.mjs` ships `DirectML.dll` (and any ONNX Runtime provider libraries) beside `nekkod`.
- [x] **PF34**, Decision models in the app: the Decisions tab, TypeSafe Jev, and a `decide` agent tool. · Done: 2026-10-01
  - Host `engine/decisions.ts`: catalog (Laya English; its download is off while no correct export is hosted, see PF32), export folders the user adds (`settings.decisionModelDirs`, validated for an ONNX file, `tokenizer.json` and `rl_agent_config.json`, external-data weights counted, removal only forgets the folder), load/unload/status through the daemon, request checks (state size, question count), and TypeSafe Jev (`POST https://api.typesafe.ai/v1/systemone`, `jev-latest`, key check via `/v1/models`). Channels `decisions:*`.
  - `decide` tool (`packages/core/src/agent/tools.ts`): offered only while Laya is loaded or a TypeSafe key is set; a flat question list (`id`, `type`, `instructions`, `options`) the host reshapes into named questions, accepting options as labels or `{label, description}` since models send both.
  - Verified live with the release daemon on the RTX 5090: the corrected export added as a folder, loaded fp16 on DirectML in 1.3 to 2.0 s, a 3-question ticket answered in about 30 ms through the backend (billing 0.98, refund 0.88), the router's `/v1/decisions` giving the same answer, and Gemma 4 12B calling `decide` on its own and answering from it in every checked run where the model server came up (7 of 7). Jev is covered by tests only (no key here).
  - Found on the way: llama-server b11011 occasionally died on its first request with a CUDA flash-attention error, shown as an HTTP 503. Cause and fix in T173.
- [ ] **PF32**, A hosted, correct ONNX export, and the other two checkpoints.
  - The English recipe that passes the golden test is in docs/decision-models.md; nobody hosts its output yet. `laya-multilingual` and `laya-typed-decisions` have no export at all.
- [ ] **PF33**, Verify CoreML on a Mac and CUDA on Linux, and decide whether release builds ship the Linux CUDA build.

### Live provider model catalogs and update checks (added 2026-10-02)

Philip's report: a ChatGPT subscription sign-in rejected its selected `gpt-5` (`The 'gpt-5' model is not supported when using Codex with a ChatGPT account`) and the picker's compiled-in list had aged out. Ask: read the account's live catalog so newly released models appear without an app release, and add update checks beside the version check with a Settings section holding a toggle per check (app version, provider model lists, skills catalog).

- [x] **T176**, ChatGPT provider reads `GET <base>/codex/models?client_version=<codex-cli pin>` with the OAuth token + `chatgpt-account-id` (the picker catalog, not metered `/v1/models`), normalized into `ModelInfo` with fuzzy catalog fallbacks for missing fields; failures, empty/malformed responses and api-key mode fall back to a curated list refreshed to the current generation. Custom-model allowlist merges and de-dupes. Both engines: TS `chatgpt.ts` and Rust `chatgpt.rs`, held identical by the golden layer (a stub network means the Rust golden now predicts zero requests for credential-less providers). Slug-anchored capability regexes (`gpt-5`) now accept the `gpt-N` family so future ids do not lose reasoning/thinking toggles. · [spec](SPEC.md#models--providers) · Done: 2026-10-02
- [x] **T177**, Per-check update infrastructure: `AppSettings.updateChecks` (appVersion/modelLists/skillsCatalog, defaults app+models on, skills opt-in; `autoUpdate` still seeds appVersion for old settings files), host `update-checks.ts` scheduled every six hours (staggered first run, pause/resume on settings change), a `modelsUpdated` event carrying `{ providerId, added }` (coalesced, emitted on first baseline only when real entries exist) that models.ts clears through `listProviders` cache-busts, and a `runUpdateChecks` manual RPC. The skills toggle refreshes the live Vaizer catalog (snapshot fallback unchanged) and re-emits `skillsCatalogUpdated`. Wired through dispatch, preload, web client, desktop ipc-server, server, cloud and relay. Settings → Updates gains the three toggles plus a catalog refresh button; the updates microcopy now names what each check does. · [spec](SPEC.md#distribution--platforms) · Done: 2026-10-02
- [x] **T178**, ChatGPT subscription chats stopped failing on `Unsupported parameter: temperature`. Philip's report: picking any ChatGPT-plan model ended the turn with `chatgpt 400`. The Codex backend accepts only the Codex CLI's request shape, so `temperature` and `max_output_tokens` (which the host attaches to every run) are rejected outright rather than ignored. Both providers now send neither: the effort setting goes out as `reasoning.effort` (low/medium/high; `normal` sends no rung so the model keeps its own default, rungs above the Codex ladder clamp to `high`), `think` still adds `reasoning.summary: auto`, and this provider carries no client-side output cap. Fixed in TS `chatgpt.ts` and Rust `chatgpt.rs` together, with the golden layer re-recorded so the two stay identical. · [spec](SPEC.md#chat--cowork-unified-surface) · Done: 2026-10-02
- [x] **T179**, The same `Unsupported parameter` failure on the OpenAI-compatible providers, fixed two ways. Philip hit the same 400 through the OpenAI API-key path: `openai-compat.ts` sent `temperature` (and `max_tokens`) unconditionally for every kind it fronts. Layer one, preventive: a model id that parses as OpenAI's reasoning generation (o-series, `gpt-` with a two-digit or 5+ number, `gpt-oss`, `codex`) sends `reasoning_effort` plus `max_completion_tokens` on the `openai` kind and `reasoning.effort` on `openrouter`, with `normal` again meaning the model's own default; local kinds and non-reasoning ids keep `temperature` and `max_tokens`. Layer two, learned: when a 400/422 still blames a sent field (`error.param`, bare `param`, a FastAPI `detail[].loc` entry, or the field's name near a rejection word in plain text), the field is dropped, or renamed when the message says "use X instead" (the `max_tokens` to `max_completion_tokens` migration hint), the request retries, and the adjustment is remembered per provider and model so the next chat opens on the learned shape, mirroring the Anthropic provider's SamplingMemory contract. Required fields (`model`, `messages`, `stream`) are never dropped, and a near-match only counts droppable fields so "for this model" cannot blame `model`. Both engines again, `openai-compat.ts` and `openai_compat.rs`, held identical by re-recorded goldens including six retry streams. · [spec](SPEC.md#chat--cowork-unified-surface) · Done: 2026-10-02
- [x] **T186**, Anthropic provider reads `GET <base>/v1/models?limit=1000` (#269), so new Claude models appear without the hand-edits Opus 5.5 (#202) and Sonnet 5.5 (#276) needed. Rows map to `ModelInfo`: `display_name`, else the id; context window from the API's `max_input_tokens`, else `claudeContextWindow`, else 200k (the API gives Haiku 5.5 1M where the table says 200k); rows without an id dropped; stable-sorted by family (Opus, Sonnet, Fable, Mythos, Haiku, then unknown ids) so a new Haiku doesn't top the picker. Network errors, non-OK status, empty or malformed bodies, a missing key and a 5s timeout (body included; opening a chat waits on this list) fall back to the shipped `CLAUDE_MODELS`. Same auth headers as chat; a subscription OAuth token is accepted (checked live 2026-10-09). Both engines: TS `anthropic.ts` and Rust `anthropic.rs`, held identical by six new golden model cases. · [spec](SPEC.md#models--providers) · Done: 2026-10-09
- [x] **T188**, Haiku 5.5 is no longer a 200k Haiku (#413). `claudeContextWindow` / `claude_context_window` give Haiku 5 onwards 1M (the API reports 1M for `claude-haiku-5-5`); Haiku 4.5 and older stay 200k. The rule is by version, like the rest of the table. `claudeMaxOutputTokens` / `claude_max_output_tokens` give Haiku 5 onwards 128k (`max_tokens` on its `GET /v1/models` row, read 2026-10-10). `usesNativeEffort` / `uses_native_effort` no longer exclude Haiku, so Haiku 5 onwards offers the five-rung effort ladder that `rejectsSampling` already sent it (the menu showed low/normal/high while the request carried `output_config.effort`); its `/v1/models` row lists effort low to max as supported. New golden request case `haiku-5-5-xhigh`. `claude-haiku-5-5` added to the shipped `CLAUDE_MODELS` in both engines, above Haiku 4.5; `models.json` re-recorded. · [spec](SPEC.md#models--providers) · Done: 2026-10-10

### Nekko Server, image runtime and unified storage (added 2026-09-30)

No real user-profile move without confirmation. · [spec](SPEC.md#running-a-model-with-nothing-else-installed) · Added: 2026-09-30 · Done: 2026-10-01
  - Image half done 2026-10-01 on the RTX 5090, with the real daemon, backend and sd.cpp `master-900-c92d73c` CUDA installed through the app: FLUX.2 klein 9B Q8_0 and SD3.5 Large Q8_0 (both borrowed read-only from LM Studio) generated 1024x1024 images through the Rust router's `/v1/images/generations` (55 s and 48 s, cold load included), and klein generated from the in-app panel (28.6 s cold, 4.4 s warm at 4 steps). Found and fixed on the way: GGUF diffusion weights hold only the transformer, so neither model could start without hand-found companions (T171); a channel call failed mid-download because Node closes idle keep-alive sockets after 5 s while the daemon pooled them for 90 s (backend now keeps them 120 s, the daemon retires them at 30 s).
  - Migration-dialog half done 2026-10-01: the dialog could never complete a move (T174); after the fix, a confirmed move of a real legacy macOS dev profile completed and the next launch opened without the dialog.
- [x] **T174**, The desktop migration dialog completes the move instead of reappearing on every launch. · Done: 2026-10-01
  - Cause: `preservePackagedProfile` points Chromium at `~/.nekko-agent/desktop` at module load, so by `whenReady` Chromium has written `Local State` and the single-instance lock (`SingletonLock`/`Cookie`/`Socket`, `lockfile` on Windows) there. `migrateUserData` saw a non-empty new desktop profile and refused ("already contains browser data"), the app quit, and the next launch asked again. Every desktop move with a legacy profile hit it, dev and packaged.
  - Fix: `migrateUserData` ignores Chromium's startup files in the new desktop profile and does not move the old copies; the running browser owns them, and the old ones stay in the old folder. Everything else (Local Storage, Cookies, caches) moves as before.
  - Caveat: on Windows the old cookies were encrypted with the key in the old `Local State`, so they will not decrypt under the new one. Local Storage is unaffected.
  - Verified: a new `user-data.test.ts` case seeding the new profile with those files fails on `main` and passes; the same move on an empty-file copy of a real legacy macOS dev profile fails on `main` and completes on the fix; a live `npm run local` move of that profile completed and the next launch skipped the dialog.
- [x] **T173**, Every llama.cpp load names its context, which stops a first-request CUDA crash. · Done: 2026-10-01
  - Cause: a load with no context size (every load on demand, and any model never configured) let llama.cpp take the trained context for each of its 4 default slots. For Gemma 4 12B that is 262k x 4, a KV cache for about a million tokens, and `--fit` (on by default) then filled the GPU to within 1 GiB. The first flash-attention kernel is loaded lazily, and with the GPU that full the load sometimes failed: `CUDA error: shared object initialization failed` in `fattn-mma-f16.cuh`, the model server exiting before it was ready. It reproduced with nothing else on the GPU (2 of 12 starts), so the Laya session on DirectML was not the cause.
  - Fix: `defaultContextTokens` (shared): the trained maximum capped at 64k when nothing asked for a size; an explicit size from the drawer or a preset is passed as before, and the unasked-for default does not count as a changed setting, so the same load is not a reload. Also cuts Gemma 4 12B's load from 10 to 25 s to 3.9 s.
  - Verified on the RTX 5090 with the engine's exact arguments: 12 of 12 starts and first requests clean at the default, against 2 of 12 failing before.
- [x] **T172**, Image generation moves into chat: a per-chat MultiModal | Image switch. · [spec](SPEC.md#running-a-model-with-nothing-else-installed) · Done: 2026-10-01
  - Shared: `Session.chatType` (`multimodal` | `image`; `mode` was already the tool policy) and per-chat `imageParams`; assistant `ChatMessage.generated` (model, size, steps, CFG, the seed actually used, ms); an `image_status` agent event (loading, generating) that the live-activity reducer and board read as the turn's status; `SessionSummary.imageCount`; turn excerpts keep picture-only replies; an `artist` agent role for image chats. Channels `chat:generateImage` and `session:images` (the board's thumbnails, fetched only for cards on screen, so summaries stay small).
  - Host `image-chat.ts`: one turn appends the prompt and the picture, picks a real seed for -1, emits the usual turn events, stops on the chat's Stop (the model server may finish; the image is dropped), refuses a second concurrent turn, keeps prompts queued mid-turn, and writes nothing for incognito chats. A chat model later reading that history sees each picture as a one-line note, never an empty turn.
  - Renderer: `ChatTypeToggle` at the head of the controls strip; `ImageModeControls` (image models only, size, steps, CFG, seed, the encoder download, a runtime-missing hint); `ImageLiveTurn` placeholder with stage and seconds; generated images in the transcript with their settings; the Command Center card, sidebar label and reply box for image chats. Nekko Server's image panel keeps only setup and gains Generate in chat.
  - Verified in the web edition on the RTX 5090: Generate in chat from Nekko Server, a first image from the Command Center reply box (14.6 s, model load included), a second from the composer (4.1 s warm), SD3.5 adopting 28 steps / CFG 4.5 when picked, and the switch swapping controls both ways.
- [x] **T171**, Image models fetch their own text encoders and VAE. · [spec](SPEC.md#running-a-model-with-nothing-else-installed) · Done: 2026-10-01
  - `engine/image-companions.ts`: companion sets for FLUX.2 klein 9B (FLUX.2 VAE + Qwen3-8B Q4_K_M, 5.4 GB), FLUX.2 klein 4B (same VAE + Qwen3-4B, 2.8 GB), SD3.5 Large/Medium (CLIP-L, CLIP-G, T5-XXL fp8, VAE, 6.7 GB) and FLUX.1 (CLIP-L, T5-XXL fp8, VAE), matched from the GGUF architecture plus name and path, with pinned byte sizes and the family's tuned steps and CFG (klein 4 steps / CFG 1). Files land once in `<models>/.companions/image` under non-colliding names, shared across a family. SD3.5's and FLUX.1's VAEs are gated on Hugging Face: fetched with the user's token when one is set, otherwise MIT TAESD decoders (`madebyollin/taesd3`, `taef1`, 10 MB) stand in, and the panel says so. FLUX.2 dev has no set (its encoder is a 24B Mistral).
  - `diffusionArgs` resolves each role from the model's image settings, then files beside the weights, then the shared set, and refuses to start a family missing a required role, naming it. New channels `engine:imageCompanions` / `engine:downloadImageCompanions`; the image panel shows each file with its size and one Download button, polls until they land, and adopts the family's sampling defaults.

Technical approach: keep one model router and add a runtime-specific binary/argument/health adapter for image children. Runtime tags are source-controlled and updated only by PR; upstream metadata supplies real byte counts. Extend the existing five-touch API contract for installation previews and generation. Resolve local user data centrally in the host package, retain explicit deployment overrides and per-account cloud isolation, and gate any legacy migration before host initialization.

Resident state gains started/last-used/expiry timestamps, per-model TTL changes apply without a reload, `autoload` loads marked models in order at engine start, and a per-model `lastLoadError` translated from the child's log stays on the row until a load succeeds. `engineDownloadCompanions` fetches mmproj/template sidecars into a Nekko-owned `.companions` dir so borrowed folders stay read-only, `resolveCompanions` covers split shards and JSON-embedded chat templates, and new IPC (`engineDownloadCompanions`, `engineSetResidentTtl`, `engineSetAutoload`) is wired through dispatch, preload, and the web client. · [spec](SPEC.md#models--providers) · Done: 2026-09-30

### Reply suggestions (added 2026-09-30)

After each reply, the model writes what the user is most likely to say next, shown as one-click chips and as composer ghost text.

- [x] **T163**, Model-written reply suggestions: a new `chat:suggest` channel (full five-touch chain) calls `suggestReplies` in the host, a bounded sideband request on the reply's own provider/model asking for 2-4 short follow-ups plus the single most likely next message. The renderer pins the result to the reply it was written for, renders the follow-ups as one-click send chips above the composer, and shows the likely next message as composer ghost text that Right Arrow accepts into the draft. Unattended sessions (task/training/sub-agent) and offline-remote combos are skipped, any failure yields no suggestions, and `parseReplySuggestions` tolerates fenced JSON, alternate field names, and bare-line output from small models. · [spec](SPEC.md#working-in-a-codebase) · Done: 2026-09-30

Compaction is cancellable while in progress; starting a new chat preserves the unmodified original transcript.

### Nekko Agent rebrand and next product wave (added 2026-09-07)

Product name: **Nekko Agent**. Mascot: **Nekko**. Primary: **nekkoagent.com**; backup: **nekkoagent.com**. Repo/folder target: **nekko-agent**; CLI: **nekko-agent**. See [positioning](SPEC.md#positioning-carry-into-website--readme), [pillars](SPEC.md#messaging-pillars), and [roadmap](SPEC.md#roadmap-beyond-one-machine). Do not sweep historical Shipped entries or past release assets.

- [x] **AN1: Initial marketing and product identity implementation.** Website, social image, outline favicon, metadata, README, visible desktop/mobile branding, installer artwork, and mascot names updated. Four pillars are distinct; runtime installation and automatic routing are not overstated; team/distributed/cloud runners are roadmap. T124/T135 activity/outline design preserved. Matching desktop/mobile website and light/dark Settings captures plus motion recordings saved in temporary storage. Website checked at six widths with ten download scenarios. Native mobile and packaged-upgrade verification remain AN5 gates, not claimed done. Implemented locally: 2026-09-07; not deployed.
- [x] **AN6a: Explicit frontier-to-local delegation.** `spawn_agent` now accepts optional `provider_id`/`model_id`; default inheritance remains unchanged. Explicit targets require enabled configured providers and verified exact chat-model IDs. Invalid/unavailable targets fail before creating a child and never silently fall back. Children inherit effective execution mode and disabled tools; Ask/ask-everything now approve both sub-agent starts and MCP calls before indirect execution. Incognito delegation is blocked rather than persisted. Host-side offline checks require a local-compatible loopback endpoint; unoffered tools cannot run, offline context previews skip connectors, and hidden spec refreshes are suppressed. Added 50 focused mocked-provider tests, including the context-preview failure reproduced before its fix. Implemented locally: 2026-09-07. See [experience and limits](SPEC.md#delegating-frontier-planning-to-a-local-worker).
- [ ] **AN6b: Explainable routing policy and wider privacy audit.** Add opt-in local-worker defaults, automatic task/capability/resource-aware selection, and visible routing reasons. Validate against live frontier/local providers and real CLI/MCP clients. Audit explicit spec generation and other independent provider actions for the same offline policy; configured loopback validation is not a network sandbox. Add cancellation propagation and a real concurrency/resource scheduler before promising parallel fleet execution. *Status 2026-09-30: partially served, not closed. T157/T160 gave routing real inputs (per-provider capacity + cost) and visible reasons (`pickAcrossProviders` returns and toasts a reason per switched pick), but there is no persisted routing rationale surface, the privacy audit of sideband calls (`chat:suggest`/`chat:fill` now exist beside spec generation) is undone, and cancellation/scheduler work is untouched.*
- [x] **AN7: Managed inference runtime.** Decomposed into four phases after the 2026-09-09 design session: **A** control plane, **B** fit planner, **C** model catalog + downloads, **D** bundled engine. A and B shipped as T149/T150; C and D shipped together as T154, which is what removed the separate-inference-server requirement. Runtime and model downloads still require explicit consent, which T154 keeps: nothing multi-hundred-megabyte happens without a press. Design: [managed inference runtime](docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-09-managed-inference-runtime-design.md), plan: [managed runtimes](docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-09-managed-runtimes.md).
- [x] **T149**, **Runtime control plane (AN7 phase A).** Local model servers are now started, stopped, and driven rather than merely observed. New `packages/host/src/runtimes/` puts all three behind one `RuntimeAdapter` whose **`RuntimeCapabilities`** record (not its kind) tells the UI what to offer, which is why there is no runtime-kind branching anywhere in the renderer and why phase D's bundled engine will need no UI change. **Ollama** is supervised as a child process we own (`supervisor.ts`: PID ownership, 60s health-poll budget, a 200-line log ring buffer, SIGTERM then SIGKILL, and a `process.on('exit')` sweep so quitting never leaves an orphaned server holding VRAM); it loads via `/api/generate` with `num_ctx`/`num_gpu`/`keep_alive` and reads geometry from `/api/show`'s architecture-prefixed `model_info` map (matched on the key suffix, so a new architecture needs no code change). **LM Studio** drives its real `lms server start|stop` lifecycle plus `lms load --context-length --gpu --ttl`, keeping T99's lesson that `lms` exits 0 on "Model Not Found"; its weights come from `lms ls --json`, the only place LM Studio publishes a size. **vLLM is connect-existing only**: its adapter has *no* `start` method at all, so it cannot be started even by mistake, and its `/metrics` Prometheus text is parsed for KV-cache pressure and queue depth. **Ownership is the behavioural change**: stopping a process Nekko Agent did not start now asks first and names it, where T98 killed whatever held the port silently; the old `servers.ts` port-killer remains only as the confirmed-force path. Six new IPC channels through the full five-file chain. 30 host tests. · [spec](SPEC.md#models--providers) · Done: 2026-09-09
- [x] **T150**, **VRAM fit planner (AN7 phase B).** New pure `packages/core/src/capacity/` answers "will this fit, where will it run, and why" before the load: weights + KV cache (`2 x layers x kvHeads x headDim x context x bytesPerElement x parallelSlots`) + overhead against one pool, yielding a verdict, estimated CPU-resident layers, structured reasons carrying their own numbers, and suggestions that are **re-planned before being offered** so a chip never proposes something that would not help. Three honesty rules are enforced by tests, not convention: **`unknown` is a first-class verdict** (never infer capacity from a model name), **unified memory is never summed with system RAM** (a 64 GB Mac is not 128 GB), and **discrete GPUs are not pooled**. Where a server reports less than the planner needs, the answer is **partial rather than absent**: LM Studio's real weights are still shown and a will-not-load verdict is still certain when the weights alone overflow. Reconciliation closes the loop, measured residency after a load feeds a per-runtime EMA calibration record (`runtime-calibration.json`, keyed by runtime version, absurd residuals discarded), so the overhead constant improves instead of staying an unaudited guess. UI: runtime cards replace the standalone Stop-server button, with a power toggle, copy/edit address, resident models showing their real GPU/CPU split, and a fit drawer whose context slider drives a live segmented bar with a marker at the device's edge. 38 planner tests + 23 verdict-copy tests. **Verified live** against the real LM Studio on this Mac (10 models, 219 GB): full stop/start cycle through the API, real weights (37.0 GB for hermes-4-70b) projected against the M1 Max's 49.7 GB free with the unified pool counted once, vLLM correctly refusing to start, and Ollama correctly reporting not-installed rather than offering a dead button. · [spec](SPEC.md#models--providers) · [docs](docs/local-models.md) · Done: 2026-09-09
- [x] **T154**, **The Nekko engine (AN7 phases C + D).** The model server Nekko Agent runs itself, so a fresh install runs a model with nothing else present. Built as one feature because neither half is useful alone: an engine with no models cannot run anything, and a GGUF with no engine is a 4 GB file. **Acquisition**: `engine/builds.ts` picks a llama.cpp build from the GPU probe rather than the platform (CUDA only with an `nvidia-smi` reading, Vulkan for an unidentified GPU, Metal on Apple Silicon, CPU otherwise), matching the architecture explicitly because the same accelerator ships for both, comparing CUDA toolkit versions numerically, and deriving the `cudart` companion from the archive actually chosen so a CUDA 13 runtime cannot land beside a CUDA 12 build. `releases/latest` is a **marker release** carrying one text file that names the current nightly, so recent releases are scanned for the first with real archives. **Catalog**: a short curated starter list plus Hugging Face GGUF search, quantizations discovered from each repo's file tree (so real bytes, not model-card claims), split models collapsed onto their first shard with the rest as companions, and projectors excluded from the model list. **Downloads**: resumable via `Range` (and starting over when the server ignores it rather than appending onto bytes it did not extend), `.part` until the GGUF parses, real cancellation that tears down the stream instead of trusting the abort signal to reach it, and a `verify`/`after` split so unpacking an archive cannot consume the file the transfer still has to rename. **GGUF reader**: `engine/gguf.ts` reads the header only, walking string arrays so a 256k-token vocabulary cannot hide the geometry behind it, preferring a published `attention.key_length` over `embedding / heads` (wrong for Gemma 3), and returning null rather than a guess for anything it cannot parse. This is what makes a projection for our own model **exact** where LM Studio's is partial. **Router**: `engine/server.ts` is one OpenAI-compatible address fronting a `llama-server` child per loaded model, bodies piped through untouched in both directions so streaming and tool calls need no knowledge of their shape; load-on-demand, LRU eviction past the resident limit, idle TTL, optional Bearer key with `/health` deliberately left open, CORS off unless configured, and a LAN binding that states what it costs. **Auto-fit**: `core/capacity/autofit.ts` solves one budget fraction into context, GPU layers and KV dtype, with context as the outer loop and precision the inner one (keep the longer conversation, pay with a cheaper KV element), keeping T150's three honesty rules and describing the cost in memory rather than in invented tokens per second. **UI**: the engine gets its own section above the connected servers, with install, library, catalog, downloads and server tabs, and a per-model drawer whose Simple and Advanced surfaces are one state. The renderer needed **no runtime-kind branching**, which is what T149's capability record was for. 77 new tests. **Verified live** on the RTX 5090 Windows machine: engine b10996 installed, Qwen3 4B Q4_K_M (2.5 GB) downloaded from Hugging Face, geometry read from the file (36 layers, 8 KV heads, 40960 context), loaded at 32k taking a **measured 7.94 GB against a 7.75 GB projection**, answering through the router at 162-292 tok/s, streaming deltas proxied intact, and a cold load-on-demand in 3.4 s. Four bugs that only live running could find are recorded in the commit that fixed them. · [spec](SPEC.md#running-a-model-with-nothing-else-installed) · Done: 2026-09-16
