Fifth major product pillar, not a variation of chat model routing. See the [planned experience and safety contract](SPEC.md#one-click-setup-for-safe-offline-pc-control-planned). Reuse `host/system.ts`, `host/gpu.ts`, provider discovery, and existing approvals where appropriate. Current telemetry and shell/file tools do not constitute an offline voice/desktop-control stack. AN7 supplies the runtime lifecycle foundation; specific models and runtime dependencies are not selected until their requirements, licenses, and supported platforms are verified.

Placement puts GPU-preferring picks on the roomiest single device (never pooled) with RAM fallback; a demote-and-retry loop trades intent size for a complete stack; RAM holds back a fixed OS/app reserve; disk is checked combined. Exposed as `machine:readiness` through the five-touch chain (no UI yet; AN9f renders it). Verified live on the RTX 5090 Windows box via `scripts/itest-readiness.mjs` (13 unit cases + live probes; Windows reports honest `unverified` since CPUID flags need native code). · Done: 2026-09-11

- [x] **T125**, **macOS Developer ID signing + notarization, live as of v0.6.0.** The release workflow now signs, hardens, notarizes, staples, and verifies the macOS build, so a release `.dmg` now installs with no `xattr` workaround. Nekko Agent is packaged by **electron-builder**, which does all of this internally, so this deliberately does *not* copy Hypergate's hand-rolled `codesign`/`productbuild`/`productsign`/`notarytool` pipeline (that exists only because Hypergate ships a Rust + Node-SEA `.pkg`), and no `Developer ID Installer` certificate is needed: `.dmg`/`.zip` are signed with the Application cert. `hardenedRuntime` went `false` → `true` with new `build/entitlements.mac.plist` (`allow-jit` + `allow-unsigned-executable-memory` for V8, `disable-library-validation` because `@lydell/node-pty`'s prebuilt `.node` is dlopen'd from outside the asar, `allow-dyld-environment-variables` for spawning shells/agents/MCP servers); `notarize` stays `false` in the committed config and the workflow passes `-c.mac.notarize=true`, so a local `npm run dist` never demands an Apple API key. `scripts/after-pack.cjs` keeps ad-hoc signing as the unsigned-build fallback but early-returns when `CSC_LINK`/`CSC_NAME` is set so it can't fight the real signature. Enabling the hardened runtime surfaced a **latent crash**: `QrScanner.tsx` calls `getUserMedia` with no `NSCameraUsageDescription`, and a missing usage string kills the process rather than denying a prompt, so that plus `device.camera` were added. CI fails closed on a partially configured secret set (a signed-but-unnotarized app is still Gatekeeper-blocked while looking fine) and verifies every signed build with `codesign --verify --deep --strict`, `stapler validate`, and `spctl --assess` (note the ticket staples to the **`.app`, not the `.dmg`**). Verified locally against the real `Developer ID Application: Nekko Labs LLC (3HM5598S99)` identity. New `docs/signing.md`; README's "Nekko Agent is damaged" section rewritten and kept only as a pre-v0.6.0 note. **Unblocked 2026-08-14**: Philip set the five `nekko-labs` org secrets by hand (they are write-only, so Hypergate's could not be copied, and the `.p12` export needed his keychain password + org-admin). Two things bit on the way in, both worth knowing before the certificate rolls. **(1)** A first attempt had `MACOS_SIGNING_CERTS_P12` set and the other four missing, and the fail-closed validation caught it exactly as designed. **(2)** The second attempt failed at `security import` with `MAC verification failed during PKCS12 import`, because `gh secret set` without `--body` reads its value from **stdin**, so pasting the five commands as a block made `MACOS_CERT_PASSWORD` swallow the *next command's text* as its value. electron-builder echoes its `-P` argument on failure, which is how the command text was visible in the log. Set interactive secrets one command at a time, or with `read -rs "P?prompt: "` piped to `--body` (note `read -p` is bash-only; Philip's shell is zsh). A local `openssl pkcs12 -info` check is misleading here: OpenSSL 3 rejects Keychain's `RC2-40-CBC` payload with an `unsupported` algorithm error unless `-legacy` is passed, which looks like a bad password but is not, and the MAC verifying is the part that actually proves the password. First notarized build verified in CI: `valid on disk`, satisfies its Designated Requirement, `flags=0x10000(runtime)`, `Authority=Developer ID Application: Nekko Labs LLC (3HM5598S99)`, `stapler` validated, and `spctl` **accepted / source=Notarized Developer ID**. · [spec](SPEC.md#distribution--platforms) · Added: 2026-08-07 · Done: 2026-08-14

### Model-server polish, ChatGPT sign-in fix, and workspace/chat refinements (added 2026-10-01)

Philip's batch of feedback from a live session: the ChatGPT subscription connect dies on the fixed localhost callback, the Model server page's power control and "Start with Nekko" are in the wrong places, the page and its dropdowns are too narrow, downloaded models should default to a full-GPU fit, model rows should say where they run and warn when they cannot fill the GPU, the chat's right rail needs a model settings group, the model picker should lead with names and group by location, native select popups render white on white, the workspace column should resize, and the composer should understand markdown.

- [x] **MS1, ChatGPT sign-in falls back honestly when the fixed callback port is taken.** `oauth.ts`'s `startLoopback('chatgpt')` only tries port 1455; on `EADDRINUSE` the session degrades to manual but keeps `http://localhost:1455/auth/callback` as `redirect_uri`, so the browser still lands on the dead listener (photo 1's "can't connect" page). Fix: on bind failure, close any stale same-provider pending session still holding a listener and retry once; only then go manual. In manual mode the pasted answer is already a full URL, so the SubscriptionSignIn copy for ChatGPT must say to copy the address bar of the error page (not "the code"). Cover with tests: stale-session reclaim frees the port; an unrelated holder produces a manual session whose redirect URI stays 1455. Built as specified; SubscriptionSignIn's ChatGPT manual copy now describes the address bar of the error page; both reclaim and unrelated-holder cases are tested in `oauth.test.ts`. · Done: 2026-10-01
- [x] **MS2, Server power is a sliding toggle, with status and Start with Nekko beside it.** EngineSection's On/Off button becomes the shared sliding switch (extract `Toggle` into `components/primitives`, reuse in `EngineServerSettings` and `EngineLoadDrawer`); the serving/stopped state sits immediately right of it; "Start with Nekko" moves out of the EngineServerSettings accordion onto the same row (switch + label, writes `settings.autoStart` on click). Built as specified: shared `Toggle` lives in `components/primitives/`, the header row is power switch + status chip + Start with Nekko. · Done: 2026-10-01
- [x] **MS3, The Model server page is wider and its dropdowns stop clipping.** Left column `minmax(340px,0.85fr)` becomes roughly 40% wider; the "Reachable from" select widens (`w-40` to `w-64`), ports keep working beside it; the composer's model-picker menu widens from `w-80` (photo 2's long ids wrap badly). Done: left column widened to `minmax(460px,1.5fr)` and the picker menu widened with room for name + path. · Done: 2026-10-01
- [x] **MS4, A downloaded model defaults to the fullest GPU fit, and auto-start turns on.** In `engine/index.ts`'s `onDownloadsChanged` wrapper: when a main GGUF/MLX model job flips to `done`, run `autoFit` (budget 0.9, GPU-first context stops) and `savePreset` the winning `LoadParams` (`gpuLayers` = full `n_gpu_layers` when `planFit` says `full`), plus `saveSettings({ autoStart: true })`. No GPU devices probed or fit `unknown`: skip the preset, still flip auto-start. `DownloadJob` gains `dest` so the completion hook can find the library row without reparsing. Built as specified in `engine/index.ts` (`dest` on `DownloadJob`, autoFit preset + `autoStart: true` on done; preset skipped only when the hardware probe cannot answer). · Done: 2026-10-01
- [x] **MS5, Model rows say where they run and warn when they cannot fill the GPU.** `ResidentModel` gains `gpuLayers`/`totalLayers` (from `LoadParams` when GPU was chosen; `loadedOn` already exists). `models()` merges a `gpuFit` verdict (`full`/`partial`/`cpu`/`wont`/`unknown`) computed once per row with `computeFit` over the hardware snapshot and the model's saved preset (or engine defaults). ModelLibrary: a "on CPU" warning style on non-full rows, placement text ("GPU · all layers" / "GPU+CPU · 20 of 33 layers" / "CPU") on loaded rows, plus a `loaded on` chip on resident models. Built as specified: `gpuLayers`/`totalLayers`/`loadedOn` flow from server children to `ResidentModel`, `models()` carries `gpuFit`, rows group by location, warn on `partial`/`wont`, and show placement. · Done: 2026-10-01
- [x] **MS6, The right rail gets a collapsible Model group with reload.** New `ModelDock` in ContextInspector between the limits and resources docks: current loaded engine models with placement (GPU/CPU-RAM, layers), context, and a compact settings form (context, GPU offload, KV cache, keep loaded) that reloads through `runtimeLoad`; collapse persists in `brandKey('nekko.modeldock')`. Only renders when the engine provider exists. Built as `ModelDock` (components/engine), seated between ProviderLimitsDock and ResourceDock in ContextInspector; per-resident rows show placement dot/label plus a folded settings form (GPU layers, context, KV cache, keep-loaded) that reloads via `runtimeLoad`; collapse persists under `nekko.modelDock.open`; hidden until the engine is installed. · Done: 2026-10-01
- [x] **MS7, The picker leads with names and groups by location.** `modelRow()` carries `name`, `path`, and a location label; the OpenAI-compat `listModels` maps them into `ModelInfo.name`/`details`; `ModelPicker` renders name first, path as subtext (price stays for priced models), and sub-groups rows by location when a provider has more than one. Menu widens per MS3. Built as specified; server-provided names are honored for local engine kinds only, which keeps the OpenAI golden behavior intact. · Done: 2026-10-01
- [x] **MS8, Native select popups stop rendering white on white.** Broaden `select.input option` to `select option` in styles.css so `ctl-menu` and every other native select gets themed option colors in both themes. One-line change to `select option` in styles.css. · Done: 2026-10-01
- [x] **MS9, The workspace column resizes horizontally.** `WorkspacesView`'s fixed `w-64` sidebar gains a drag handle on its right edge (same divider styling as the context rail), min 200 max 480, width persisted in `brandKey('nekko.workspaceSidebar')`, keyboard arrows on the separator, double-click resets. Textarea text goes transparent with `caret-color` set, selection still visible; scroll stays synced. Bubbles already render markdown, this closes the input side. Built as `ComposerHighlight.tsx` behind the transparent-text/visible-caret textarea in ChatPane; `scrollbar-gutter: stable` keeps wrap points synced; seven focused tests cover headings, lists, emphasis, code, and hashtags. · Done: 2026-10-01
- [x] **MS11, Windows in a split keep one gap and stop sliding under the right panel.** A split row stacked the space twice: the container's `gap: var(--pane-gap)` around a Divider that is itself `--pane-gap` wide put 21px between windows where the field rhythm is 7, and children sized `flex: 0 0 x%` of the box meant a row ran past its edge by exactly the divider widths, so the rightmost window's edge painted over the inspector. The split container drops its `gap` (the divider is the space itself, as intended) and children now size as `flex: <size> 1 0` grow weights, filling the box minus dividers exactly. · Done: 2026-10-01

### Agent loop: progress-based runaway detection (added 2026-10-01)

Observation: the fixed per-reply step budget (`DEFAULT_MAX_STEPS`, was 80) was hit several times on real coding turns that were still making progress, each time forcing a manual "continue". Interim fix: default raised to **250** (TS `packages/shared/src/settings.ts` and Rust `crates/nekko-loop/src/run.rs`, kept in sync). The longer-term intent is to drop the blunt count as the primary guard and detect a loop that has actually stopped making progress.

- [x] **Observe first.** The `done` event now carries `stop` (`complete` / `step_limit` / `loop` / `runaway`) and `steps` (tool round trips), from both the TS loop and the Rust port. The host appends one line per reply to `replies.jsonl` in the data dir (`packages/host/src/replies.ts`: ts, session, provider, model, steps, stop, budget; counts only, skipped for incognito, cleared with usage). `UsageSummary.replies` folds it into totals per ending, p50/p90/max steps, and the last 10 budget/loop stops, shown as **Agent replies** under Command Center → Insights → Usage. *Done: 2026-10-01.*
- [x] **Repetition detector.** `packages/core/src/agent/progress.ts` (Rust twin `crates/nekko-loop/src/progress.rs`): trips when the same call (tool + input) returns the same result 3 times within the last 12 results (which also catches A→B→A→B cycles), or one tool errors 5 times in a row. A re-run whose result changed is progress and does not count. First trip: a transient nudge ("you appear to be stuck in a loop: …; try a different approach or explain what is blocking you") and the window resets. Second trip: the reply ends early through the same tools-withheld wrap-up pass as the step budget, with a note naming the repeated call. Golden scenarios cover both paths so TS and Rust stay identical. *Done: 2026-10-01.*
- [ ] **Progress signals.** Count a step as progress when it touches something new: a file read or written for the first time, a new command, a plan step marked done, a new error message. Trip on a long stretch (for example 30 steps) with no new progress signal, rather than on a total count.
- [ ] **Soft check-in instead of a hard stop.** When a detector trips, inject a nudge ("you appear to be repeating X; change approach or wrap up") first. Only on a second trip, wrap up as today. Optionally surface a non-blocking "still going, N steps, M min, $X" chip with Stop / Keep going in the chat.
- [ ] **Cost and time budgets.** Spend (USD for cloud, wall-clock for local) and context-window pressure, which are what a user actually cares about. The tool-step limit itself is gone entirely (T182) and is not coming back as a setting or per-chat override; loop and output guards remain active. Spend and wall-clock budgets are still open.

- [x] **T151**, **Remote phone messaging: drive a chat from a messaging app.** **Shipped the Telegram slice**: `settings.messaging.telegram` (`enabled`, `botToken`, `allowedChatIds`), a long-poll Bot API client (`packages/host/src/messaging/telegram.ts`, `getUpdates`/`sendMessage`/`editMessageText`/`answerCallbackQuery`, injectable fetch, 25 s long-poll so no public URL is needed), and the bridge service (`messaging/service.ts`): each allowed chat id binds to one persisted session (`messaging.json`), replies stream into one message edited on a 1.5 s flush rather than one message per delta, `tool_approval_required` becomes an inline Approve/Deny keyboard resolved through `approveTool` (the edited message notes the decision came from Telegram), `ask_user` questions become inline keyboards with typed replies accepted as free-text answers, app-originated turns on a bound session mirror out lazily, callback payloads are checked against live pending state so stale buttons report expired instead of acting, and the service restarts when the settings block changes and stops on host shutdown. Configured like a connector: a **Messaging channels** section on the Connectors page takes the bot token and the allowlist, everything off by default and default-deny when the list is empty. `messaging:status` rides the five-touch chain (channel → `NekkoApi` → host → dispatch → preload → web client). Eight host tests cover refusal, binding, streaming edits, approvals, questions, and expired callbacks. Deliberate deviations from the original plan: trust is the explicit chat-id allowlist rather than reusing T100 pairing codes (Telegram identifies chats, not devices, and a single-use QR inside Telegram adds nothing the allowlist does not); the relay push-token reuse is unbuilt because Telegram itself is the delivery channel; Slack and WhatsApp adapters are unbuilt, the `TelegramApi` boundary and the service's channel-keyed maps are the seam for them. Original plan, for context: T100 shipped phone remote *control* — a paired browser speaking the app's own protocol over the relay — which is the whole app on a phone. What it is not is the thing OpenClaw, Claude Code and Codex all offer: sending your agent a message the way you message a person, from the app you already have open, and getting the reply there. Scope: an inbound **messaging channel** as a first-class transport beside the relay, with a `MessagingAdapter` per provider (Telegram bot API and Slack are the cheapest first two — bot token, no phone-number provisioning; then WhatsApp Business / SMS via a BYO Twilio key; iMessage stays out of scope, it has no supportable API). Shape it like the existing connectors, not like a new app: adapters live in `packages/host/src/messaging/`, register the same way `WORKFLOW_ACTIONS` entries do, and are configured on the Connectors page. Behaviour that makes or breaks it: (1) **binding** — an inbound message resolves to one session, so a channel/thread maps to a chat and a reply in that thread continues it rather than starting over; unknown senders are refused, and pairing reuses T100's single-use code + device registry rather than inventing a second trust model; (2) **streaming without spam** — the agent's reply is edited into one message as it streams (Telegram/Slack both support message edit) instead of one message per delta, with tool activity folded into a single status line; (3) **approvals** — a `tool_approval_required` event becomes an inline Approve/Deny action in the message, and a guardrail decision made from a phone is recorded with the device that made it; (4) **push when it matters** — reuse the relay's existing per-device push tokens so "needs your input" reaches the phone even when the messaging app is closed. Two hard constraints carried from SPEC's scope boundaries: an inbound message is untrusted input (never an instruction to the *harness*, only a prompt to the *agent*), and messaging transports are off by default with the channel allowlist explicit, because a bot token in the wrong room is a remote shell. Needs: `messaging:*` IPC through the five-touch chain, a settings block per adapter, and integration tests against each provider's sandbox. *Planned: 2026-09-16.*

### Local models with no middleman (152)

- [x] **T152**, **Run llama.cpp directly, with controls that fit both kinds of user.** **Shipped as T154**, which built this together with the phase C catalog it depended on; the original plan is kept below because it is what T154 was built against. Today every local model went through a separate server the user installs and babysits (LM Studio, Ollama, vLLM). This is AN7 **phase D** — the bundled engine — made concrete: a fourth `RuntimeAdapter` that talks to `llama.cpp` itself, so the answer to "how do I run a local model" stops being "first install LM Studio". Build it against the contract T149 established, so the renderer needs no runtime-kind branching: `RuntimeKind` gains `'llamacpp'`, `RUNTIME_CAPABILITIES` gains its row (`canStart`/`canStop`/`canLoad` true, `canSetContext`, `canSetGpuLayers`, `canSetParallel: 'per-load'`, `canSetKvCacheType: 'per-load'` — llama.cpp takes all four as flags, which makes it the *most* controllable runtime we have, not the least), and `packages/host/src/runtimes/llamacpp.ts` supervises `llama-server` with the same PID-ownership, health-poll, log-ring and orphan-sweep rules as `supervisor.ts` already enforces for Ollama. Its OpenAI-compatible `/v1` endpoint means the existing `openai-compat` provider path serves chat unchanged; what is new is the control plane and the binary itself (ship per-platform builds with CPU/CUDA/Metal/Vulkan variants selected by probe, downloaded on **explicit consent** per AN7's standing rule, never silently).
  - **Two control surfaces over one model, because two kinds of user are asking.** The advanced surface mirrors LM Studio's right sidebar — context length, GPU layer offload, KV cache type (f16/q8_0/q4_0), batch size, threads, parallel slots, flash attention, rope scaling, mmap/mlock — each control disabled with a reason when the planner says it cannot help. The simple surface is a single question: **how much of this machine may it use?** One slider from "leave my machine usable" to "everything it has", which resolves to concrete flags through the T150 fit planner rather than through a lookup table, and states the trade in plain words ("~28 tok/s, other apps stay responsive" vs "~52 tok/s, expect stutter elsewhere"). The two surfaces are the same state; moving a slider in one moves the numbers in the other, so simple mode is a *view*, not a lesser mode.
  - **Auto-fit picks context and quantization together**, since they trade against each other in one pool: given the machine's free memory and the chosen utilization budget, the planner solves for the largest (quantization, context) pair that fits with the projected KV cache, and says why it landed there. It must keep T150's three honesty rules: `unknown` stays a first-class verdict, unified memory is never summed with system RAM, and discrete GPUs are not pooled. A quantization the user has not downloaded is offered with its download size, not silently excluded.
  - Depends on AN7 phase C (model catalog + downloads) for where the GGUFs come from. *Planned: 2026-09-16.*

### Provider coverage and capacity-aware routing (157, 160)

- [x] **T160**, Expanded the Capacity panel beyond OAuth subscriptions. `limitsKeyFor` (shared) is now the single source of truth for "does this provider have an authorized usage read": subscription providers read by `tokenKey` (Claude, ChatGPT, OpenRouter sign-in as before), and an OpenRouter **API key** gets a `provider:<id>` key that the host polls through `GET {baseUrl}/key`, the endpoint OpenRouter documents for the inference credential itself (windows for the free-tier daily allowance, credit balance, free-tier flag). Kinds with no read authorized for the inference key (OpenAI: its usage API requires a separate org admin key, which is not the configured credential; nothing else today) get no key, and the dock row says **no usage API** rather than implying data or inventing a quota. Devin has no provider kind in the app, so there was nothing to read. Verified headless over the web edition: seeded OpenAI key reads "no usage API", a seeded OpenRouter key shows its live free-model window and credit balance. · [spec](SPEC.md#models--providers) · Done: 2026-09-30
- [x] **T157**, Switch providers automatically on remaining capacity and cost, shipped as the per-chat **Follow capacity & cost** opt-in in the Auto profile menu (`session.autoProviderSwitch`, off by default, disabled in offline chats). `pickAcrossProviders` in `packages/shared/src/model-select.ts` does the selection: the home provider keeps the pick while it can serve; a binding window rate-limited or past 95% (`PROVIDER_SPENT_THRESHOLD`) triggers a move to the same-or-better capability tier (`modelTier` is the equivalence map) on the pool with the most headroom, measured limits beating assumed-full ones at a tie; a healthy but pricey home triggers the move only when a same-tier candidate is at most 60% of its blended per-MTok price (`PROVIDER_CHEAPER_RATIO`), provider-published prices first, local/subscription pools counting as free, unpriced models never claimable as cheap. The switched pick chips as `→ <model> · <provider>`, `send()` toasts the reason once on the turn that moves, and the send runs through the switched provider so the per-turn usage record keeps real attribution. The pick resolves before dispatch, never mid-stream, so transcript and tool state are untouched; other providers' model lists are fetched lazily only while the chat is in Auto with the opt-in on. Verified end-to-end headless: an OpenRouter key at 100% of its free-model window routed a Quality-profile turn onto a tier-equivalent o1-pro on a second provider, toast naming the spent window. One deliberate gap: with the opt-in off there is no "offer" surface (the spec's "offers, and opted in takes"), the cheaper-elsewhere signal is simply not surfaced. · [spec](SPEC.md#models--providers) · merged #211 · Done: 2026-09-30

- [x] **Running out of context does not stop the work** (2026-10-15, [SPEC.md](SPEC.md#features)): three layers, both engines. (1) `packages/core/src/agent/fit.ts` + `crates/nekko-loop/src/fit.rs`: with `contextWindow` set, a request past 80% of the window has its oldest tool outputs over 600 chars (never the last four messages) replaced in the *sent* copy by a one-line stub until it is under 60%; the count only grows within a run so the prefix stays cacheable. (2) `streamWithRetry` / `stream_with_retry`: a context-overflow error (`isContextOverflowError` / `is_context_overflow`, plain lower-case substrings so both ports match) gets one retry with everything eligible elided. (3) `packages/host/src/auto-continue.ts`, started in `createHost` on the shared agent-event bus (ignores `relayOnly` copies): an overflow error compacts the chat and sends a visible continue turn, at most `MAX_AUTO_CONTINUES` (2) in a row until a reply finishes; a finished reply with the transcript at 85% of the window compacts even with no pane open. Not for incognito, sub-agents, goal runs, archived or queued chats. The window comes from `packages/host/src/context-window.ts` (`contextWindowFor`: model list `contextLength`, Ollama `/api/ps`, else `guessContextWindow`; 60 s cache, 2.5 s cap) for in-process turns and host-built daemon specs, and `crates/nekkod/src/providers.rs` `context_window_for` for daemon-admitted turns (`facts.contextWindow`); compaction sizes its chunks from `knownContextWindow`. Renderer: `describeInterruption` names an overflow "Out of context" in the paused colour; a host-started reply clears the error notice. Tests: 3 new `runs.json` scenarios (golden regenerated from TS, `cargo test -p nekko-loop` matches), `auto-continue.test.ts`, `fit.rs` unit test; `chat-routing.test.ts` default-route check relaxed for the extra same-provider listing. Not yet exercised against a live small-window model.

- [x] **T182** (2026-10-03) Remove the tool-step limit entirely (supersedes #261, which made `0` mean unlimited). Every reply ended at once on "Stopped at this reply's 0-step tool limit": the dev app ran a `target/release` daemon built before #261, which read the host's `maxIterations: 0` as a zero-step budget. The concept is now gone, not re-tuned: no `maxIterations` / `max_iterations` in the TS or Rust loop, no `DEFAULT_MAX_STEPS` / `MAX_STEPS_RANGE` / `clampMaxSteps` / `maxSteps` setting (an old settings file still loads; the key is dropped), no budget wrap-up or `step_limit` stop (old reply records still read, counted as "Other"). The loop-detector wrap-up, runaway cut-off, empty-turn retry, cancel and output caps are unchanged. Skew guard: `daemon:info` advertises `loop:unbounded`, and the host hands `loop:run` only to a daemon that does (`daemonRunsLoops`), else the turn runs in process; a dev checkout's `findDaemon` takes the newer of `target/release` and `target/debug`. Command Center reply stats and Settings → Agent loop no longer mention steps or limits; TS/Rust loop goldens drop the budget scenarios.
- [x] **T183** (2026-10-03) Workspace card PR links, and compaction that finishes. Card line 3 drops the project folder (the card sits under its project heading) and the uncommitted count, leads with the branch (worktree glyph when linked), then a separator and each PR as its own `#N` link coloured by state (`SessionPrLinks`); more than three fold to a PR glyph + count whose hover/click list (portalled past the sidebar's clipping) links each one. Every pointer/key/drag event on a link stops at the link, so it never opens the chat; the line-1 PR badge is gone and the completion check is `h-3.5`. Compaction: real chats were 350k-970k chars, ~85% tool output, summarized whole in 60k chunks one at a time at the model's default effort, with no progress and no per-call limit, so on a reasoning model it ran for many minutes and looked stuck; the old banner also stayed on "running" forever after a cancel. Now `compaction.ts` clips tool output (head/tail 1500 chars) and arguments, summarizes three chunks at a time at `effort: 'low'`, times each call out at 4 min, emits `compaction` agent events (`CompactionProgress`: state, done/total, target), and joins a running job instead of refusing it. `compactSession(id, { newChat })` lands the summary in a forked chat (title "… (continued)") and leaves the source untouched, or redirects a running job there. Nothing is deleted any more: a `ChatMessage.compaction` marker goes in after the summarized turns, `sinceCompaction` / `estimateTranscriptTokens` and the context preview count from it, and both loops send `fromLatestCompaction` (TS `loop.ts`, Rust `nekko-loop` `from_latest_compaction`): the summary folded into the next user message so roles still alternate. Store summaries (`nekko-store` `transcript_tokens`) count the same way; new golden cases hold TS and Rust to it. Regenerate never pops a trailing summary. The renderer keeps progress app-wide (`compactionStatus.ts`) so the banner survives remounts, and draws the marker as a divider plus the summary card (`CompactionSummary`, older summaries folded).

Per-project worktree setup command (`WorkspaceFolder.worktreeSetup`) runs in each new chat checkout before its first turn, streamed to the Agent commands log, 15-minute cap with a Windows tree kill, failures reported in the checkout notice. `.worktreeinclude` copies listed AND gitignored files (`ls-files -o -i --directory` filtered through `check-ignore`), never overwriting. New `chatWorktrees:list` / `chatWorktrees:remove` channels (all five touches) back a Chat worktrees list in Settings > Git management; removal is refused while running or dirty, deletes the branch only with `branch -d`, and tolerates Windows leaving the emptied folder behind. A removed checkout is restored on the chat's next turn from its branch (or from HEAD once the merged branch is gone); a folder without its `.git` link counts as removed. Terminals split from a chat pane and the Agent commands log open in the chat's worktree (`createTerminal({ sessionId })`, resolved before the daemon hop). The web client now surfaces the host's error text instead of `HTTP 400`. `npm run dev` failed with "Electron uninstall" because Electron 43 downloads its binary lazily on `require('electron')` while electron-vite reads `path.txt` directly; `predev`/`prestart` now run `scripts/ensure-electron.mjs`. Verified: 6 worktree unit tests; two chats sent concurrently over an isolated web edition got separate worktrees with `.env` copied and setup run; remove with a terminal open, then restore on the next turn, kept the committed work; dirty removal refused with its message.

The box explains itself: `B` is billions of parameters, sizes are downloads, "fits here" is this machine's memory. Rows carry a **recommended** pick on curated models, a **fits here / tight fit / needs more memory** chip from `downloadFitVerdict` (new `shared/engine.ts` helper over a rough `size x 1.2` working set against `EngineStatus.memory.budgetBytes`, deliberately not the exact planner), and the model page marks the suggested build (Q4_K_M, else the lightest that fits) and explains what a build is. Provider/model pickers now show real price labels (`formatModelPriceLabel`): local = Free, subscription = Included in plan with list price muted, API key = $/MTok from `ModelInfo.pricing`.
  - **(2) A downloaded GGUF now starts.** The catalog already fetched `extraFiles` (projectors, shards); `server.ts` never used them. Now `quantsFor` discovers every sidecar a repo ships (mmproj projector, `chat_template.*`, tokenizer config, split shards, size-capped), the loader resolves companions from the model's own folder, `buildArgs` passes `--mmproj` and `--chat-template-file` when they exist, a missing split shard or required companion is an actionable error naming the absent file, and `LocalModel` reports whether the GGUF embeds its own chat template.
  - **(3) OpenRouter is a real provider.** `OAuthProvider` gains `openrouter`; its OAuth is PKCE without client id, scopes, or echoed state (`callback_url` on a dynamic loopback port), and the exchange at `/api/v1/auth/keys` returns a **permanent API key**, which is stored in the token store, never reaches the renderer, and is treated as non-expiring by `ensureFreshToken`. `resolveSubscriptionProvider` generalized so a `tokenKey` credential also satisfies `auth: 'apikey'` providers. The openai-compat provider sends `HTTP-Referer`/`X-Title`, validates through `/api/v1/key` (which also reports usage), lists `/models` with real context lengths and per-token pricing, and surfaces rate-limit/credit/auth/network failures as readable text. Onboarding and provider cards treat it as a first-class online option; it is deliberately **not** in the subscription-limits panel, since it bills per token.
  - **(4) Workspace cards name the work.** After a new chat's first reply, the host asks the same model for a short task-shaped title and applies it via a `session_meta` event; it is best-effort (a failed title never fails the reply), runs once, and never overwrites a title the user typed.
  - **(5) The right rail shows the agent's plan.** `buildSystemPrompt` gets a `canPlan` context and planning guidance; the agent reports its derived plan through `update_plan`, which `applyPlanUpdate` (new shared helper, also used by mission runs) folds into `Session.agentPlan`; `session_meta` pushes it to the renderer and `PlanRail` renders it as a live checklist **separate from** the user's editable prompt plan, so the rail is no longer the prompt restated as bullets.
  - **(6) The engine port is stated, not implied.** `ENGINE_PORT_DEFAULT = 11500` stays the single source; `EngineServerSettings` shows the full endpoint even while stopped and explains local-only vs LAN binding and the API-key consequences plainly.
  - **(7) Workflows speak plainly.** A collapsible **How workflows work** strip defines automation / manual trigger / scheduled job / event triggers / groupings; the sidebar's trigger filter groups kinds by family ("You start it" / "On a schedule" / "On an event") with each kind's hint as its tooltip; Group-by's "Listener" reads **Trigger** with an explanation of what it answers; and the new-workflow picker separates **Start from scratch** (blank) from **Start from an example** (templates, described as editable starting points) — the empty state offers both.
  - **Verified**: shared/host/desktop typechecks; host engine + oauth + catalog unit tests; desktop build. Visual captures in the PR. · [spec](SPEC.md#the-model-server-tab) · Done: 2026-09-30

### Latest, the workbench shows where the work is going (2026-09-28)

- [x] **T156**, **Three chats' worth of feedback, finished together because they touched the same surfaces: which branch, worktree and PR a chat is working against, a context gauge that was five times too small and still undercounted, effort levels that did not match the model, and runs that stopped showing when you looked away.** (T-number to re-check at merge per the shared-checkout note.)
  - **Git position on the card and in the chat header.** New `host/git.ts`: one `git status --porcelain=v2 --branch` per folder, cached 5s and coalesced, never throws; plus `rev-parse --git-dir --git-common-dir --show-toplevel` to recognise a **linked worktree** (`parseWorktree`), and `branchPr` (`gh pr view <branch>`, cached a minute per folder + branch, held at most 3s on a card's first read) for the PR the branch is going into. Wired through the five layers as `workspace:gitStatus`. The card now reads **source / model** ("Claude / Opus 5") and the chat's **context fill** (`155k/1M`, coloured by fill) instead of the account's usage window, which moved to its own panel; line 3 carries the worktree (merged into the folder when they are the same folder), the branch (truncating instead of running off the card), dirt and drift; the branch PR joins the PR badges. The chat header shows the same worktree, branch and PR.
  - **Context that is the right size.** The catalog said 200k for every Claude model; all current ones hold 1M (Haiku 4.5 holds 200k). New `shared/model-capabilities.ts` decides this once by family and version (`claudeContextWindow`, `guessContextWindow`) for the catalog, the host's headroom bar and the card. The conversation is counted as it is replayed (`historyText` / `estimateTranscriptTokens`: text, tool arguments and tool output; stored reasoning is not replayed), and the context block is rendered from each item's full content instead of its 160-character preview, which had meant attached files and guidelines reached the model as one truncated line while the inspector reported their full size. The preview sent to the renderer drops the full contents, so a refresh does not ship every attached file over IPC. Duplicate guidelines are gone: a `CLAUDE.md` that only points at `AGENTS.md` is skipped, as is a verbatim copy. Measured on a real 440-message chat: **6,292 tok of 200k before, 156,236 of 1M after**.
  - **Effort follows the model.** `EffortLevel` gains Anthropic's rungs (`low`/`medium`/`high`/`xhigh`/`max`, with `normal` meaning "the model's default"); `modelEffortLevels`, `modelDefaultEffort` and `effectiveEffort` say which a model has, that Opus 5.5 defaults to `medium`, and what a saved level becomes on a model without it. The menu shows **Default (high)** plus the five rungs on Claude 4.7+/Fable, the three temperature steps elsewhere; the Anthropic provider sends the rung as-is and never a rung the model lacks. Behaviour change: a saved `high` used to be sent as `max`; it is now `high`, and `max` is its own choice. **Opus 5.5** added to the catalog.
  - **Capacity panel.** `ProviderLimitsDock` above the resource meters: every signed-in cloud provider, a bar per usage window with reset time, credit balance, and a status that tells "not signed in", "reading", "no quota reported" and "metered" apart (`useProviderLimitsPortfolio` records which providers have answered). Auto-switching on it is **T157** (shipped: the *Follow capacity & cost* opt-in in the Auto profile menu).
  - **Resizable composer.** A grip on the composer's top border trades message-box height against the transcript (transcript floor 160px), persisted in `localStorage`; double-click resets to growing with the draft.
  - **From the earlier chats in the same tree:** questions are asked one per step with Next/Back, in their answer's shape, as a vertical option list; every session's live turn is folded app-wide (`liveRuns.ts`) so a workspace switch no longer drops streamed output and a pane adopts the run on mount; the reply status says what is happening in a few words (`shortLiveStatus`); sub-agents appear in the rail while they run; the plan rail's steps are typed into, reordered by drag and assigned to sub-agents; narration between tool calls stays in the conversation and splits the step groups; and Anthropic's per-model usage windows (the Fable limit) survive a response header that does not carry them (`mergeWindows`).
  - **Installed means set up (the CLI/MCP installer chat, finished here).** The local API server defaults **on** (`ensureApiServerToken` mints the token before the first sync, or on-by-default would be a server that never listens). `ApiServerBind` gains `custom` + `host`, and `advertisedUrl` separates what clients are told from what is bound (`apiServerBindHost` / `apiServerClientUrl`, with `lanAddress()` so a LAN bind is quoted as something dialable). The CLI ships **inside the app**: `apps/cli/scripts/bundle-app.mjs` builds one CommonJS file (node-pty stubbed, the version inlined because `import.meta.url` does not exist in CJS; the first build crashed on exactly that), shipped via `extraResources`, run by the app's Electron with `ELECTRON_RUN_AS_NODE=1` through a launcher in `<userData>/bin`. PATH on Windows goes to the **user** PATH via .NET's `SetEnvironmentVariable` rather than `setx %PATH%` (which copies the system PATH into the user one and truncates at 1024). Linked automatically only in packaged builds, so a dev run never edits a developer's PATH. `cli-link.json` in the CLI's data dir carries url, token, `enabled` and the app's `pid`; the CLI's precedence is flag, env, a live link, then the local data dir, and a link whose process is gone (or that a clean quit marked not serving) is ignored. `local-access.ts` runs the refresh on start and on every server change: link file, plus `refreshSubagent` for MCP entries **this app wrote** (`isOwnEntry`: the npx form or our launcher; a hand-written `node …/dist/index.js mcp` is never touched). MCP installs from the renderer are filled with the live target by the desktop transport, so the renderer never holds it. Onboarding pre-checks detected tools and installs the checked ones on Next. The Server tab owns bind preset, custom host, port, advertised address, token and a bash/PowerShell env block (masked on screen, whole on copy); the CLI tab shows install state with Install/Reinstall.
  - **Verified end to end** in the desktop app under an isolated user-data dir, home and link dir, seeded with a fake Claude Code (`npx` entry plus an unrelated server) and Codex config: first launch served on 127.0.0.1:1439 with a minted token, wrote the link with its pid, and re-pointed the Claude entry while leaving the unrelated one alone; the bundled CLI (`status`, `sessions --json`, run through the app's Electron) reported "the app on this machine"; onboarding showed Codex pre-checked and Next wrote its TOML entry with a `.bak`; changing the port to 1450 in the Server tab moved the listener and updated the link, the Claude entry and the Codex section in place; quitting marked the link not serving and the CLI fell back to the local data dir. The Windows PATH script was dry-run against an entry already on the user PATH (reports present, writes nothing).
  - **Verified** over the web edition in an isolated data dir seeded with the real chats, one pointed at a linked worktree of this repo: clicking into the chat (which had been crashing on a missing `ProviderLimitsDock` import) opens it; the card and header showed worktree `nekko-agent-workflows`, branch `ci/release-trigger-fallback` and its merged PR #149; the inspector listed one Guidelines entry at 16% of 1,000,000; the effort menu on Opus 5 listed Default (high) + five rungs; dragging the grip grew the message box from 52 to 318px, persisted, and a double-click reset it. Typecheck x9 and 1,299 tests green. · [spec](SPEC.md#chat--cowork-unified-surface) · Done: 2026-09-28

### Latest, Model Server splits out and the library reads the whole machine (2026-09-17)

- [x] **T155**, **Three asks, one shape: the local model story got its own tab, the library got the rest of the machine's models, and every panel got the same corner.** (T-number to re-check at merge per the shared-checkout note.)
  - **(1) Model Server is its own tab; Models is Model Providers.** One page had been answering two unrelated questions: *where do models come from* (a Claude subscription, an API key, a server on another machine) and *what on this computer runs them*. They are two destinations now. **Model Server** (`ModelServerView`, view id `modelserver`, new `ModelServerColorIcon`) carries the engine, its library, its folders, its server settings, and the **Nekko Agent as a server** block (local API / CLI / MCP) that used to sit at the bottom of Models under two other things, since "what on this machine can other software talk to" is the same question twice. **Model Providers** keeps the provider cards and gains a one-line card pointing at the new tab. `nav.modelserver` is translated in all 7 locales, both destinations are in the command palette, and the onboarding "Nekko Agent (this app)" card deep-links to Model Server with copy that stops calling managed inference "on the way" -- it shipped.
  - **(2) The library reads the folders the rest of the machine already keeps models in.** A GGUF is a GGUF whoever downloaded it, so making someone re-download Qwen because Ollama has it was a worse product for a simpler implementation. `engine/folders.ts` knows the default locations, per platform, of Ollama, LM Studio, Jan, GPT4All, llama.cpp, KoboldCpp, LocalAI, Bionic, text-generation-webui and the Hugging Face cache (which is what vLLM serves from), honouring `OLLAMA_MODELS` / `HF_HOME` / `HUGGINGFACE_HUB_CACHE` where they are set. On first run `seedFolders` probes each and adopts the ones that actually hold models -- once only, so a folder removed on purpose stays removed. `createLibrary` scans a **list of roots** instead of one: borrowed folders walk deeper (5 vs 3, because a Hugging Face cache files a model at `models--org--repo/snapshots/<commit>/x.gguf`), `blobs/` directories are skipped, duplicate roots are dropped by resolved path (vLLM and the HF cache are the same directory under two names), and every row carries `folderId` / `folderProvider` / `managed`. **Ids stay compatible**: our own models keep their bare path-relative ids so anything already saved in a preset or typed into another tool still resolves, and borrowed ones are prefixed by folder, with a numeric suffix on collision. **Ollama needed its own reader** (`engine/ollama.ts`): it stores models as content-addressed blobs with no extension, so a directory walk finds a pile of hashes; its manifests are parsed instead, the `application/vnd.ollama.image.model` layer's digest maps to `blobs/sha256-...`, and the default registry and the `library` namespace are dropped so a model appears as `llama3.2:latest`. Borrowed folders are **read, never written**: `remove` refuses and names the app that owns the file, and the row shows `borrowed` rather than a delete button that would fail after the click. New **Folders** tab (`ModelFolders.tsx`, `engineFolders` / `engineFoldersSave` IPC) lists every folder with what it contributes, an on/off that keeps the row, an editable path, add and remove, plus the known layouts found on disk that nobody has added yet. And the engine's install gate no longer hides the library: a machine with twenty models on it said "not installed" and nothing else, so the tabs render whether or not the runtime is downloaded and only **Load** is held back.
  - **(3) A model gets its own page.** Opening one from **Find models** takes the whole view instead of unfolding a drawer inside a list, because "should I run this" needs what a row cannot hold. `ModelDetail.tsx` shows the publisher's model card, downloads and likes, first-published and last-updated, declared task, license, base model, every tag, and the ladder of builds with each one's real transfer size and whether it is already in the library. The catalog gained the data behind it (`full=true` on search; `likes`, `lastModified`, `createdAt`, `pipeline_tag`, and the `base_model:` / `license:` tags; and `detail()`, which also fetches the README). The card is **parsed into elements we construct** -- headings, lists, tables, fenced and inline code, emphasis, links -- never `innerHTML`; publisher HTML is reduced to its text, and images are dropped rather than fetched, since fetching a badge would tell a third-party host that this machine opened this model.
  - **(4) Every panel has the same corner, and the chat's top finally rounds.** Only **one** element in the whole app carried `.squircle`, so the workbench was a rounded window flanked by two full-bleed square columns. New `panel` + `panel-ring` utilities: the sidebar, the windows, the inspector and the empty state are all panels on one `--surface-2` field at `--pane-gap`. The rounding bug turned out to be the ring, not the clip -- `overflow: hidden` was cutting the corner correctly all along, but the active-pane outline was an **`inset` box-shadow, which paints on the element's own background layer**, so the title strip's opaque background covered exactly the stretch of ring that traces the top corners. It is a `::after` above the children now. A base rule also gives `rounded-lg/xl/2xl/3xl` the superellipse (`rounded-full` deliberately excluded: a superellipse at 50% is not a circle).
  - **Verified** over the web edition in an isolated data dir against this machine's real installs: the seed found **Ollama (4 models, 29.6 GB)** and **LM Studio (16 models, 175 GB)**, and the library listed all 20 across 2 folders, each row badged with the app it came from and offering `borrowed` in place of delete, with the Ollama entries named from their manifests. Find models -> Qwen2.5 7B Instruct opened its page with 190k downloads, 75 likes, 24 builds, "2y ago", the full build ladder with sizes, and a model card whose links rendered as links and whose quant table rendered as a table. The chat pane's top-left corner was shot at 64x before and after: the accent ring stops dead below the title strip before, and traces the full squircle after. 21 new host tests (multi-root scanning, id compatibility, delete refusal, duplicate roots, Ollama manifests, catalog detail, front-matter handling); typecheck x9 and 1202 tests green. · [spec](SPEC.md#running-a-model-with-nothing-else-installed) · Done: 2026-09-17

### Latest, workspaces replace tabs (2026-09-16)

- [x] **T154**, **The centre has no tabs any more: each thing on the left is a workspace, and a workspace is however many windows you arrange around the work.** Four pieces of feedback, one shape.
  - **(1) The run details belong to the card, not to a panel.** `RunInfo` was one card at the foot of the sidebar describing whichever chat happened to be open, so the answer to "what is *that* agent running" meant opening it first. It is gone; `WorkspaceCard` states it per workspace in **three dense lines**: title + live state + PR badge, then model / provider / the usage window closest to being spent, then the project folders and the window count. Same facts, on every row, at 10px and 15px line height so a full sidebar still scans.
  - **(2) No more tabs; windows split in any direction.** `renderer/layout.ts` is a new pure split tree (`WbPane` leaves, `WbSplit` branches with parallel `sizes`) with `splitPane` / `removePane` / `movePane` / `resizeSplit` / `extent` / `canSplit`, 21 unit tests. Splitting beside a sibling on the same axis **joins** that split instead of nesting inside it, and a split left with one child collapses into it, so three windows in a row stay one row of three. Ceiling of **8 across and 8 down**, checked by building the candidate tree and measuring it rather than by reasoning about the tree in place (a split beside a same-axis sibling widens the workspace; one across it does not). Windows are added through the **split compass**: hovering a window's + draws that window with a target on each side and a dashed line running out to each, the line under the pointed-at direction running faster and in the accent; pick a side, then pick chat / terminal / files / browser. Drag a window's title strip onto any side of another to move it (the drop target is a full-window overlay, because the thing you drop onto is usually a browser or terminal that would swallow the drag first; `edgeAt` picks the nearest edge in *fractions* of the window, so each corner belongs to its shorter side). Every divider drags, with pointer **capture** so it keeps tracking over a window's own iframe. A **file explorer** is now a window kind of its own (`files`, `ExplorerPane`), so a folder can sit beside the work instead of only on the far right.
  - **(3) Workbench is Workspaces.** The surface, its sidebar header, its empty state, and the docs. The rename forced the name collision into the open: `settings.workspaces` is the **project folder** list that the UI has always called Projects, so the store's old `activeWorkspaceId` / `setActiveWorkspace` became **`activeProjectId` / `setActiveProject`** and the new names describe the layout. Typecheck could not have caught that on its own (both are `string`), so every caller was swept by hand.
  - **(4) Opus 5 rejected `temperature`.** Anthropic removed the sampling parameters from the 4.7 generation onward and replaced them with `output_config.effort`; sending one is a 400, not a warning, so every Opus 5 / Opus 4.8 / Sonnet 5 / Fable chat failed outright. `ChatRequest` now carries the effort setting alongside the temperature and each provider sends whichever its model takes. `rejectsSampling(model)` splits on **version, not a list of ids** (`fable`/`mythos` always; `opus`/`sonnet`/`haiku` at major ≥ 5 or 4.7+), so a model released after this build lands on the right side of it, and anything that does not parse as a Claude id keeps the old behaviour. Our three effort levels map to `low` / `high` / `max`, with `normal → high` because that is the API's own default: `medium` would have quietly made every Claude chat think less than it did before.
  - **Verified** over the web edition in an isolated data dir (headless Edge over CDP at dSF 2, seeded with one project folder): the compass opened on the pane's + and rendered the four targets and their lines, picking **To the right → Terminal** split the chat and started a live PowerShell beside it, **Below → Files** nested a column under it, opening `SPEC.md` from the explorer left the explorer in place, dragging a divider moved only its own pair (100/200 → 200/100 with the other two untouched), and dragging a window's title strip onto another window's left edge moved it across the row while a bottom-edge drop nested a new column. 21 layout tests + 4 `edgeAt` tests + 8 provider tests added; typecheck x9, 968 tests, and `build:web` green. · [spec](SPEC.md#chat--cowork-unified-surface) · Done: 2026-09-16

### Latest, the workbench tells you what it is doing (2026-09-16)

- [x] **T153**, **One pass over the ten things the workbench was hiding: a limits number that was wrong, a context gauge that was frozen, a step log that hid the sequence, a model list that hid half the models, and panels that hid the work.** Ten pieces of feedback, landed together because they are all the same complaint: the app knew something and did not say it.
  - **(1) The 6200% limits chip.** The two Anthropic surfaces disagree on scale and nobody had noticed: the `anthropic-ratelimit-unified-*` **response headers** report utilization as a fraction (`0.62`), while `GET /api/oauth/usage` reports **whole percent** (`62`). Both went through the same `x 100`, so the startup poll rendered a 62%-used week as **6200%** and it only corrected once a real request replaced the snapshot with header-derived numbers. `parseAnthropicUsageJson` now reads percent, its `rate_limited`/`warning` thresholds move from `1.0`/`0.8` to `100`/`80`, and every parser routes through one `clampPercent` so a future scale surprise degrades to "pegged at 100%" rather than to a number nobody can read. Two regression tests pin both halves (62 stays 62; 6200 clamps).
  - **(2) A context gauge that moves while the turn does.** It refreshed on mount and on `done`, so a twenty-minute run showed the same number throughout and jumped at the end. It was always *able* to be live: the agent loop appends each assistant message and tool result to the session as it goes and `chat.ts` checkpoints after every completed step, so a mid-turn `previewContext` is real, not stale. The pane now refreshes on `tool_result` (throttled to 1.5s) and carries a live estimate of what has streamed since, settled against each refresh by subtracting a mark rather than resetting (tokens that arrive during the round trip would otherwise vanish). The breakdown gains a **This reply** band, and the window size now comes from the *picked* model's real `contextLength` instead of the bundle's id-based guess, so changing model moves the denominator immediately. The Context Inspector follows the same events. (To the question behind the ask: yes — the agent's own text, its tool calls, and their results are all replayed in the next request of the same turn, so they are window usage the moment they exist.)
  - **(3) Chat left, everything else in a split right.** The workbench was up to three interchangeable columns, so a file the agent opened became a tab *beside* the chat and pushed it out of view. `WbGroup` now carries a fixed `role`, `paneRole(kind)` routes every pane, and `addPane` creates the column it belongs to: chats hold the left, files/browsers/terminals/diffs/PRs/Hypergate get the right with their own tab strip. Between them a real drag handle (pointer **capture**, so the drag keeps tracking over a pane's own iframe or terminal, plus arrow keys, clamped 20-80%, persisted). This closes **T86**.
  - **(4) A run-status card in the sidebar.** Model + provider, live status, the binding usage window, the chat's projects, and each PR with its review and check state as a link into the PR pane. Every fact already existed — in a chip under the composer, a badge in the header, a panel on the far right, a dot on a row — which was the problem.
  - **(5) Models that say why they can't run, for any provider.** New pure `model-availability.ts`: a `ModelAvailability` on `ModelInfo` that a static catalog fills in, combined with live usage windows by `resolveModelAvailability`. `LimitWindow.modelId` becomes **`modelFamily`** (`opus`, not `claude-opus-4-8`) so a window keeps covering the next generation of its family instead of silently ceasing to match. A blocked model **stays in the list**, greyed, unselectable, with the reason on its own line ("Your 7-day Opus limit is used up, it resets in 2d"), and Auto mode stops considering it. Nothing is Claude-specific: OpenAI/OpenRouter catalogs declare nothing and get the live-limits layer for free, and `blockLabel` already names plan, not-downloaded, signed-out and retired for the phases that will need them. The Claude catalog also gains what was missing: **Opus 5 beside Opus 4.8, Sonnet 5 beside Sonnet 4.6, and Fable 5.1** (the previously-added id was wrong).
  - **(6) The conversation takes three quarters of its pane**, not a fixed 768px column with dead margin on both sides. Measured on the **pane**, not the viewport: the workbench splits, so a viewport breakpoint is the wrong question.
  - **(7) The working steps read as a sequence.** "Worked on 6 steps · read_file, grep" hid the order, which is the interesting part. Each step is now its own numbered row, in order, one line each, still collapsible as a run and still expandable per step. A **thought row shows where the thought landed**: new pure `summarizeThought` searches *backwards* for a conclusion sentence ("So the fix is…", "Therefore…") and falls back to the last full sentence, because the first sentence of a reasoning block is almost always the model restating the question. `summarizeToolCall` does the same job for a tool's arguments, most-specific key first so a grep shows its pattern rather than its path.
  - **(8) A plan rail beside the chat.** `PlanRail` decodes the prompt into the steps it asks for as you type (pure `decomposePrompt`: an explicit list is the user's own decomposition and is never second-guessed; otherwise sentences that read as instructions, split on real sequence connectives, and a prompt with no instruction shape stays one step), and every step is editable, reorderable, skippable. The editing is the point: "no, don't open a PR" is a click here rather than an interruption three tool calls in. Skipped steps are sent as explicit **exclusions** when the plan is sent (opt-in checkbox, off by default). Below it, live sub-agents with what each is running right now — from the event stream, since the session list only refreshes when a turn ends, which is exactly when the answer stops being interesting. The plan is parked on the session (`Session.plan`) so it survives a tab switch.
  - **(9) Two future features written up**: **T151** remote phone messaging (a messaging channel as a transport beside the relay; since shipped, the Telegram adapter) and **T152** llama.cpp as a fourth `RuntimeAdapter` with an LM Studio-grade advanced surface and a one-slider simple surface over the same state.
  - **(10) Project docs you can actually open.** Spec/tasks rows are clickable (existing → the file pane; missing → says "not created" and the button reads **Create**, not "Build"), "Build all from chat" becomes **"Set up this project"**, and `AGENTS.md` / `CLAUDE.md` are listed whether or not they exist, with Create writing a starter and opening it. Guidelines aren't generated from the conversation the way a spec is: a guideline is a rule you decide, not a summary of what was said.
  - **Verified** over the web edition in an isolated data dir (seeded transcript, headless Edge over CDP at dSF 2, data dir deleted between the before and after runs): the step timeline rendered all seven steps in order with both thought rows summarizing their conclusion rather than their opening; the model picker showed Opus 5 and Opus 4.8 as **Limit reached** with the reset time while Sonnet 5 / Sonnet 4.6 / Fable 5.1 / Haiku 4.5 stayed selectable; typing a three-part prompt decoded three editable steps live; opening a terminal and opening `AGENTS.md` both landed in the right split with the chat still on screen; **Create** wrote the starter `AGENTS.md` to disk and opened it; and the rail withdrew on its own when the split squeezed the chat pane under 900px. Full typecheck ×9, 936 tests, and `build:web` green. · [spec](SPEC.md#chat--cowork-unified-surface) · Done: 2026-09-16
