### Latest, the mascot goes hand-drawn outline (2026-08-28)

- [x] **T135**, **Aphelion redrawn as a 2D hand-drawn outline cat with a line boil.** Philip: *"Instead of a realistic cat mascot, I'm thinking an animation 2d handdrawn, outline cat would be awesome."* `Mascot.tsx` redrawn from the filled astronaut illustration (cream suit, translucent glass, six hand-tuned colors that only worked on dark chrome) to pure ink outline: every stroke is `var(--ink)` on `var(--paper)`, so both themes come free; the ginger tail and inner ears stay as the one spot color and the sparkles moved to ginger + `var(--accent)`. The five activity poses and their state machine are untouched (same silhouettes, ported path-for-path), and the resting loaf gained **tucked front paws**, two little paper-filled rolls peeking under the chest, plus a soft haunch line. The signature move is the **line boil**: an SVG `feTurbulence`/`feDisplacementMap` filter whose seed re-seeds discretely at ~9fps, so the ink wobbles like traditional frame-by-frame animation even while a pose holds still. The filter is applied via a CSS class (`.aphelion-boil`), not a filter attribute, so reduced motion and a hidden document switch it off entirely; the body and helmet carry a paper fill purely so nearer shapes occlude the lines behind them (the inked-animation overlap trick). `AphelionAvatar` and `MiniAphelion` share the outline dome (the mini at a heavier stroke for 18px legibility); the boil stays exclusive to the rail mascot so the composer's send control stays calm. Prototyped and pose-verified in a standalone harness (light + dark screenshots, a gif cycling all five poses); captures come from the harness rather than the running app because another session's dev server owned the checkout. Typecheck and 40 desktop tests green. · [spec](SPEC.md#vision) · Done: 2026-08-28

### Latest, Workflows + runaway-output backstops (2026-08-13)

- [x] **T127**, **A looping model can no longer hang the app.** (Numbered T126 while in flight; renumbered at merge because the Hypergate task below landed on that number first, per the shared-checkout note.) Philip: *"for some reason it crashes and loops."* A qwen3.6-27b reply in LM Studio streamed **108,790 characters of reasoning, the same sentence 2,534 times, over nine minutes** (session `s_msqwff3v`, message 114), and the window locked up. Nothing in the stack was stopping it, in three separate places:
  - **No output cap was ever sent.** `openai-compat` and Ollama requests carried no `max_tokens` / `num_predict` at all, so a degenerate stream ran until it filled its own context window; Anthropic's was hardcoded at 4096. `ChatRequest.maxOutputTokens` now reaches every provider as its native knob, from a new `maxOutputTokens` setting (default `MAX_OUTPUT_TOKENS_DEFAULT` 8,192, range 256–200,000, in Settings → Agent loop beside the step budget).
  - **No repetition guard.** New `core/src/agent/runaway.ts` watches a stream's trailing window with two amortized tests (line collapse: many non-blank lines, ≤3 distinct; probe recurrence: the last 160 chars appearing 6+ times, which also catches cycles with no line breaks). It trips within ~2.5k characters on the observed case. The loop wires one guard per channel (text and reasoning are independent), and on a trip **cancels the request** through its own `AbortController` chained to the caller's signal, rather than abandoning the iterator and leaving the server streaming. The reply keeps its useful head (4k chars), drops the cycle, discards the half-emitted tool call, and **ends the turn** instead of spending the rest of the step budget looping; the message says what happened and that it's usually the model rather than the prompt.
  - **A React state commit per token.** `setLiveText`/`setLiveReasoning` ran once per streamed delta, re-rendering the whole transcript per token. Deltas now batch on a 50ms timer (a timer, not `requestAnimationFrame`, so a backgrounded window still flushes) with the live buffers capped at 40k chars, and the pending buffer is cleared on turn start, turn end, and unmount so a late flush can't duplicate a reply.
  - **Verified**: 7 new guard tests (including the exact 2,534-line failure, and negative cases for long varied prose, generated code with repeated shapes, and short repetition), 3 new loop tests (a never-ending generator ends promptly, cancels the stream, doesn't run the next iteration's tool call; the output cap reaches the provider), 185 core + 29 host + 36 desktop tests green, typecheck across the workspaces. · [spec](SPEC.md#context-guardrails--memory) · Done: 2026-08-13
- [x] **T128**, **The Goals tab is now Workflows: GitHub-Actions-style automation with routed steps.** A step is an agent **prompt**, an installed **skill**, another **workflow**, or a **shell** command.
  - **Routing, not just sequence.** `packages/shared/src/workflows.ts` models a step's `onSuccess`/`onFailure` as a transition (`next` / `end` / `fail` / `goto step`), so build → verify → *back to build* is expressible; `MAX_STEP_LOOPS` (10) bounds re-entry so a workflow that never converges still terminates with a message naming the step. Steps carry retries, a timeout, and continue-on-error (GitHub's semantics: the failure is still recorded, it just doesn't stop the run).
  - **Agent steps report their own outcome.** `stepOutcomeInstruction` appends a PASS/FAIL token request and `readStepOutcome` reads the last one, so an agent step routes the way a shell exit code does. All of a run's agent steps share **one session**, so verify sees what build did.
  - **Engine** (`packages/host/src/workflows.ts`): CRUD over `workflows.json`, runs in `workflow-runs.json` (capped at 20 per workflow), one run per workflow at a time, cancel that aborts the in-flight step, nested workflows bounded by `MAX_WORKFLOW_DEPTH`, a 15s scheduler for cron/interval triggers, and `reconcileWorkflowRuns` on boot so a run interrupted by a quit is written off rather than looking stuck. A shell step's command comes from the workflow the user wrote (never from the triggering event), so it runs unattended like a CI step instead of raising a guardrail prompt nobody is there to answer.
  - **Triggers**: manual, schedule (a 5-field cron parser in shared, so the editor validates and previews the next fire, plus a plain interval), CLI, Slack, and git-provider events **normalized across GitHub/GitLab/Bitbucket/other**. `dispatchWorkflowEvent` is the single door, reachable from the CLI in-process and, in the web edition, over the existing `POST /api/:channel` route, which is what a Slack app or a webhook posts to. New `nekko-agent workflow list|run <name>|trigger <command>|runs`.
  - **UI**: a filter rail (search that reaches into step bodies, state, trigger kind, category) and a list grouped **by category** or **by event listener** (a workflow appears under every event it listens for), groups collapsing and capping rows at 12 with a "show more" so hundreds stay navigable. A row expands into the **step canvas** and run history; `layoutWorkflow` in shared was made generic over node/edge shapes so the Skills tab's layered node graph now draws both. Failure edges are drawn in the danger color, loop-backs dip under the row dashed and labelled, terminal Done/Failed nodes close the graph, and with a run selected each node wears that run's outcome and attempt count. The editor redraws the canvas as you type, flags steps nothing routes to, and re-points routes left dangling by a deleted step. Templates: build/verify loop, PR reviewer, nightly sweep, empty.
  - **Goal runs kept**: the tab is gone, but goal runs now list in the **Training** tab so existing ones stay reachable, and `/goal` still works. Also fixed in passing: the desktop transport never forwarded `tasksUpdated`/`trainingUpdated`, which the host has always emitted, so those surfaces only refreshed when remounted.
  - **Two CodeQL findings on the PR, both fixed rather than dismissed.** `slugify` trimmed separators with `/^-+|-+$/`, which backtracks quadratically on a name that is mostly punctuation (a workflow name is user input); it now splits, filters, and joins, with a test that slugs 50k dashes in well under a second. And the flagged "insecure randomness reaching a security context" traced back through the new engine to `createSession`, whose ids were `Math.random()`-derived; a session id names a file and the remote transport lets one reach the network, so it now takes a `randomBytes` suffix in the same readable `s_<time>_<rand>` shape (ids are never parsed, so old sessions are unaffected).
  - **Verified**: 31 domain tests (routing edges, loop-back detection, unreachable steps, grouping, filtering, event matching per provider/repo/branch, cron incl. the Vixie day-of-month/day-of-week OR, templates) and 25 engine tests (ordering, retries, continue-on-error, the loop cap, a build/verify loop that *converges*, dangling transitions, timeouts, nesting depth, history trimming). Hands-on over the web edition against an **isolated data dir**: created the loop-back template from the UI and confirmed the canvas draws the dashed danger-colored "on failure, retry" edge; ran a 3-step shell workflow and got `s1 success, s2 attempt1+2 failure (continue-on-error), s3 success` with the row updating live over the push event; confirmed listener grouping lists a two-trigger workflow under both CLI and Schedule, that search matches a step body ("typecheck" → 1 of 2), dark mode, and no horizontal overflow at 375px. · [spec](SPEC.md#workflows-procedural-automation) · Done: 2026-08-13

### Latest, macOS metrics that are true, and runs that survive being cut off (2026-08-28)


- [x] **T133**, **Three pieces of feedback from running Nekko Agent on a Mac: tok/s was wrong, the GPU monitor said there wasn't one, and a timed-out run threw all its work away.** Philip: *"the tokens per second are not accurate (I'm running it on macos so not sure if that changes anything) / it says no nvidia gpu detected. could we show the macOS gpu usage instead when on a mac? / I ran a long running chat on nekko-agent and it timed out, but my issue is that it lost all of work progress and just said failed and I would have to start all over. Can we persist the progress so retries or pausing don't start all over?"*
  - **(1) tok/s was tokens over the whole wait, not over generation.** `ChatPane` divided the turn's output tokens by `Date.now() - turnStart`, and `turnStart` is set when the *turn* begins. That denominator includes prompt processing, every tool the agent ran, and any approval the user sat on, so a reply that generated for 10s and then ran a 40s test suite reported 10 tok/s instead of 50. The wrongness is not macOS-specific, it just shows up there because a local model's real rate is a number you can check against LM Studio. Providers now report **`outputMs`**, the decode phase only, on the `usage` chunk and the `usage` agent event: Ollama's server-side `eval_duration` where it's offered (the figure `ollama run --verbose` prints, and it excludes transport too), otherwise a `DecodeClock` timing first generated chunk → usage report, which drops time-to-first-token by construction. Tool-argument deltas start the clock as well, so a step that only calls a tool still has a rate. The renderer sums `outputMs` across the reply's steps and divides by that; `decodeRate`/`formatRate` moved into shared so the rule is one tested function, and single-digit rates keep a decimal (8.6 and 9.4 tok/s are different answers). `elapsed` deliberately stays wall clock: the two measure different things and are no longer expected to divide into each other.
  - **(2) macOS has a GPU, we just never asked it.** `gpu.ts` only ever shelled `nvidia-smi`, so every Mac fell through to "No NVIDIA GPU detected." The tool that reports real GPU residency there, `powermetrics`, needs root, which no monitoring chip should ask for. New `gpu-macos.ts` parses `ioreg -r -d 1 -w 0 -c IOAccelerator` instead, which any user can read: each accelerator entry's `PerformanceStatistics` carries `Device Utilization %` (the same 0-100 figure `nvidia-smi` reports) and the driver's current allocation. `gpu.ts` now dispatches on platform (`darwin` → ioreg, else nvidia-smi) rather than paying for a spawn that always fails. Apple Silicon has **no discrete VRAM**, so the reading is flagged `unified`, measured against `os.totalmem()`, and aggregated without summing one pool per GPU; the UI renames the meter **GPU memory** via shared `gpuMemoryLabel`, sub-labels it "unified with system RAM", and the dock names its probes (`os + ioreg`). The dead-end message became "No GPU stats available on this machine", which is true everywhere instead of naming a vendor we never checked for. Parsing is split from the spawn and tested against a real captured dump, including the `"In use system memory (driver)"` key that sits directly before the one we want and would read as zero under a loose match.
  - **(3) A cut-off run lost everything, and Retry finished the job.** Three separate defects compounded. `sendChat` persisted only on `done`/`error`, so a run killed mid-flight (timeout, crash, quit) left every completed step in memory only; it now **checkpoints on each `tool_result`**, written *before* the event is sent so anything re-reading the session finds the step it was just told about. `runAgent`'s stream `catch` yielded an error and returned **without pushing the partial turn**, so the reply's text died with the connection; `endInterrupted` now keeps it, marked `interrupted`, dropping only a tool call the model was mid-way through emitting (it never ran, and truncated arguments aren't trustworthy). And `retryLast` called `truncateSession` back to the last user message, which is what actually threw the work away, so the one visible recovery action was the destructive one. New `resume.ts`: `repairInterruptedHistory` answers any tool call that never ran with a stand-in result, which is what makes an interrupted transcript valid to send again (Anthropic and OpenAI both reject an unanswered tool request), and `RESUME_PROMPT` is injected as one **transient** turn, never persisted, only when the transcript ends on the assistant's own cut-off reply. `SendOptions.resume` skips both the append and the truncate. The notice now leads with **Resume** (shown when `hasResumableProgress`) and keeps the destructive path under its honest name, **Start over**. A user-initiated stop reports as `Stopped` and wears the warning colour, since it isn't a failure, and it keeps its partial answer too.
  - **Also**: `packages/shared/src` had 30 stale build artifacts (`chat.js`, `chat.d.ts`, …) committed into it back in June, which shadow the real modules for anything importing `./chat.js`, so the package could not be unit-tested at all; removed, and `@nekko-agent/shared` gained a `test` script and a place in the root `test` chain, which was missing.
  - **Verified** on the reporting machine (M1 Max, 64 GB): against the real LM Studio serving `qwen3.8-27b-uncensored-mlx`, one generation reported **19 tok/s** on the new decode-time denominator versus **9 tok/s** on the old wall-clock one, with 1.80s of time-to-first-token correctly excluded. The GPU probe returns `{source: 'ioreg', name: 'Apple M1 Max (32-core GPU)', utilizationPct: 29, 22486/65536 MB, unified: true}` and the numbers move between samples. Over an isolated web edition (own port + throwaway data dir) the chip reads `CPU 33% RAM 91% GPU 29% 22/64 GB` and its panel lists `GPU memory` + `Apple M1 Max (32-core GPU) · 29% util`, while the chat's Resources dock renders `GPU 31% utilization` / `GPU memory 35% · 22 / 64 GB used · unified with system RAM` / `os + ioreg`. 34 new tests (parser against the real dump, decode-rate arithmetic and formatting, provider decode timing incl. the excluded prefill, history repair, and host-level checkpointing that asserts the tool result is on disk at the moment the event fires and that resuming does not re-run it), 427 green across eight workspaces, typecheck and `build:web` clean. **Not fixed, and worth a follow-up:** the likely trigger of the original timeout is undici's 300s `headersTimeout`/`bodyTimeout` defaults on Node's `fetch`, which a long prefill on a large local context can exceed. Raising them needs `undici` as a direct runtime dependency of the packaged app, so it was left alone rather than bundled in with this; the run is now resumable either way. · [spec](SPEC.md#chat--cowork-unified-surface) · Done: 2026-08-28

### Latest, releases cut themselves on a version bump (2026-08-13)

- [x] **T129**, **A version bump on main now ships the release.** Philip: *"let's also update that every PR for nekko-agent or hypergate cuts a new github release and installers when the version number is updated."* Releases were tag-only: `release.yml` and `docker.yml` trigger on `v*`, so someone had to remember to tag after merging. New `version-release.yml` watches pushes to main that touch `package.json`, and when the version there has no matching tag yet it tags it and ships.
  - **Why it dispatches instead of only tagging.** A tag pushed with `GITHUB_TOKEN` does **not** trigger other workflows (GitHub blocks that to stop recursion), so tagging alone would leave `release.yml` unfired and look like a silent failure. It therefore tags, then `gh workflow run release.yml --ref <tag>` and the same for `docker.yml`; dispatching at the tag makes `github.ref_name` the tag, which is what every step in those workflows already reads. `release.yml` gained a `workflow_dispatch:` trigger for this (also useful for re-running a release by hand). No personal access token needed.
  - **Guards.** A push that touches `package.json` without changing the version (a dependency bump, a script edit) finds the tag already present and does nothing, so it's a no-op rather than a duplicate release. The version must look like a release version, and **apps/desktop and apps/server must agree with the root**, checked *before* tagging, because the installers carry the desktop version and the web edition the server one, so a partial bump would otherwise publish assets labelled with the old number.
  - Same workflow added to **hypergate**. · Done: 2026-08-13
- [x] **T131**, **The version-bump trigger needed a schedule, because our own merges can't fire it.** T129 shipped with `push: branches: [main]` alone, and v0.6.0's release did not happen: `auto-merge.yml` enables auto-merge with `GITHUB_TOKEN`, so the merge is attributed to `github-actions[bot]`, and **a push from that token never starts a workflow run**. That is the same recursion guard T129 already worked around for the bot-pushed *tag*, one step earlier in the chain than expected, and the failure mode is silence: the merge looked clean, CodeQL ran (GitHub's managed workflows are exempt), and nothing said the release had been skipped. v0.6.0 was cut by dispatching the workflow by hand. The fix is a `schedule: '*/15 * * * *'` tick that notices an untagged version bump whoever merged it, with the `push` trigger kept for a human merge (instant) and `workflow_dispatch` to cut one now. Every path is idempotent, it stops as soon as the tag exists, and a `concurrency` group stops a tick and a push racing to create the same tag. **A PAT in `auto-merge.yml` would make bot merges fire it directly and let the schedule go**, which is a call for Philip since it means provisioning a secret. · [spec](SPEC.md#distribution--platforms) · Done: 2026-08-13
- [x] **T132**, **`publish-release` could never publish, because a draft has no tag lookup.** T129's publish job resolved its release with `GET /repos/{owner}/{repo}/releases/tags/{tag}`, and that endpoint **only resolves published releases**: it 404s on a draft, which is exactly what `electron-builder --publish always` had just created one job earlier. So the step failed on its first API call every time, `set -e` aborted, and the release it existed to publish stayed a draft. It went unnoticed because the macOS job had been failing first, and `needs: release` meant the publish job was skipped rather than run and failed, so v0.6.0 was the first run where all three builds passed and the bug could surface at all. Fixed by listing `releases?per_page=100` (the list *does* include drafts for a token with push access, newest first) and selecting on `tag_name`, plus an explicit not-found guard so a genuine miss says so instead of failing on a `null` id. Verified the new lookup against the real v0.5.1 draft (resolves, `draft=true`) and a nonexistent tag (empty, caught by the guard). v0.6.0 itself was published by hand with the same body the job would have written. · [spec](SPEC.md#distribution--platforms) · Done: 2026-08-14
- [x] **T130**, **Released v0.6.0.** Root, desktop, server, and CLI bumped 0.5.1 → 0.6.0 (a minor bump: Workflows is a new feature and it replaced a nav destination), lockfile versions updated to match. Carries T126 (Hypergate one-click), T127 (runaway-output backstops), T128 (the Workflows tab), and T129. Cut by T129's automation on merge rather than by a hand-pushed tag, which is also its first real test. **The tag was cut but the release did not ship on 2026-08-13, and this entry claimed otherwise for a day**: the macOS job failed on T125's partial-secret check, so the draft carried Windows and Linux only, and `needs: release` correctly skipped the publish job. Re-run on 2026-08-14 once the signing secrets were complete, which produced all 11 assets including the first notarized `.dmg`. Publishing still had to be done by hand because of the `publish-release` bug in T132. · Done: 2026-08-13, actually shipped: 2026-08-14

### Latest, Hypergate in one click, and as a tab (2026-08-13)

- [x] **T126**, **Nekko Agent and Hypergate now connect in one click from either side, and Hypergate runs as a tab in the Nekko Agent window.** (T-number to re-check at merge per the shared-checkout note; T125 was taken by the macOS signing task.) Philip: *"nekko-agent and hypergate should be a 1 click easy connection in either direction. Neither of them are connecting easily. Also, let's add the ability once nekko-agent is connected to hypergate, hypergate runs as a tab in the same nekko-agent window."*
  - **The bug.** `detectNekkoMcp` required `/health` to answer `service: "hypergated"`. The daemon was renamed with the product and answers `hypergated`, so detection never matched, the one-click card the Settings copy promised could not appear, and what showed instead was a stale **Get Hypergate** nudge pointing at a repo that no longer exists. Fixed and rebuilt as `detectHypergate` (accepting both service names), running at app start rather than only when Settings mounts.
  - **A scoped token, not the master one.** `connectHypergate` claims this install's own agent from `POST /api/clients/resolve {key:'nekko-agent', create:true}`, falling back to the gateway token on daemons that predate that endpoint. This is what makes the pairing visible from Hypergate's side too, where Nekko Agent had been sitting at "Offline, never used": it now shows Active, with its own per-server permissions and usage history.
  - **One entry, written in one place.** The host owns the settings write (`withHypergate`), so a deep link can connect with no view mounted. Re-connecting replaces the `hypergate` row instead of adding a second, keeps a name the user chose, and drops the pre-rename `nekko-agent-mcp` row that would otherwise offer every tool twice.
  - **Hypergate as a tab.** New `hypergate` pane kind and `HypergatePane`: the manager's own UI over loopback, with the live version, server count and connected agent in its header, no address bar, and an offline state that offers to reconnect when the daemon restarts. Opened by connecting, by **Open tab**, or from the command palette.
  - **`nekko-agent://hypergate/connect?port=N`.** Registered in main, with the single-instance lock that makes it safe: a second launch now hands its argument to the running window instead of starting a rival host on the same data directory, and a cold launch replays the link once the page can hear it. The link carries a port and nothing else. `deepLinks.ts` checks it, the daemon is asked what it is, and a dialog naming the version, port and server count waits for a click, because any local program can open a URL and a gateway's tools reach every chat.
  - **Verified** against an isolated daemon on port 7801 (never Philip's real one on 7777) and the packaged app (`electron-builder --dir`, isolated `--user-data-dir`, driven over CDP): detection and the token exchange; a gateway that answers the scoped token with 9 tools and a bogus one with 401; the Settings card; one click producing a connected entry, 9 tools in the agent loop, and the Hypergate tab reading "connected as Nekko Agent"; the deep link both handed to a running instance (which stayed one process) and cold-launching the app. A wrapped, card-overflowing button and a singular/plural slip were caught in the packaged build and fixed. 16 host + 40 desktop tests green, typecheck across the workspaces. · [spec](SPEC.md#tooling--integrations) · Done: 2026-08-13

### Latest, one title bar instead of three (2026-08-06)

- [x] **T122**, **The window's chrome is the app's, and the menu bar is gone.** (T-number to re-check at merge per the shared-checkout note.) Philip: *"let's remove the file/edit/etc toolbar. Let's also make the minimize/close/maximize buttons feel integrated on the same line and color as the background."* Three strips stacked above the UI — an OS title bar, a File/Edit/View/Window menu, then the app — and the window buttons sat in a grey frame that belonged to no theme the app has.
  - **No menu bar.** `Menu.setApplicationMenu(null)` on Windows and Linux; macOS keeps its menu, which lives in the system bar rather than in the window and is where ⌘Q/⌘H/⌘W come from. Reload (Ctrl/⌘+R) and devtools (F12 / Ctrl+Shift+I) were the only items worth keeping and are bound with `before-input-event`; everything else duplicated a shortcut `shortcuts.ts` already owns or a gesture Chromium handles itself. Cut/copy/paste keep working without menu roles on Windows and Linux, which is why macOS is the exception.
  - **The app draws the title bar.** New `components/TitleBar.tsx`: a 38px strip in `--paper` carrying the Nekko Agent wordmark, `-webkit-app-region: drag` so it moves the window and double-click maximises. `titleBarStyle: 'hidden'` everywhere, plus `titleBarOverlay` on Windows/Linux and `trafficLightPosition` on macOS — both fed `TITLEBAR_HEIGHT` from the new `src/windowChrome.ts`, shared by main and preload, so the buttons land on the wordmark's line rather than above it.
  - **The buttons follow the theme.** The overlay is a native surface, so its colour has to be pushed: `applyTheme` now calls `syncTitleBarOverlay` (`renderer/chrome.ts`), which reads the resolved `--paper` / `--ink-soft` and sends them over a dedicated `chrome:titlebar-overlay` channel. Deliberately **not** in `IpcChannels`, every entry of which is routed through the shared Host dispatcher and reimplemented by the web transport — and a browser tab has no window chrome. The bridge is exposed as `window.nekko-agentChrome`, separate from `window.nekko-agent`, and its absence is how the renderer knows it isn't in the shell.
  - **The rail lost the wordmark it only showed on hover.** `.rail-label` is `opacity: 0` at rest, so the 48px header row above the nav was blank in the collapsed rail; the wordmark moved to the strip and the rail now starts on its first destination. Net, the app is a row shorter than before rather than a row taller.
  - **Verified** against the packaged build (`electron-builder --dir`, isolated `--user-data-dir`, screenshots off the real window because CDP only captures the page): no menu bar, one strip, wordmark top-left, native buttons top-right in `#0c0c11`; switching to light repainted them to `#fbfbfd` with `#52525e` glyphs. Structural check over CDP confirmed `platform: 'win32'`, exactly one `.titlebar` at 38px with `webkitAppRegion: 'drag'`. Full typecheck ×8 green. · [spec](SPEC.md#mascot-polish--onboarding) · Done: 2026-08-06

### Latest, chat + PR + nav + MCP polish (2026-08-05)

- [x] **T121**, **Nine pieces of chat-surface feedback from a PR-conflict run: honest agent wrap-ups, a non-vanishing final answer, a calmer working animation, a slimmer PR card, an Aphelion send button, project auto-filing, hover-reveal skills, the Agent tab, and an MCP menu.** (T-number to re-check at merge per the shared-checkout note.) **(1) Agent diagnoses access failures + honest wrap-up (prompt):** two new operating principles in `core/agent/prompt.ts`, an empty/unauthorized/not-found result (empty `gh`/API output, 401/403/404, "permission denied", an auth prompt) now makes the agent stop after a try or two and name the likely cause (a private or inaccessible repo, missing/expired credentials or `gh` auth, a wrong remote, a rate limit) with how to fix it, and it must end every turn with an honest wrap-up (what it did, what actually happened including anything blocked/unverified, the concrete next step) rather than claiming a false "done". Prompted by a run that flailed on a private repo and then reported success. **(2) Final answer never vanishes:** `toStreamBlocks` in `ChatPane.tsx` treated any assistant message carrying tool calls as a working step, so when a run ended on a tool-calling message (step budget spent, abort, or a model concluding in the same message as its last tool call) its wrap-up text folded into the collapsed "Worked on N steps" group and disappeared. It now surfaces the **last** assistant message's own text as the answer bubble even when that message also made tool calls (mid-run narration still folds in). **(3) Border beam, border only:** dropped the `.composer-beam::after` interior color wash (and the `beam-wash` keyframe) in `styles.css`, keeping just the rotating border ring, so the composer surface stays still as you type. **(4) PR card modernized + slimmed:** `PrCard.tsx` open-state relaid out as info-left / actions-right, a solid green gradient **Approve** primary, quiet ghost **Decline**, bordered **Review**, subtle violet **Merge**, and a compact ↗ open-on-GitHub icon, all right-aligned so the card is one slim row. **(5) Send is Aphelion:** the gradient send button is now the `AphelionAvatar` cat with no fill (new `.send-avatar` utility, transparent bg, lift + drop-shadow on hover); `SendIcon` import dropped. **(6) Auto-file chats under projects:** new pure `shared/project-detect.ts` (`detectSessionWorkspace`, 8 tests in `core/project-detect.test.ts`), signals = an attached path inside a workspace (decisive) or the prompt naming the workspace by display name / folder basename, with stopword-filtered generic names, word-boundary matches, and a null return on a two-project tie. `ChatPane.send()` calls it for a project-less chat on first run and `setSessionWorkspace`s the match; the sidebar's no-project bucket was renamed **General** and moved to the top (`WorkbenchView.tsx`). **(7) Skills on hover:** the + menu's Skill row is now a hover-reveal side flyout (`skillsHover` state + a 140ms close-delay so the pointer can cross the seam) instead of a click-to-open second page; the `attachMenuPage` state and back-arrow are gone. **(8) Agent tab:** the "Chat" nav destination is **Agent** (i18n `nav.chat` retranslated in all 7 locales) wearing the `AphelionAvatar` cat instead of the speech-bubble icon; the rail header lost its gradient logo mark and is now just the "Nekko Agent" wordmark (`App.tsx`, `navIcons` `ChatColorIcon` no longer imported). **(9) MCP menu in the strip:** new `McpMenu` in `ChatControls.tsx`, right of Tools, lists every configured server with a live status dot (connected/offline/checking via `getMcpStatus` probed on open), name + transport, and an enable/disable switch (global `settings.mcpServers[].enabled` via `updateSettings` + `refreshSettings`), plus **Add MCP server** (jumps to Settings) and a **Hypergate** recommendation (hypergate.app) for secure server management; SPEC "Instrument strip" + "MCP client" updated. Verified over an isolated seeded web edition: nav reads **Agent** with the cat avatar and a wordmark-only rail; a new chat shows the **General** group at the top; the strip carries **MCP** right after Tools, whose menu shows the empty state + Add MCP server + Hypergate; the **+ → Skill** flyout opened on hover listing `/goal`, `/research`, `/plan`, …; the send control is the Aphelion SVG (transparent bg confirmed via computed style); and `composer-beam::before` remains while `::after` / `beam-wash` are gone. Typecheck ×8 clean, 144 core tests + 36 desktop tests green. · [spec](SPEC.md#chat--cowork-unified-surface) · Done: 2026-08-05

- [x] **T30**, Cloud foundation (`apps/cloud`): multi-account hosted edition wrapping the same host + renderer per account. Email+password auth (scrypt), bearer tokens, file-backed `CloudStore` (Postgres-swappable). Per-account isolation via `withDataDir` (`AsyncLocalStorage`) + settings cache keyed by data dir. Entitlements (free/pro/team) gated server-side; OSS app never license-checks. Thin `CloudLogin` renderer gate. 13 cloud tests + live smoke. `npm run cloud` (:4318). PR #54.
- [x] **T31**, Payments (Stripe): `apps/cloud/src/billing.ts` hand-rolled against the Stripe REST API (no SDK dep, same dependency-free DNA as the relay's APNs/FCM senders), gated on `STRIPE_SECRET_KEY`. `POST /api/billing/checkout` ({plan} → Checkout Session URL), `POST /api/billing/portal` (Customer Portal URL), and a raw-body `POST /api/billing/webhook` whose `Stripe-Signature` is verified with HMAC-SHA256 (`node:crypto`, replay-window check). `planChangeFromEvent` maps `checkout.session.completed` / `customer.subscription.updated|deleted` → a plan, applied via `store.setPlan` so entitlements update immediately; `CloudStore` now carries `stripeCustomerId` (+ `findByStripeCustomer`/`setStripeCustomer`). `/api/auth/config` advertises `{billing}`. 19 new cloud tests (signature verify incl. tamper/replay, event mapping, and a full signed-webhook → account-upgraded HTTP path via Fastify `inject`); 32 cloud tests total. Live Stripe charges need the user's keys (`STRIPE_SECRET_KEY`/`STRIPE_WEBHOOK_SECRET`/`STRIPE_PRICE_PRO`/`STRIPE_PRICE_TEAM` + `CLOUD_PUBLIC_URL`). Renderer Upgrade UI deferred (server-side complete).

New shared `RunModelPicker` in `RunBoard.tsx`: provider select (enabled providers, "App default" first) + a model select fed live by `listModels(providerId)` (free-text model-id fallback when a provider can't list, e.g. no key yet); the override only applies as a complete provider+model pair, since `createTrainingRun` pairs `input.providerId ?? default` with `input.modelId ?? default` and a half-set pair would mismatch. Verified over the web edition: picker renders in both forms, loads the live Ollama model list, and a goal created with an override lands the chosen provider/model on its driving session. · Done: 2026-07-19
- [x] **T96**, **Goal-loop debug (bounded context + termination guards) + Command Center unified board + Goals mission-control reframe.** Four pieces of feedback ("goals shouldn't be a chat"; the Command Center board should lead, obviously a board, with the stats after a divider; the goal steer box was tiny and oddly placed; goals ran thousands of iterations and got stuck). **Engine (the debug):** the Goals/Training loop drove one ever-growing chat session, replaying the whole transcript to the model every turn, and re-armed forever relying solely on the model emitting `RUN_DONE_TOKEN`, so a weak/stuck agent looped for thousands of turns repeating itself. Fixed in `core/agent/loop.ts` + `host/{chat,training}.ts`: a new opt-in `SendOptions.maxHistoryTurns` windows the model input to the last N user-turn groups (cut on user boundaries so tool_use/tool_result pairs stay intact; `windowHistory` pure + unit-tested; normal chats send full history unchanged), and run turns pass `RUN_HISTORY_TURNS=4`; continue-turn prompts were made self-contained (re-state goal + constraints) since old turns are no longer replayed. `tickRun` now has host-enforced guards so a run can't spin forever: **time-budget** enforcement (`config.timeBudgetMin`), a **turn safety cap** (`config.maxTurns`, default `RUN_MAX_TURNS_DEFAULT=400`; resuming past it grants a fresh leg), **stall detection** (a `progressSignature` over plan+experiments plus an `outputSignature` of the answer; no measurable progress AND a repeating answer for `STALL_LIMIT=8` turns → **pause** with a log asking for a hint; adding a hint or resuming resets it), and an **error-loop cap** (`ERROR_LIMIT=5` consecutive failed turns → fail). **Command Center:** replaced the separate "Active agent work" grid + "All chats" lanes with ONE unified kanban **Command board** (Running / Recent / Idle lanes, `BoardLane`, sub-agents still nested, each lane capped + "+N more") as the hero at the top, then a **divider**, then the stat tiles, then Fleet/Optimize/etc.; removed `KanbanCard`. **Goals:** reframed as mission control, the hint box moved out of the cramped 300px rail into a full-width **"Steer the mission"** console (`HintComposer` gained `title`/`helper`/`buttonLabel`), "Open chat" became a low-key **Transcript** link, and a new **Mission control** card surfaces the turn/time budgets the loop enforces. Verified over an isolated seeded web edition (DOM-checked): board is the hero with Running/Recent/Idle lanes populated + sub-agent/goal sessions excluded, divider sits between board and stats, and the Goals dashboard shows the full-width steer console, Transcript button, Mission control card, pending-hint chip, and the stall-pause entry in the activity log, zero console errors. 111 core (+4) + 32 cloud tests, typecheck ×8, build green. · Done: 2026-07-20

No new IPC (artifacts ride the existing `training:*` + `trainingUpdated` chain; the tool routes through `sendChat`). Build + typecheck ×8 + 111 core tests green. · Done: 2026-07-21

### Phase 14, Local-server management + VRAM + thinking toggle + context tooltip (98–99)

- [x] **T98**, **Model-server management, GPU/VRAM monitor, per-chat thinking toggle, and an expanded context tooltip.** Four pieces of feedback on the Models/Chat surfaces. **Models tab, loaded state + management:** local server cards now show which models are **loaded** in memory and auto-refresh it every 6s while connected. Ollama reports loaded + per-model VRAM from `/api/ps` (`size_vram`); LM Studio uses its native `/api/v0/models` `state` field (new `lmStudioModels()` in `openai-compat.ts`, falling back to `/v1/models`); vLLM's served model is marked always-resident. Ollama keeps per-model **load/unload**; every local provider gains a **Stop server** action, a new host module `servers.ts` resolves the PID listening on the provider's port (Windows `netstat`, POSIX `lsof`) and kills its tree (`taskkill /T /F` or SIGTERM→SIGKILL), which is the unload path for servers with no HTTP unload (LM Studio/vLLM). New channel `server:stop` → `host.stopServer`. **VRAM/GPU:** new host `gpu.ts` shells `nvidia-smi` (CSV query, 2.5s TTL cache, null when absent) exposed via `gpu:stats` → `host.getGpuStats`; shared `GpuStats`/`GpuDevice` types. New renderer `GpuStats.tsx` (`useGpuStats` poll hook + `VramInline` for the metrics bar + `VramPanel` for the Command Center). The Command Center gains a **GPU & VRAM** panel (per-GPU total/used/free + utilization bar) and the Chat metrics bar a compact **VRAM x / y GB** readout with a per-GPU hover breakdown. **Thinking toggle:** `Session.thinking?: boolean` + `ChatRequest.think` threaded through `runAgent` into both providers (Ollama native `think`; OpenAI-compatible `chat_template_kwargs.enable_thinking`, local kinds only so cloud endpoints aren't sent unknown fields); Ollama's `message.thinking` deltas now stream as reasoning. Shared `modelSupportsThinking()` (reasoning-family id/name regex) drives a per-chat **on/off toggle** that replaces the passive "thinking" indicator for reasoning-capable models. **Context tooltip:** the metrics bar's by-source hover became a Claude-Code-style breakdown, a segmented usage bar, each source's tokens + share of the window, a **Free space** row, and the total. Shared helpers `isLocalProvider`/`LOCAL_PROVIDER_KINDS`. Verified live over the web edition against a real LM Studio (7 models, `/api/v0/models` state confirmed) + an RTX 5090: Stop-server button local-only, VRAM panel + inline readout show live GPU numbers, the thinking toggle appears only for qwen3 and flips on/off (persisted), and the expanded tooltip renders per-source %+Free space. - [x] **T99**, **Per-model load/unload for LM Studio via the `lms` CLI.** Follow-on to T98's design note: LM Studio's OpenAI-compatible HTTP API has no per-model unload, so T98 left "Stop server" (kill the process on the port) as its only unload path. This wires true per-model control by shelling out to LM Studio's `lms` CLI, giving LM Studio the same load/unload chips Ollama has instead of the all-or-nothing Stop server. New host module `lms.ts`: `lmsProbe(baseUrl)` (available only for a **localhost** instance with `lms` installed, resolving the binary from `~/.lmstudio/bin/lms(.exe)` then PATH; `version` result cached 15s so the 6s Models poll doesn't spawn a process each tick), `lmsLoad`/`lmsUnload` (`lms load <key> -y` / `lms unload <key>`; success is gated on exit-0 **and** the output not matching a failure phrase, since `lms` exits 0 even on "Model Not Found"; ANSI-stripped CLI headline surfaces as the toast on failure; 3-min load timeout so a large model isn't killed mid-load). Model ids line up across surfaces: the `/api/v0/models` id = the `lms` identifier, so a listed model's id passes straight through. `host.loadModel`/`unloadModel` route `lmstudio` through `lms.ts` (Ollama unchanged); new `lms:probe` → `host.lmsAvailable`; `loadModel`/`unloadModel` returns widened to carry an optional `message`. Shared `LmsProbe` type. Models page: LM Studio cards probe on mount and, when available, render Ollama-style per-model **load/unload** chips (toasting the CLI error on failure); a remote instance or a missing `lms` falls back to T98's static "loaded" badge (its tooltip now explains why) + Stop server. Verified against the real `lms` on this machine: probe true on localhost / false+reason on a remote URL; a real embedding model **loaded then unloaded** through the module end-to-end (confirmed via `lms ps`), and bogus keys return `ok:false` with the real "Model Not Found" headline. ### Phase 16, Chat UX pass + in-chat pull requests (101)

- [x] **T101**, **Grouped tool activity, empty-response recovery, per-prompt actions, live turn stats, project tags, and in-chat PRs.** Seven pieces of chat-surface feedback. **(1) Grouped activity:** a turn's working steps (reasoning + tool calls + inter-tool narration) now fold into a single collapsible **activity group** ("Worked on 6 steps · read_file, grep") via a pure `toStreamBlocks(messages)` in `ChatPane.tsx`; only the final answer stays a bubble, live and persisted alike. **(2) Empty-response stall (the reported bug):** with some local models a turn ended with `finish_reason:"stop"` and ~1 token, so the loop yielded `done` on a blank message and the chat looked dead. `core/agent/loop.ts` refactored to stream through a helper that accumulates into a `Turn`; an empty turn (no text/reasoning/tools) **retries once with a transient nudge** (appended to the sent messages only, never persisted), and if still empty finalizes with a visible marker instead of silently stopping (2 new loop tests, 130 core total). **(3) Prompt actions:** the single centered **Regenerate** button was removed; each user prompt now reveals a subtle on-hover row, its timestamp, Copy, Edit & resend, and **Reset here** (rewind to that message + re-run, reusing `truncateSession` + `sendChat`). **(4) Live turn stats:** a `TurnStatus` subtext under the conversation shows elapsed · tokens/sec · tokens generated while streaming (accumulating output tokens across the turn's iterations) and a muted last-turn summary when idle. **(5) Project tags:** a chat that references a project shows subtle chips on its sidebar row (supporting workspaces + free-form `tags`) and a project chip on its center tab (`WorkbenchView.tsx`). **(6+7) In-chat PRs:** a chat "has" a PR when a GitHub PR URL appears in its transcript (`gh pr create` output, or pasted). New pure `shared/pr.ts` (`extractPrUrls`/`parsePrUrl`/`collectSessionPrUrls` + `PrInfo`/`PrDiff`/`PrAction` types; 7 new tests) and host `pr.ts`: hydrate live state, diff, and actions via the **`gh` CLI**, falling back to the **GitHub REST API** with the connector PAT when `gh` is absent (15s cache; sticky `ghMissing` probe). Three new IPC channels (`pr:sessionList|diff|action`) through the full chain (ipc/host/dispatch/preload/web-client). Renderer `PrCard.tsx`: an inline **PR card** (open → Approve green / Decline faded-red / Review blue + a subtle Merge, each behind a one-click confirm; merged → purple star-dusted "successfully merged 🎉" banner), a **`PrPane`** diff side-pane (new `WbPane` kind `'pr'`, Devin-style per-file syntax-tinted diff), and **badges** on the sidebar row + chat header; store gains `prsBySession` + `refreshSessionPrs` + `openPrPane`, ChatPane refreshes on open/turn-end, and WorkbenchView back-fills badges for chats that reference a PR (bounded to 8). All actions are user-initiated. Verified over an isolated seeded web edition: the 5-tool run collapsed to one "Worked on 5 steps" group, the prompt hover row showed timestamp + Reset here (no Regenerate), sidebar/header PR badges + project tags rendered, the **open #84 card fetched live via `gh`** (draft, +6963/-5130, checks passing, all four buttons) and the **merged #95 card** showed the merged banner, and **Review opened the real 40-file diff** in a side pane, zero console errors. ### Phase 17, Composer drafts + discoverable shortcuts (106)

- [x] **T106**, **Composer drafts survive anything, chats open ready to type, and the new-agent / new-terminal shortcuts are discoverable.** Three pieces of workbench feedback. **(1) Saved drafts:** the workbench only mounts the pane you're looking at, so switching tabs, leaving the Chat view, or quitting threw away whatever was half-typed. New `renderer/composerDrafts.ts` parks `{ text, images, savedAt }` per session in `localStorage` (the pattern the store already uses for the Files pane), and `ChatPane` seeds both `draft` and `pendingImages` from it via lazy `useState` initializers. Writes are debounced 400ms, with a flush on unmount **and** on `beforeunload` so the last keystrokes can't die inside the debounce window; sending or queueing clears the entry. Images are data URLs, so the store carries a ~4MB budget: on overflow it evicts other chats' least-recently-touched drafts first and, as a last resort for a lone oversized draft, keeps the text and drops the images (losing what you typed is the worse failure). Corrupt JSON degrades to "no draft". A `draftLoadedFor` ref guards against the component ever being reused for another chat, which would otherwise write one chat's words into the next. **(2) Focus on open:** `ChatPane` focuses the composer once per chat and puts the caret after any restored text, skipping the grab when you're already typing in another input. Keyed on `providers.length` too, because the textarea is disabled until providers load. Deliberately synchronous rather than in a `requestAnimationFrame`, which never fires while the window isn't compositing (found in verification: the callback was silently dropped, and an effect re-run cancelled it for good). **(3) Shortcuts:** chords and their printed labels now live together in `renderer/shortcuts.ts` (⌘ on macOS, Ctrl elsewhere) so they can't drift. New agent stays **Mod+N** (Claude Desktop / ChatGPT desktop / Cursor all use it for New Chat); new terminal becomes **Mod+`**, the terminal key from VS Code, Cursor, and Zed, with Mod+Shift+` (VS Code's literal "new terminal", arrives as `~`) and the previous Mod+J both kept as aliases. Hints surface as a new `.kbd` chip in the workbench **+** menu (beside "New agent" and on the Terminal section header), on the empty-state buttons, in tab-strip tooltips, and in the command palette, which also gained a "New terminal" command. Verified over the web edition: Ctrl+N opens a chat with the composer focused, Ctrl+` spawns terminals, a typed draft plus a pasted image round-trip through a tab switch **and** a full reload, Queue clears the text, and all four kbd chips measure inside their parents. Desktop typecheck + build green; `composerDrafts.test.ts` (7 cases: round-trip, per-chat isolation, empty-clears, images-only, oldest-first eviction, text-kept-over-images, corrupt JSON) wired into `npm test` via a new `test` script on `@nekko-agent/desktop`. · Done: 2026-07-26

### Phase 18, Agent step budget + resource monitors (107)

- [x] **T107**, **Step budget that wraps up instead of failing, "turn" > "reply"/"iteration" wording, and CPU/memory/GPU/VRAM monitors with per-monitor polling.** Four pieces of feedback from a long exploration that died on `Reached max tool iterations`.
  - **(1) The reported bug, lost replies.** `runAgent` capped tool round trips at **12** and, on reaching it, yielded an `error`, so a normal multi-file exploration threw away everything it had done. The default is now `DEFAULT_MAX_STEPS = 80` (shared, user-overridable 5-400 via `AppSettings.maxSteps` + `clampMaxSteps`, wired from `host/chat.ts`), and reaching the budget takes **one final pass with the tools withheld** (`stream(turn, extraMessages, sendTools)` gained a tools override; a transient wrap-up nudge is sent but never persisted) so the reply lands as a real answer plus an honest note naming the limit. New **Settings > Agent loop** section (`AgentLoopSection`, committed on blur/Enter so a half-typed number never becomes the setting). 2 new loop tests (130 core total) cover the wrap-up answer, the note, `tools: []` on the wrap-up round, and the nudge staying out of the transcript.
  - **(2) Wording.** A chat exchange is a **reply** and one cycle of a long-running run is an **iteration**; "turn" is gone from the UI. The failure banner reads **"Reply failed"**, the footer **"Last reply"** (`TurnStatus` > `ReplyStatus`), composer/queue/reasoning/context-panel/spec copy reworded, RunBoard + Goals read "Iterations" / "Iteration budget", and the run log says "Iteration failed". Persisted field names (`run.turns`, `maxTurns`, `maxHistoryTurns`) are untouched, wording only.
  - **(3+4) Resource monitors.** New shared `monitor.ts` (`MonitorKind`, labels/hints, `DEFAULT_MONITORS`, `resolveMonitors`, `monitorSources`, `SystemStats`) + host `system.ts` (CPU load as a delta between `os.cpus()` snapshots, re-primed after a >30s gap so a re-enabled monitor doesn't report a since-boot average; RAM from `os.totalmem/freemem`; no native module) behind a new `system:stats` channel through the full chain (ipc/host/dispatch/preload/web-client). `GpuStats.tsx` + `GpuHud.tsx` are replaced by one `ResourceMonitor.tsx`: a module-level **shared sampler** (one 4s poll feeding every surface, `useSyncExternalStore`), `ResourceHud` (the bottom-right chip, now CPU/RAM/GPU/VRAM, with **on/off switches per monitor** on hover) and `ResourceDock` (the chat context panel's full monitoring section: CPU + cores + processor, memory %+GB, GPU util, VRAM %+GB, per-device used/free). **Off means not sampled**: the sampler polls only the probes the enabled monitors need, so GPU/VRAM off spawns no `nvidia-smi`, CPU/memory off samples no CPU times, and all-off polls nothing (chip reads "Monitors off"). The chip and the section no longer **overlap**: the section publishes its rect (`store.monitorDockRect`, no-op writes skipped) and the chip **warps into it** (`.monitor-chip` transition + `.monitor-absorb` glow), measured from `offsetLeft/offsetTop` rather than `getBoundingClientRect` so a mid-flight transform can't compound the offset; `visibility` rides the transition so the chip leaves the tab order once it lands. Dead `VramInline` removed.
  - **Verified** end to end over an isolated web edition (own port + throwaway data dir) against this machine's real hardware: chip showed live CPU/RAM/GPU/VRAM, the section rendered `32 cores, AMD Ryzen 9 9950X3D` / `33 / 47 GB` / `RTX 5090, 7.4 GB used, 24 GB free`, the warp landed dead-centre on the section (chip centre [1119,539] vs section centre [1120,539]) and returned to rest on close, and network counts confirmed the polling contract: all on > `system:stats` + `gpu:stats` once per tick, GPU+VRAM off > `system:stats` only, all off > **zero** probes, re-enabled > both resume. Typecheck x8 + 130 core tests + desktop/web build green. · Done: 2026-07-26

### Phase 19, Skills in the + menu + the orbit app icon (108)

- [x] **T108**, **Skills reachable from the + menu, and a new app icon (the orbit mark).** The two items from the chat that died on the old step cap.
  - **+ menu skills.** The composer's **+** menu was Photo / File / Folder only; skills existed but you had to know to type `/`. It now has a second page: **Skill** (with a count) opens a list of every skill the chat can run, built-ins **plus** installed marketplace skills via `matchSkills('', installedSkillDefs)` (the same source and ordering `/` uses, so the two menus can't disagree), each with its ★ flag, `/name` and description, and an **Add skill** row that jumps to the Skills tab. Picking one calls the existing `armSkill`, so the composer gets the usual skill chip. Menu state is a small `attachMenuPage` union; closing always resets to the first page, Escape steps back a page before closing and returns focus to the + button, and the trigger gained `aria-haspopup`/`aria-expanded` plus a label that names what it now does.
  - **App icon.** The shipped icon was still the pre-rebrand orange pixel-cat, and the NSIS installer art still said **"Nekko Agent"**. Replaced with an abstract **orbit mark** (deliberately not the mascot: at 16px a cat is a smudge). New `apps/desktop/scripts/make-icons.cjs` holds the artwork as vector and rasterizes it with Electron (already a devDependency, so no image toolchain): `icon.svg`, `icon.png` (512), `icon.ico` with real 16/24/32/48/64/128/256 entries, the PWA/apple-touch `icon-512.png`, and both installer BMPs (24-bit, written by hand since NSIS reads nothing else). Two traps handled: `capturePage` honours the display scale factor (a 125% monitor was producing a 640px "512" icon and an .ico whose directory lied about its entry sizes), and Windows won't open a 16px window, so every asset is drawn at >=256px and downsampled to its exact target, which supersamples the curve as a bonus. The comet's tail is one tapered ribbon of opaque bands rather than stroked segments (translucent round caps double-composite into visible beading) and its fade follows arc length rather than a straight gradient axis (the trail wraps 300 degrees, so its tail lands next to its head and a linear gradient collapsed to a sliver of violet).
  - **Note on nano banana.** The ask was to generate the icon with nano banana (`gemini-2.5-flash-image`). The supplied AI Studio key is valid (it lists 56 models) but every image model returns `429 limit: 0` on the free tier, so image generation needs billing enabled on that project. The generation script is written and ready; the shipped icon is the vector one.
  - **Verified** over an isolated web edition: the + menu shows `Skill 11`, the page lists ★ /goal first through /pr, picking `/review` closes the menu and arms the chip ("Runs on send · shown in context"), **Add skill** lands on the Skills tab, and Escape steps back rather than closing. Icon assets checked entry by entry (declared size == actual for all seven .ico entries) and eyeballed as a contact sheet from 16 to 256.
